Being nowhere, one had no right even to the dress of the period. In the bottom of the lake … hidden, and forgotten. Round the far-off lake were feathery green trees, not minding. She sat imagining their trunks, filmed over with the murk of London winters, but all the more beautiful now, standing out black amongst the clouds of green. There were trees in the distance ahead, trees, forgotten. She was here to look at them. It was urgent, important. All this long time and she had never once looked. She lifted her eyes cautiously, without moving, to take in the wide belt beyond the stretch of grass. It was perfect. Full spring complete, prepared and set there, ungrudgingly, demanding nothing but love; embanked between the sky and the grass, a dense perfect shape of various pure colour, an effect, that would pass; but she had seen it. The sharp angle of its edge stood out against a farther, far-off belt of misty green, with here and there a dark maroon blot of copper beech.

===Chinese===
坐着等待生活抛出些东西来，好把自己从空虚之感里分神片刻——这是卑劣的。坐着，从一处挪到另一处，穿着时兴的衣装。既无处安身，便连时兴的衣装也无权消受。在湖底……隐没着，且被遗忘。远方那片湖的四周长着羽毛般茸茸的绿树，浑不介意。她坐着，想象那些树干，蒙着伦敦历历冬天的沉翳，如今却愈见其美，黑黢黢地立在团团绿云之间。前方远处有树，一株株树，被遗忘。她就是来看它们的。这事紧迫，要紧。这么长久以来，她竟一次也不曾看过。她小心翼翼地抬起眼睛，身子不动，把草地之外那一道宽阔的林带收进眼底。它是完美的。完整的盛春，备办停当，陈设在那里，慷慨无吝，只索要爱；围筑在天与草之间，一团稠密而完美的形体，由种种纯净的彩色织成，一种终将消逝的效果；可她见过了。它边缘的锐角，衬着更远更远处一带雾蒙蒙的绿，间或缀着一团团深栗色的铜山毛榉。

===Original===
"Whatever happens, as long as one lives, there is the spring."

"Do not be too sure of this."

"Of course, if the world suddenly came to an end."

"This appreciation of spring is merely a question of youth."

"You can't be sure."

"On the contrary. Do you imagine for instance that this old woman on the next seat feels the spring as you do?"

===Chinese===
「无论发生什么，只要人还活着，就有春天。」

「对此不要太笃定。」

「当然，除非世界突然完蛋。」

「对春天的这种赏悦，不过是青春的问题。」

「你可说不准。」

「恰恰相反。譬如说，你能想象邻座这位老太太也像你这样感受春天吗？」

===Original===
Miriam rose unable to look; wishing she had come alone; or had not spoken. The green vistas moved all about her, dazzling under the height of sky. "I'm perfectly sure I shall always feel the spring; perhaps more and more." She escaped into irrelevant speech, hurrying along so that he should hear incompletely until she had firm hold of some far-off topic; dreading the sound of his voice.

===Chinese===
米丽亚姆站起身来，没法看过去；但愿自己是独自来的；或者方才不曾开口。绿色的远景在她四周移动，在高远的天空下晃眼。「我完全确信我会永远感受春天；兴许一年比一年深。」她逃进不相干的话里，加快脚步，让他听不真切，直到自己牢牢抓住某个遥远的话题；她惧怕听到他开口的声音。

===Original===
The flowerbeds were in sight, gleaming in the gaps between the tree trunks along the broad walk … ragged children were shouting and chasing each other round the fountain. "I must always here think," he said as they passed through the wicket gate "of this man who preaches for the conversion of infidels, Jews, Christians, and other unbelievers."

She hurried on preparing to face the rows of Saturday afternoon people on the chairs and seats along the avenue, their suspicious English eyes on her scrappy, dowdy, out-of-date English self and her extraordinary looking foreigner. Her spirits lifted. But they must be walking quickly and talking. The staring self-revealing faces must see that it was a privilege to have converse with anyone so utterly strange and far away from their English life.

"I'm not interested in him," she said as they got into their stride.

===Chinese===
花坛已在望，在大路沿路树干的间隙里闪烁……衣衫褴褛的孩子们绕着喷泉叫喊着互相追逐。「在这儿我总是不由得想起，」他们穿过小门时他说，「那个布道劝化异教徒——犹太教徒、基督徒以及其他不信者——的人。」

她加紧脚步，准备迎受林荫道边椅座上一排排星期六下午的人们，他们多疑的英国眼睛将盯住她那零碎寒碜、过了时的英国自我，和她那相貌奇特的外国人。她的情绪昂扬起来。不过他们必须走得快、谈得起劲。那些瞪视着的、自我暴露的脸孔必须看出：同这样一个彻底陌生、远在他们英国生活之外的人交谈，是一种特权。

「我对他不感兴趣，」两人迈匀了步子时，她说。

===Original===
"Why not?"

"I don't know why. I can't fix my thoughts on him; or any of these people who yell at crowds." Not quite that; but it made a sentence and fitted with their walk.

"It is perhaps that you are too individualistic," panted Mr. Shatov. There was no opening in this for an appearance of easy conversation; the words were leaping and barking round her like dogs.

But she turned swiftly leading the way down a winding side path and demanding angrily as soon as they were alone how it was possible to be too individualistic.

===Chinese===
「为什么？」

「我不知道为什么。我没法把心思钉在他身上；也钉不到任何对着人群叫嚷的人身上。」不尽然是那么回事；可它凑成了一句话，也配得上他们的步子。

「这或许是因为，你太个人主义了，」沙托夫先生喘着气说。这话里没有留出一处开口，容得下一场从容谈笑的表象；那些词句像狗一样在她四周蹿跳狂吠。

可她霍然转身，领路拐进一条蜿蜒的侧径，一等单独到了一处，便怒气冲冲地质问，怎么可能「太」个人主义。

===Original===
"I agree to a certain extent that it is impossible. A man is first himself. But the peril is of being cut off from his fellow creatures."

"Why *peril*? Men descend to meet. Are you a socialist? Do you believe in the opinions of mediocre majorities?"

"Why this adjective? Why mediocre? No, I would call myself rather one who believes in the *race*."

"*What* race? The race is nothing without individuals."

===Chinese===
「在某种程度上我同意这是不可能的。人首先是他自己。但危险在于与同类隔绝。」

「为什么是*危险*？人们肯降格相会。你是社会主义者么？你相信平庸多数的意见么？」

「为什么用这个形容词？为什么是平庸？不，我宁愿把自己称作一个信仰*族类*（the race）的人。」

「*什么*族类？没有个体，族类什么也不是。」

===Original===
"What is an individual without the race?"

"An individual, with a consciousness; or a soul, whatever you like to call it. The race, apart from individuals is nothing at all."

"You have introduced here several immense questions. There is the question as to whether a human being isolated from his fellows would retain any human characteristics. Your great Buckle has considered this in relation to the problem of heredity. But aside of this, has the race not a soul and an individuality? Greater than that of its single parts?"

"Certainly not. The biggest thing a race does is to produce a few big individualities."

===Chinese===
「没有族类，个体又算什么？」

「还是个体，带着一份意识；或者说一个灵魂，随你怎么称呼。脱离了个体的族类，压根儿什么也不是。」

「你在这里引入了好几个巨大的问题。其中有这样一个问题：一个人若与同类隔绝，还能不能保有任何人性的特征。你们那位伟大的巴克尔（Buckle）在联系遗传问题时考虑过它。可撇开这个不谈，族类难道就没有一个灵魂、一种个性么？难道不比它各个部分所有的更伟大么？」

「当然没有。一个族类所成就的最大事业，是产出寥寥几个伟大的个性。」

===Original===
"The biggest thing that the race does is that it *goes on*. Individuals perish."

"You don't know that they do."

"That is speculation; without evidence. I have the most complete evidence that the race survives."

"It may die, according to science."

===Chinese===
「族类所成就的最大事业，是它*延续不绝*。个体是要消亡的。」

「你可不知道他们真的消亡。」

「那是臆测；没有证据。我握有最完备的证据：族类存续。」

「照科学的说法，它也可能死。」

===Original===
"That also is a speculation. But what is certain is—that the greatest individual is great only as he gives much to the race; to his fellow creatures. Without this, individuality is pure-negative."

"Individuality *cannot* be negative."

"There speaks the Englishwoman. It is certainly England's highest attainment that the rights of the individual are sacred here. But even this is not complete. It is still impeded by class prejudice."

"*I* haven't any class prejudice."

===Chinese===
「那同样是一种臆测。但可以确定的是——最伟大的个体，其伟大只在于他给予族类的多；给予他的同类的多。舍此，个性便是纯粹-消极的。」

「个性*不可能*是消极的。」

「听，这是英国女人在说话。个体的权利在这片土地上神圣不可侵犯，这确实是英格兰最高的成就。可就连这一点也不完全。它仍然受着阶级偏见的阻碍。」

「*我*就没有半点阶级偏见。」

===Original===
"You are wrong; believe me you have immensely these prejudices. I could quite easily prove this to you. You are in many ways most exceptionally for an Englishwoman emancipated. But you are still pure-Tory."

"That is only my stamp. I can't help that. But I myself have no prejudices."

"They are so far in you unconscious." He spoke with extreme gentleness, and Miriam looked uneasily ahead, wondering whether with this strange knowledge at her side she might be passing forward to some fresh sense of things that would change the English world for her. English prejudices. He saw them as clearly as he saw that she was not beautiful. And gently, as if they were charming as well as funny to him. Their removal would come; through a painless association. For a while she would remain as she was. But even seeing England from his point of view, was being changed; a little. The past, up to the last few moments, was a life she had lived without knowing that it was a life lived in special circumstances and from certain points of view. Now, perhaps moving away from it, these circumstances and points of view suddenly became a possession, full of fascinating interest. But she had lived blissfully. Something here and there in his talk *threatened* happiness.

===Chinese===
「你错了；相信我，这些偏见你多得很。我可以轻而易举地向你证明。就一个英国女人而言，你在许多方面解放得出类拔萃。可你仍旧是纯粹-托利。」

「那不过是我的戳记。我拿它没办法。可我本人没有偏见。」

「它们在你身上至今还是无意识的。」他说得极端温柔，米丽亚姆惴惴地望着前方，心里琢磨：有这样一种奇异的知识守在身边，自己是否正走向某种对事物的新感知，它将为她把英国的世界整个改换。英国的偏见。他看这些偏见，看得同看出她并不美一样清楚。而且看得温存，仿佛它们在他眼里既逗人又可爱。它们的祛除终会到来；经由一段无痛的结交。有一阵子她还会是眼前这个样子。可即便只是从他的视角看英国，也正在把她改变；一点点。直到片刻之前的整个过去，是她活过来的一段人生，却不知那是在特殊境况中、从某些视角活过的人生。如今，或许正在离开它，这些境况与视角忽然成了一笔家产，满是迷人的兴味。可她一直活得极乐。他谈话中东一处西一处的什么，*威胁*着幸福。

===Original===
He seemed to see people only as members of nations, grouped together with all their circumstances. Perhaps everything could be explained in this way. … All her meaning for him was her English heredity, a thing he seemed to think the finest luck in the world, and her free English environment, the result of it; things she had known nothing about till he came, smiling at her ignorance of them, and declaring the ignorance to be the best testimony … that was it; he gave her her nationality and surroundings, the fact of being England to him made everything easy. There was no need to do or be anything, individual. It was too easy. It must be demoralising … just sitting there basking in being English. … Everything she did, everything that came to her in the outside world turned out to be demoralising … too easy … some *fraud* in it. … But the pity she found herself suddenly feeling for all English people who had not intelligent foreign friends gave her courage to go on. Meanwhile there was an unsettled troublesome point. Something that could not be left.

===Chinese===
他看人，仿佛只看见一个个民族的成员，连同他们的全部境况归拢成簇。也许一切都能用这个方式解释。……她之于他的全部意义，就是她的英国血统——他似乎视之为世上顶好的运气——以及她自由的英国环境，血统结出的果；这些她直到他来以前一无所知的东西；他微笑着看她的无知，还宣称这无知是最好的证词……就是这么回事；他把国籍和周遭一并递给她，在他这里，「身为英国」这一事实叫一切变得轻易。再无须去做什么、去成为什么，个人的什么。太轻易了。这必定是败坏人的……就这么坐在那儿，晒着「身为英国人」的太阳。……她做的一切、外界落到她头上的一切，到头来都在败坏人……太轻易了……里头总有某种*欺诈*。……可她忽然发觉自己对一切没有聪明外国朋友的英国人生出的怜悯，给了她继续下去的勇气。与此同时，还有一桩未了的、麻烦的症结。一件不能就此撂下的东西。

===Original===
"Perhaps," she said, "I daresay. But at any rate, I have an open mind. Do you think that the race is *sacred*, and has purposes, superman you know what I mean, Nietzsche, and that individuals are fitted up with the instincts that keep them going, just to blind them to the fact that they don't matter?"

"If one must use these terms, the race is *certainly* more sacred than the individual."

"Very well then; I know what I think. If the sacred race plays tricks on conscious human beings, using them for its own sacred purposes and giving them an unreal sense of mattering, I don't care a button for the race and I'd rather kill myself than serve its purposes. Besides, the instincts of self preservation, and reproduction are *not* the only human motives … they are not human at all. …"

===Chinese===
「也许吧，」她说，「我也说不准。可无论如何，我头脑是敞着的。你是不是以为，族类是*神圣的*，自有其目的——超人那一套，你知道我的意思，尼采——还以为个体们被装配上种种让他们活下去的本能，恰恰为了蒙蔽他们，不让他们看清自己无足轻重这个事实？」

「倘若非用这些字眼不可，那么族类*当然*比个体更神圣。」

「那好；我知道自己怎么想。倘若那神圣的族类对有意识的人耍弄诡计，把他们用于自己神圣的目的，又给他们一种虚幻的重要之感，那这族类我一个子儿也不在乎，我宁可自杀也不为它的目的效劳。再说，自保的本能、生殖的本能，*并非*人类仅有的动机……它们压根儿不是人的。……」

## VII / VII

===Original===
The picturesque building had been there, just round the corner, all these years, without once attracting her interested notice. The question she directed towards it, crossing the road for a nearer view, went forth, not from herself, but from the presence, close at her side, of Michael Shatov. During the hour spent in her room, facing the empty evening, she had been aware of nothing, outside the startling disturbance of her own movements, but the immense silence he had left. Driven forth to walk away its hours out of doors, she found, accompanying her through the green-lit evening squares, the tones and gestures of his voice, the certainty, that so long as she should frequent the neighbourhood, she would retain the sense of his companionship. The regions within her, of unexpressed thought and feeling, to which he had not reached, were at once all about her as she made her old, familiar, unimpeded escape through the front door, towards the blur of feathery green standing in the bright twilight at the end of the grey street; but beyond these inner zones, restored in a tumult of triumphant assertion of their indestructibility, the outer difficult life of expression and association was changed. If, as she feared, he should finally disappear into the new world towards which, with such urgent irritated determination, she had driven him, she would, for life, have reaped a small fund of his Russian courage and indifference.﻿ ﻿… It was with his impulse and interest, almost it seemed, actually in his person, that she drew up in front of the placard at the side of the strange low ecclesiastical looking porch. But as she read its contents, he left her, sped into forgetfulness by the swift course of her amazement. She had come, leaving her room at exactly the right moment, directly, by appointment, to this spot. Glancing once more for perfect assurance, at the liberal invitation printed in large letters at the foot of the heavenly announcement, she went boldly into the porch.

===Chinese===
这些年来，那座别致的建筑一直在那儿，就在拐角上，却从不曾一次引起过她饶有兴味的注意。此刻她穿过马路凑近去细看，投向它的那道疑问，并非发于她自己，而是发自紧守在她身旁的迈克尔·沙托夫的存在。在房间里对着空茫的夜晚捱过的那一个钟头里，除了自己一举一动那令她惊心的纷扰，她只意识到他留下的那片巨大的沉寂。被驱出门去、到户外把这几个钟头走散，她发觉，伴着她穿过暮色里泛绿的广场的，是他嗓音的种种腔调与手势，是一种确信：只要她还常来这一带走动，她就会保有与他作伴之感。她体内那些他未曾抵达的、未获表达的思想与情感的疆域，在她照旧熟稔而畅行无阻地从前门出走、走向灰街上尽头那团立在明亮暮色里的羽状绿影时，立时四面围拢了她；可在这些内层疆域之外——它们刚刚在一场宣告自身不可摧毁的凯旋喧嚣中复位——那表达与交往的外部艰难生活已经变了样。倘若，如她所惧，他终于消失进那个新世界——她曾以那样急切而恼怒的决心把他赶将进去的世界——那么她这一生，也算收下了一小笔他的俄国式的勇气与漠然。……她带着他的冲动与兴味——几乎可说，实实在在带着他本人——在那道古怪的低矮教堂模样的门廊旁、在告示牌前站定。可她一读告示的内容，他便离开了她，被她疾速涌起的惊异卷进了遗忘。她来得正是时候，一离开房间便依着约定径直来到了这个地点。她又瞥了一眼，求个十拿九稳，看那印在天启般布告脚下、以大字印出的慷慨邀请，随即壮起胆子走进了门廊。

===Original===
At the top of the shallow flight of grey stone steps up which she passed almost directly from the ecclesiastical doorway, a large black-draped figure, surmounted by the sweeping curves of an immense black hat voluminously swathed in a gauze veil of pale grey, stood bent towards a small woman standing on the step below her in dingy indoor black. The large outline, standing generously out below the broad low stone archway curving above the steps, against the further grey stone of what appeared to be part of a low ceiled corridor, was in extraordinary contrast to the graciously bending, surrendered attitude of the figure. Passing close to the group, Miriam caught a glimpse of large plump features, bold eyebrows, and firm dark eyes. The whole face, imagined as unscreened, was rounded, simple and undistinguished; blurred by the veil, it swam, without edges, a misty full moon. Through the veil came a voice that thrilled her as she moved on, led by a card bearing an arrowed instruction, down the grey stone corridor, with the desire for immediate audible mimicry. The behaviour of the voice was a perfect confirmation of deliberate intentional blurring of the large face. The little scanty frugally upstanding woman who had appeared to be of the artisan class, was either a humorous brick, or a toady, or of the old-fashioned respectful servant type, to stand it. The superfluous statement might, at least, even if the voice had become second nature, she might be thirty, have been delivered at an ordinary conversational pace. But to make the unimportant comment in the deliberately refined distressed ladylike voice, with pauses, as if every word were a precious gift.﻿ ﻿… She was waiting for some occasion, keeping her manner going, and the little woman had to stand out the performance.

===Chinese===
她几乎一出那教堂式的门口便踏上一道浅浅的灰石阶；石阶顶上，一个黑衣裹身的高大身影，头顶一顶硕大黑帽、帽身弯弧恢弘、又厚厚缠着一幅浅灰纱面纱，正朝低一级台阶上立着的一个小个子女人俯下身去，那女人穿一身晦暗的居家黑衣。这道庞大的轮廓，慷慨地凸现在石阶上方那道宽阔低矮的石拱之下，背后衬着更远处的灰石——似乎是低顶走廊的一段——与那身影温文俯就、降心相从的姿态恰成异乎寻常的对照。米丽亚姆挨着这簇人走过，瞥见一张丰肥的大脸盘、两道浓黑的剑眉和一双坚定的黑眼睛。整张脸，若想象它撤去了纱幕，是圆滚滚、简简单单、并不出众的；被面纱一模糊，它便漫漶无边地浮动起来，成了一轮朦胧的满月。纱幕后透出一个声音，听得她激灵一颤；她随着一张画着箭头指引的卡片继续沿灰石走廊往里走，心里涌起一阵立刻出声模仿的欲望。这声音的做派，恰恰证实了那张大脸是存心故意弄得模糊的。那个小个子、瘦瘪、寒俭地硬撑着的女人——看去像是工匠阶层——竟能受得住，要么是个有趣的老好人，要么是个谄媚者，要么就是旧式那种恭恭敬敬的仆妇。这番多余的话，就算那嗓音已成了第二天性、就算她可能已经三十岁，至少总可以用平常会话的速度说出来。可她偏要用那种刻意加工过的落难贵妇嗓音、带停顿地说出那句无关痛痒的评语，仿佛每个字都是一件珍贵的礼物。……她在等候某个场合，维持着她的做派，那小个子女人只好硬着头皮把这场表演撑到底。

===Original===
On her way down the corridor she met a young man with a long neck above a low collar, walking like an undergraduate, with a rapid lope and a forward hen-like jerk of the head, but with kind religious looking eyes. Underneath his conforming manner and his English book and talk-found thoughts, he was acutely miserable, but never alone long enough to find it out; never even long enough to feel his own impulses. Two girls came swiftly by, bareheaded, in reform dresses, talking eagerly in high-pitched out-turned cultured voices, their uncommunicating selves watchfully entrenched behind the polite Norman idiom. She carried on their manner of speech at lightning speed in her mind, watching its effect upon everything it handled, of damming up, shaping, excluding all but ready-made thought and opinion. Just ahead was an arched doorway and a young man with a sheaf of pamphlets standing within it. “It may,” she announced in character to an imaginary companion, “prove necessary to have some sort of conversational interchange with this individual.” Certainly it left one better prepared for the interview than saying Good Lord shall we have to *say* something to this creature? She got safely through the doorway, exchanging a slight bow with the young man as he provided her with a syllabus, and entered a large lofty quietly-lit room, where a considerable audience sat facing a raised platform more brightly illuminated, and from which they were confronted by a row of seated forms. She went down the central gangway, bold in her desire for a perfect hearing and slipped into a seat in the second row of chairs. The chairman was taking his place and in the dying down of conversation she heard a quiet flurry of draperies approaching with delicate apologetic rhythm up the gangway. It was the tall young woman. She passed, a veiled figure with bent head and floating scarf, along the little passage between the front row of the audience and the fern-edged platform, upon which she presently emerged, taking her place next to a lady who now rose and came forward, tall and black robed, and whose face, sharply pointing beneath the shadow of a plumy hat, had the expression of an eagle searching the distance with calm piercing eyes. In rousing ringing grievous tones she begged to be allowed to precede the chairman with an important announcement. Miriam inwardly groaned as the voice chid tragically on, demanding a realisation on the part of all, of the meaning for London of the promised arrival in its midst of a world-famed authority in Greek letters. She felt the audience behind her quelled into absolute stillness, and took angry refuge in the cover of her syllabus. “The Furthermore Settlement,” she read, printed boldly at the head of the page. It was one of those missions; to bring culture amongst the London poor﻿ ﻿… “devoted young men from the Universities.” Those girls in the corridor, wrapped in their code, were doing “settlement work.” They did not look philanthropic. What they loved most was the building, the grey stone corridors and archways, and being away from home on a prolonged adventure, free to weave bright colours along the invisible edges of life. She could not imagine them ever becoming in the least like the elderly philanthropists on the platform. But they were not free. The place was a sort of monastery of culture. If they wore habits they would be free and deeply inspiring. But they went about dressed longingly in the colours of sunlit landscapes, and lived their social life with ideas. There was something monastic about the lofty hall, with its neutral tinted walls and high-placed windows. But the place was modern and well-ventilated, even sternly chilly. Turning on her shoulder to examine the dutiful audience, she was startled by its effect of massed intellectuality. These people were certainly not the poor of the neighbourhood. By far the larger number were men, and wherever she looked she met faces from which she turned quickly away lest she should smile her pleasure. Even those that were heavy with stoutness and beards had the same lit moving look of kindly adventurous thought. They were a picked gathering; like the Royal Institution; but more glowing. She turned back to the platform in high hope amidst the outburst of applause greeting the retirement of the distressful lady and deepening to enthusiasm as there emerged timidly from behind one of the large platform screens a tall figure in evening dress, a great grown-up boy, with a large fresh face and helpless straight hanging arms and hands. He sat big and fixed, like an idol, whilst the chairman standing bowing over his table hurriedly remarked that an introduction was superfluous, and gazed at the audience with large moist blue eyes that seemed permanently open and expressionless and yet to pray for protection, or permission to retreat once more behind his screen. Miriam pitied him from the bottom of her heart and saw with relief when he rose that he produced a roll of papers for which a little one-legged ecclesiastical reading desk was conveniently waiting. He was going to read. But he placed his papers with large incapable fingers and she feared they would flutter to the ground, till he turned and took one fumbling expressionless step clear of the little desk and standing just as he was, his arms hanging once more heavy and helpless at his side, his eyes motionlessly fixed neither on the distance nor on any part of the audience, as if sightlessly focusing everything before him, began, without movement, or warning gesture, to speak. With the first sound of his voice, Miriam surrendered herself to breathless listening. It sounded out, at conversational pitch, with a colourless serenity that instantly explained his bearing, revealing him beyond the region either of diffidence or temerity. It was a voice speaking to no one, in a world emptied of everything that had gone before.

===Chinese===
沿走廊往里走的路上，她迎面遇上一个年轻男人，低领口上支着一截长脖子，走起路来像个大学生，一路急促大步，脑袋像母鸡似的一探一探，眼睛却和善，一副虔敬相。在他那随大流的做派底下，在他那些从英国书本和谈吐里拾来的念头底下，他痛苦得很，只是从没独处得久到发现这一点；甚至从没久到感受一回自己的冲动。两个姑娘快步走过，光着头，穿着改良派衣裙，用高亢、外扬、受过教养的嗓音热切交谈，她们那不与人通声气的自我，警惕地深沟高垒，守在彬彬有礼的诺曼习语后面。她在头脑里以闪电速度沿用了她们那副谈吐，看它对经手的每样东西起的作用——壅堵、塑形、把一切现成思想与意见之外的东西统统排除。前方刚到一处拱门，门内站着一个抱一摞小册子的年轻男人。「看来，」她拿足腔调地对一位想象中的同伴宣布道，「有必要与这位人士进行某种形式的交谈。」这当然比心里念叨天哪难道咱们非得跟这怪物*说*点什么不可？要让人对这场会面准备得更周全。她安然过了拱门，与那年轻男人微微颔首互礼，接过一份日程表，走进一间高敞而灯光沉静的大屋子；相当可观的听众面朝一座灯火更亮的升高讲台而坐，一排坐着的人影正从台上迎对着他们。她沿中央通道往里走，一心要听得完美，胆气很壮，闪身坐进第二排椅子里。主席正在就座；谈话声渐熄之际，她听见一阵轻微的衣料窸窣，踏着细巧的、带歉意的节奏，沿通道走近。是那个高个子年轻女人。她走了过去，一个戴面纱的身影，垂着头，披巾飘动，沿着前排听众与蕨草镶边的讲台之间的窄窄夹道，随即登上讲台，在一位太太身旁就了座；那太太这时起身走向前来，高挑个头，黑袍裹身，一张脸在羽饰帽的暗影下尖锐地前耸，神情像一只鹰，正以平静洞穿的眼睛搜索远方。她以激越、铿锵、沉痛的声调，恳请容许她在主席之先宣布一件要事。那声音悲悲剧剧地训诫个没完，要求全体认识到：一位世界闻名的希腊文学权威允诺莅临伦敦城中，对伦敦意味着什么；米丽亚姆在心里呻吟。她觉出身后的听众被镇得绝对的安静，便愤愤地躲进日程表底下寻个遮蔽。「『更进一步会堂』（The Furthermore Settlement），」她读到页首用大字印着的那一行。这就是那种布道事业之一；把文化送进伦敦穷人中间……「来自各大学的献身青年」。走廊里那两个姑娘，裹在她们的守则里，做的就是「会堂工作」。她们看上去不像做慈善的。她们最爱的是这座建筑本身，这些灰石的走廊与拱门，还有离家在外、长长地历一场险，得以沿着生活看不见的边缘织出鲜亮的颜色。她无法想象她们有朝一日会变得有半分像台上那些上了年纪的慈善家。可她们并不自由。这地方是某种文化的修道院。她们若穿上修女服，倒会自由，还会深深鼓舞人心。可她们偏偏穿着向往阳光风景的衣裳四处走动，与种种观念过她们的社交生活。这高敞的厅堂，中性色调的墙壁、开得高高的窗，自有一种修道院气息。可这地方是现代的、通风良好，甚至冷得严峻。她侧过身子打量那恭顺尽责的听众，被那一团攒聚起来的智识气象吓了一跳。这些人绝非本街坊的穷人。绝大多数是男人，而且无论她朝哪儿看，都会撞见一张张叫她赶紧扭开的脸，生怕自己笑出满心的欢喜。就连那些被肥胖与胡须压得沉重的脸，也带着同样轻快流动的、和善而富于冒险的思想神色。这是一场拣选过的聚会；像皇家研究院；却更炽热。她在一片掌声中怀着高涨的希望转回讲台——那掌声欢送着愁苦太太的退场，随即加深为热情，因为一座大讲台屏风后面怯生生地转出一个穿晚礼服的高挑身影，一个大个子的长大了的男孩，一张新鲜的大脸，两臂两手直垂着、无措地悬着。他大模大样端坐不动，像一尊神像；主席俯在桌上鞠着躬，匆匆表示介绍是多余的；他便用一双大而湿润的蓝眼睛凝望听众，那双眼睛仿佛永远睁着、毫无表情，却又在祈求庇护，或祈求准许再退回屏风后面去。米丽亚姆打心底里可怜他，又宽慰地看见他起身后掏出一卷纸来，一张小小的独脚教堂式读经架正巧候着。他要朗读了。可他用一双大而无能的手指安放稿纸，她直怕纸页会飘落地上，直到他转过身，摸索着、面无表情地跨出一步、离开那小读经架，就那么原样站着，双臂又一次沉重而无措地垂在身侧，眼睛一动不动，既不望着远方，也不望着听众的任何部分，仿佛无视一切地把眼前一切聚焦，毫无动作、毫无预告的手势，开了口。他的嗓音一起，米丽亚姆便把自己交给了屏息的聆听。那声音以会话的音高传出来，带着一种无色的安详，顿时解释了他的仪态，把他显明在羞怯与鲁莽两界之外。那是一个不朝任何人说话的声音，处在一个把此前一切都清空了的世界里。

===Original===
“The progress of philosophy,” went the words, in letters of gold across the dark void “is by a series of systems; that of science by the constant addition of small facts to accumulated knowledge.” In the slight pause Miriam held back from the thoughts flying out in all directions round the glowing words, they would come again, if she could memorise the words from which they were born, coolly, registering the shape and length of the phrases and the leading terms. Before the voice began again she had read and reread many times; driving back an exciting intruder trying, from the depths of her mind to engage her on the subject of the time-expanding swiftness of thought.

===Chinese===
「哲学的进展，」那些词句行进着，如金字横过黑暗的虚空，「在于一系列的体系；科学的进展，则在于往积累的知识上不断添加细小的事实。」在这小小的停顿里，米丽亚姆勒住自己，不去追随那些绕着发亮的词句向四面飞散的念头；它们还会回来的，只要她能把这些生出念头的词句冷冷地记牢，记下短语的形状与长短，还有那些关键词。

===Original===
“A system,” pursued the voice “very generally corrects the fallacy of the preceding system, and leans perhaps in the opposite direction.” She flushed warm beneath the pressure of her longing to remain cool﻿ ﻿… “Thus the movements of philosophic thought may be compared to the efforts of a drunken man to reach his home.” The blue eyes remained unaltered, while the large fresh face expanded with a smiling radiance. He was a darling. “He reels against the wall to his right and gains an impetus which sends him staggering to the left and so on; his progress being a series of zigzags. But in the end he gets home. And we may hope that philosophy will do the same, though the road seems at times unnecessarily broad.”

===Chinese===
「一个体系，」那声音继续道，「一般总是纠正前一体系的谬误，并且或许朝相反的方向倾过去。」她因渴望保持冷静而绷得发热……「因此，哲学思想的运动，可以比作一个醉汉回家路上的种种努力。」那双蓝眼睛纹丝未变，那张新鲜的大脸却舒展开一片微笑的光辉。他真是个可人儿。「他朝右边的墙上撞一下，得着一股冲劲，又打得他踉跄向左，如此往复；他的行进是一连串的之字形。可他终究到了家。我们也可以指望哲学同样如此，尽管那条路有时似乎宽得没有必要。」

===Original===
He turned back to his papers, leaving his sentence on the air in an intense silence through which Miriam felt the eager expectancy of the audience flow and hang waiting, gathered towards the fresh centre whence, unless he suddenly vanished, would come, through the perfect medium of the unobstructive voice, his utmost presentation of reasons for the tantalising hope.

===Chinese===
他转回他的稿纸，把那句话留在空气里，留在一阵强烈的静默之中；穿过这静默，米丽亚姆觉出听众热切的期待在流淌、悬垂着等候，朝那个新的中心聚拢——除非他突然消隐，否则，透过这毫不碍事的声音这完美的媒介，将从那中心涌出他对那个撩人之希望的最淋漓的呈报。

===Original===
At the end of the lecture she sat hurriedly sorting and re-sorting what she had gleaned; aware that her attention had again and again wandered off with single statements that had appealed to her, longing to communicate with other members of the audience in the hope of filling up the gaps. Perhaps the questions would bring back some of the things she had missed. But no one seemed to have anything to ask. The relaxation of the hearty and prolonged applause, had given way to the sort of silence that falls in a room after vociferous greetings, when the anticipated occasion vanishes and the gathered friends become suddenly unrecognisably small and dense. She looked at the woman at her side and caught a swift responsive glance that shocked her, clear blue and white and remote in limpid freshness though it was, with its chill understanding familiarity. Something had gone irrevocably from the evening and from herself. The strange woman was exactly like somebody﻿ ﻿… a disguise of somebody. Shattering the silence came a voice from the back of the hall. “If the lecturer *thinks*, and seems to deprecate the fact, that theology deals with metaphysical problems in an *unmetaphysical* way, that is, from the point of view of metaphysic, in an *unscientific* way.﻿ ﻿…” compared to Dr. McHibbert’s his voice was like the voice of an intoxicated man arguing to himself in a railway carriage.﻿ ﻿… “may we not *say* that when metaphysic takes upon itself to criticise the validity of *scientific* conceptions, it does so, from the point of view of *science* in an unscientific way?”

===Chinese===
讲座终了，她坐在那里匆匆把拾得的东西分了又分；她发觉自己的注意力一次又一次跟着某些打动她的单个论断走了神，渴望与听众中的其他人交谈，指望把空缺填补起来。也许提问环节会带回一些她错过的东西。可似乎谁也没有什么要问的。那阵由衷而持久的掌声松弛下来之后，已让位于一种寂静——就像一间屋子里喧腾的寒暄过后落下的那种：被期待的场合消散了，聚拢的朋友们忽然变得认不出地渺小而密实。她看了看身旁的女人，撞上一道迅速回应的一瞥，那一瞥叫她震惊——尽管清亮、蓝白、遥远，透着澄澈的清新，却带着一种冷冷的、知根知底的熟稔。某种东西已经不可挽回地从今晚、从她自己身上离开了。这个陌生女人活脱像某个人……是某个人的化装。大厅后部一个声音击碎了寂静。「倘若讲演者*认为*——而且似乎还为此事实抱憾——神学是以一种*非形而上学的*方式处理形而上学的问题，也就是说，从形而上学的观点看，是以一种*不科学的*方式。……」比起麦克希伯特博士（Dr. McHibbert）的嗓音，这声音就像一个醉汉在火车车厢里自言自语地争辩。……「我们难道不可以*说*：当形而上学自任去批评*科学*概念的有效性时，它这样做，从*科学*的观点看，恰恰是以一种不科学的方式？」

===Original===
This Miriam felt, was terribly unanswerable. But the hushed platform was alive with the standing figure almost before the muffling of the last emphatic word told that the assailant had re-assumed his seat.

===Chinese===
这一下，米丽亚姆觉得，厉害得无法回击。可几乎在最后一个加重词的余音尚未裹紧、表明来攻者已重新落座之前，那静默下来的讲台已随一个起立的身影活了过来。

===Original===
“I think I have said,” his face beaming with the repressed radiance of an invading smile, was lifted towards the audience, but the blue eyes modestly addressed the frill of green along the platform edge, “that metaphysic, with respect to some of the conceptions of science, while admitting that they have their uses for practical purposes, denies that they are exactly true. Theology does not deny the problems of metaphysic, but answers them in a way metaphysic cannot accept.”

===Chinese===
「我想我说过，」他的脸焕发着一朵微笑突袭而至、被强自压住的光辉，仰向听众，可那双蓝眼睛却谦逊地对着讲台边沿那一绺绿蕨致意，「形而上学对科学的某些概念，一面承认它们在实际用途上自有其用，一面否认它们是严格为真的。神学并不否认形而上学的问题，只是以一种形而上学无法接受的方式回答它们。」

===Original===
“In that case *Theology*” began a rich, reverberating clerical voice.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「既然如此，*神学*——」一个浑厚、带回声的教士嗓音开了腔。……

===Original===
“This is veggy boring,” said the woman.

===Chinese===
「这真是乏味得很哪，」那女人说。

===Original===
He was going to claim, thought Miriam, noting the evidence of foreign intelligence in her neighbour’s pronunciation, that religion, like metaphysic and science, had a right to its premises and denied that metaphysic was adequate for the study of the ultimate nature of reality, exactly as metaphysic denied that science was adequate.

===Chinese===
他要主张的，米丽亚姆想，一面留意着邻座发音里外国智识的痕迹，是：宗教也像形而上学和科学一样，有权拥有自己的前提，并且否认形而上学足以研究实在的终极本性，恰如形而上学否认科学足以如此。

===Original===
“Yes, isn’t it,” she murmured, a little late, through the deep caressing thunder of the clerical voice, wondering how far she had admitted her willingness to be at the disposal of anyone who found, in these tremendous onslaughts, nothing but irrelevance.

===Chinese===
「是啊，可不是嘛，」她喃喃应道，慢了半拍，声音穿行在那教士嗓音深沉、爱抚般的雷鸣里，心里疑惑着：自己到底在多大程度上招认了，愿意听凭任何人的差遣——只要此人在这般雷霆攻势里只看见不相干。

===Original===
“If one could peacefully fall asleep until the summing up.”

===Chinese===
「要是能安安稳稳睡上一觉，一觉睡到总结陈词，就好了。」

===Original===
She spoke out quite clearly, moving so that she was half turned towards Miriam, and completely exposed to her, as she sat with an elbow on the back of her chair and her knees comfortably crossed, in all her slender grey-clad length, still set towards the centre of the platform. Miriam unwillingly searched her curious effect of making in the atmosphere about her, a cold, delicate, blue and white glare. She had seemed, all the evening, a well-dressed presence. But her little oval hat, entirely covered with a much washed piece of cream coloured lace and set back from her forehead at the angle of an old-fashioned flat lace cap, had not been bought at a shop, and the light grey garment so delicate in tone and expression, open at the neck, where creamy lace continued the effect of the hat, was nothing but a cheap rain-cloak. Either she was poor, and triumphing over her poverty with a laborious depressing ingenuity, or she was one of those people who deliberately do everything cheaply. There was something faintly horrible, Miriam felt, about the narrowness of her escape from dowdiness to distinction.﻿ ﻿… Washable lace was the simplest possible solution of the London hat problem. No untravelled Englishwoman would have thought of it.﻿ ﻿… Behind the serenity of her smooth white brow, behind her cold wide clearly ringed sea-blue eyes, was the dominant intelligence of it all, the secret of the strange atmosphere, that enveloped her whole effect; so strong and secure that it infected her words and movements with a faint robust delicate levity. In most women the sum of the tangible items would have produced the eye-wearying, eye-estranging pathos of the spectacle of patience fighting a lost battle, supplied so numerously all over London by women who were no longer young; or at least a consciously resigned cheerfulness. But she sat there with the enviable cool clear radiant eyes of a child that is held still and unsmiling by the deep entrancement of its mirth.

===Chinese===
她吐字相当清楚，一面挪动身子，让自己半转向米丽亚姆，整个人整个儿亮在她眼前：她坐着，一肘支在椅背上，两腿舒服地交叠，一身灰衣的修长身量，依旧朝着讲台中央。米丽亚姆不由自主地探究起她那奇特的本事——在她四周的大气里造出一道清冷的、细巧的、蓝白的强光。整个晚上，她看去都像一位衣着体面的在场者。可她那顶小小的椭圆形帽，整个蒙着一块洗了又洗的乳白蕾丝，从前额往后戴着，角度像一顶老式平蕾丝帽兜，并不是店里买来的；那件色调与表情都如此细巧的浅灰衣裳，领口敞着，乳白的蕾丝在那里延续着帽子的效果，其实不过是一件廉价的雨披。要么她是穷的，正以一番费力的、叫人丧气的机巧战胜着贫穷；要么她是那种存心把一切都将就着办得廉价的人。这里头有某种微微可怕的东西，米丽亚姆觉得——她从寒酸逃向出众的那条路，窄成那样。……可水洗的蕾丝，是伦敦帽子问题最简单的一种解法。没出过门的英国女人绝不会想到。……在她那平滑白皙的额头的一片安详后面，在她那双清冷、睁大、轮廓分明地圈着眼的海蓝眼睛后面，是支配这一切的智识，是裹住她整个人效果的奇异气氛的秘密；那智识如此强大而安稳，以致把一种淡淡的、强健而精微的轻快，感染给了她的一言一动。在多数女人身上，这些有形各项的总和，会造出那种令眼睛疲倦、令眼睛疏远的哀婉——忍耐打一场败仗的景象，伦敦满城都是不再年轻的女人在供应这种景象；或者至少，造出一种自觉认命的开朗。可她坐在那儿，一双眼睛清凉、明澈、放着光，像一个孩子被自己欢乐的深深出神定住了、不笑也不动，令人艳羡。

===Original===
The chairman had risen and suddenly quelled the vast voice in the midst of its rising tide of tone, with the reminder that there would be opportunity for discussion a little later. A question rang out, short and sharp, exploding, as if released automatically by the renewal of stillness, so abruptly that Miriam missed its significance. The woman laughed instantly, a little clear tinkling gleeful sound, hesitatingly supported here and there amongst the forward rows of chairs by stirrings and small sounds of amusement. Miriam glowed with shame. It had been a common voice; perhaps some lonely uninstructed man, struggling with problems that were as terrible to him as to anyone; in the end desperately getting round them, by logical somersaults, so funny, that these habitually cultured minds could see only the absurdity. Her heart beat with gratitude as the lecturer, with gentle respectful gravity, paraphrased at some length an extract from the earlier part of his address. She was once more recalled by the voice at her side. Turning she found the unchanged face still set towards the platform. She answered the question in a low toneless voice that yet sounded more disturbing than the easy smooth conversational tone of her neighbour. She talked on, questioning and commenting, in neat inclusive phrases, and Miriam, turned towards her, reading the history of the duel of audience and lecturer in the flickerings across her face, of amusement or of scorn, responded freely, delighting in a converse that was more wonderful, with its background of cosmic discussion, than even the untrammelled exchange of confidences with a stranger on a bus. Presently there was a complete stillness.

===Chinese===
主席起了身，在那洪大的嗓音正涨起音潮的当口猛然把它镇住，提醒说稍后自会有讨论的机会。一个问题短促而尖利地炸响，仿佛是被重新降临的寂静自动触发的，来得那么突兀，米丽亚姆没抓住它的要旨。那女人立刻笑了，一小串清脆叮咚的快活声音，前排椅座间这里那里迟疑地应和着一些窸窣与细碎的莞尔。米丽亚姆羞得浑身发热。那本来是个普通人的嗓音；也许是个孤零零的、无人指点的男人，正与一些对他和对任何人同样可怕的问题搏斗；末了靠几个逻辑筋斗绝望地把它们绕了过去，绕得那样滑稽，以致这些素来有教养的头脑只看得见荒唐。讲演者怀着温和恭敬的郑重，把讲稿前部的一段话相当铺陈地换了个说法再说一遍，她的心感激地跳动。身旁的声音又把她唤了回来。她转过头，发现那张一成不变的脸仍旧朝着讲台。她用一种低沉、无调的嗓音回答了问题，可那嗓音听上去竟比邻座那从容圆滑的会话腔更叫人心绪不宁。她接着谈下去，发问、评点，用的都是干净周到的短语；米丽亚姆转向她，从她脸上掠过的闪动里——莞尔的或讥诮的——读着听众与讲演者这场决斗的历史，自由地应答着，享受这场交谈的奇妙——有宇宙性讨论做背景，比在公共马车上与陌生人无拘无束地互吐衷曲还要奇妙。片刻之后，是完全的寂静。

===Original===
“If there are no more questions,” said the chairman, rising.

===Chinese===
「如果没有别的问题了，」主席说着，起了身。

===Original===
“I should just *like*” broke in a ringing cheerful voice quite near at hand, “to ask Dr. McHibbert *why* if he considers that metaphysic is of no use in a man’s life, he finds it worth *while*, to *pursue* such a fruitless study?”

===Chinese===
「我倒真想，」近旁一个响亮欢快的嗓音插了进来，「问问麦克希伯特博士*为什么*，既然他认为形而上学于人的一生毫无用处，他又觉得*值得*去*从事*这样一项不结果的研究？」

===Original===
“*Don’t answer*” said the woman in clear penetrating tones.

===Chinese===
「*别回答*，」那女人用清亮穿透的声调说。

===Original===
“Don’t answer; don’t answer,” repeated in the immediate neighbourhood two or three masculine voices. The lecturer, sitting bent forward, his friendly open brow yielded up to the invading audience, his big hands clasped capaciously between his knees, sent a blue glance swiftly in her direction, hesitated a moment, and then sat silent, smiling broadly down at his clasped hands.

===Chinese===
「别回答；别回答，」近旁两三个男人的声音跟着重复。讲演者坐在那里，身子前倾，他那友善开朗的额头听任入侵的听众摆布，两只大手宽阔地扣在两膝之间，朝她的方向飞快地投来一道蓝眼光，迟疑了片刻，随即坐着不出声了，对着扣着的双手咧嘴而笑。

===Original===
“Isn’t he a perfect *darling*,” murmured Miriam while the chairman declared the lecture open for discussion and she gathered herself together for close attention.

===Chinese===
「他岂不是个十全十美的*可人儿*，」米丽亚姆喃喃道；这时主席宣布讲座进入讨论，她收拢自己，准备凝神细听。

===Original===
“There will be nothing worth heahghing till he sums up,” said her companion and went on to ask her if she meant to attend the next lecture. Miriam perceived that unless she chose to escape forcibly, her companion had her in a close net of conversation. She glanced and saw that her face was already that of a familiar associate, no longer spurring her to trace to its source the strange impression that at first it had given her of being a forgotten face, whose sudden return, unrecognisably disguised, and yet so recognisable, filled her with a remembered sentiment of dislike.

===Chinese===
「要等他总结，那之前是没有什么值得*听*的，」她的同伴说，接着又问她打不打算去听下一讲。米丽亚姆察觉，除非她硬行脱身，同伴已把她收进一张贴身的谈话之网。她瞥了一眼，看出那张脸已然是一张熟识同伴的脸，不再催逼她去追索最初那种奇怪印象的来路——那印象是：这是一张被遗忘的脸，突然归来，化装得认不出了，却又那样认得出，叫她心里充满一种记起来的厌恶。

===Original===
“Rather,” she said and then, watching the opening prospect of the long series of speeches, and protected by the monotonous booming of a pessimistic male voice “I’m so awfully relieved to find that science is only half true. But I *can’t* see why he says that metaphysic is no practical use. It would make *all* the difference every moment, to know for certain that mind is more real than matter.”

===Chinese===
「当然去，」她说；随后，望着一场长串发言的长长前景次第拉开，又得着一个悲观男人嗓音单调的嗡嗡庇护，「发现科学只有一半是真的，我可真是如释重负。可我*不明白*，他为什么说形而上学毫无实际用处。要是能确知心灵比物质更真实，那每时每刻都会*全然*不同。」

===Original===
“*Pahghfaitement.*”

===Chinese===
「*Pahghfaitement*（完全正确）。」

===Original===
Dr. McHibbert’s voice interrupted her, damming up the urgent flow of communications. She watched him, listening without attention.

===Chinese===
麦克希伯特博士的嗓音打断了她，壅住了那急切的倾诉之流。她望着他，听着，心不在焉。

===Original===
“He’s like a marvellously intelligent bolster,” she said tonelessly “but with a heart and a soul. He certainly has a soul.”

===Chinese===
「他像一只聪明绝顶的长枕头，」她无调地说，「不过是有心有魂的。他肯定有个灵魂。」

===Original===
Flattered by a soft chuckle of amusement, she added in a low murmuring man’s voice “the objectors are like candle-lit turnip ghosts,” and was rewarded by the first direct glance from the blue eyes, smiling, assuring her that she was acceptable. The ghost of the remembered face was laid. Whoever it was, if in reality it were to reappear in her life, she would be able to overcome her aversion by bold flirtation.

===Chinese===
一声轻柔的莞尔取悦了她，她用低低的、呢喃的男人嗓音补上一句「那些提异议的，像烛光里的萝卜鬼」，并得了报偿——蓝眼睛头一回直接的一瞥，含着笑，向她保证她是被接纳的。那张记起来的脸的鬼魂就此安息了。不管她是谁，倘若当真要在她生活里重新出现，她也能靠一场大胆的调笑压住自己的反感。

===Original===
When the lecturer at last rose to reply, the guiding phrases of his discourse were the worn familiar keys of a past experience. Used for the second time at the doors of the chambers they had opened within the background of life, they grated, hesitating, and the heavy sound threw the bright spaces into shadow and spread a film of doubt over Miriam’s eagerness to escape and share her illumination with people waiting outside in the surrounding gloom. The light would return and remain for her. But it was something accomplished unaccountably. The mere reproduction of the magic phrases, even when after solitary peaceful contemplation she should have reassembled them in their right relations and their marvellously advancing sequence, would not carry her hearers along the road she had travelled. The something that held them together, lively and enlivening, was incommunicable.

===Chinese===
当讲演者终于起身作答时，他那篇言辞里领路的短语，成了一把旧经验里用旧的熟钥匙。第二次用在它们曾在生活背景里打开过的那些房间门口，它们迟疑地、发涩地转动，那沉重的声响把明亮的空间投进阴影，也在米丽亚姆那份夺路而出、与外面四周黑暗中等候的人们分享她的启明的热望上，蒙了一层疑虑的薄膜。光会回来的，会为她留驻。可那是件不知怎么就成就了的事。单是复述那些有魔力的短语——哪怕她经过独自安详的沉思，已把它们按正当的关系、按它们奇妙递进的次序重新拼拢——也载不动她的听众，载他们把她走过的路再走一遍。那把它们黏结在一起的什么，活泼而又予人活泼的什么，是不可传达的。

===Original===
“Don’t huggy away. The audience will take a considerable time to disperse.”

===Chinese===
「别急着走哇。听众散场还要好一阵子呢。」

===Original===
Miriam desired only to escape into the night. Just outside, in the darkness, was the balm that would disperse her disquietude. The grey-clad woman held it suspended in the hot room, piling mountainously up. But they sat enclosed, a closely locked party of two. Conversation was going on all over the room. This woman with her little deprecating frown at the idea of immediate departure, had the secret of the congregational aspect of audiences. Miriam sat still, passively surrendering to the forcible initiation into the new role of lingerer, to the extent of floundering through absentminded responses.

===Chinese===
米丽亚姆只想逃进夜里。就在外面，黑暗里，有那能驱散她不安的抚慰。那灰衣女人却把它悬着，悬在这闷热的屋子里，堆得山一般高。可她们被围坐着，一个锁得紧紧的两人小团体。满屋子都在进行谈话。这位一想到立刻起身便微微皱起不赞成之色的女人，掌握着听众那副会众面貌的秘密。米丽亚姆坐着不动，被动地任人强行领她入门，领进「流连者」这个新角色，任自己跌跌撞撞地应付着心不在焉的答话。

===Original===
“What?” she said suddenly, turning full round. Something had thrilled upon the air about her, bringing the whole evening to a head.

===Chinese===
「什么？」她忽然说，整个人转了过来。有什么东西在她四周的空气里激了一记，把整个晚上推到了顶点。

===Original===
“Haldane’s *Pathway to Reality*,” repeated the woman as their eyes met. Miriam was held by the intense radiance of the blue eyes. Light, strangely cool and pure, flowed from the still face. She was beautiful, with a curious impersonal glowless beauty. The light that came from her was the light of something she saw, habitually.

===Chinese===
「霍尔丹的《通往实在之路》（Haldane's *Pathway to Reality*），」两人目光相遇，那女人重复道。米丽亚姆被那双蓝眼睛炽烈的辉光定住了。光，奇异地清凉而纯净，从那张安静的脸上流出来。她是美的，带着一种奇特的、无我的、不发热的美。从她身上来的光，是她惯常看见的某种东西的光。

===Original===
“But I ought not to recommend you to read. You ought to spend all your free time in the open air. Moreover, it’s very stiff reading.”

===Chinese===
「可我本不该劝你去读的。你该把全部空闲时间都花在户外。况且，那是艰涩得很的读物。」

===Original===
Miriam rose, beleaguered and flinching. How did people find out about books? Where did they get them from? This woman could not afford to buy big expensive volumes.﻿ ﻿… Why did her quick mind assume that the difficulty of the book would be a barrier, and not see that it was the one book she was waiting for, even if it were the stiffest and dryest in the world?﻿ ﻿… But the title was unforgettable; one day she would come across the book somewhere and get at its meaning in her own way.

===Chinese===
米丽亚姆站起身，被围困着，瑟缩着。人们是怎么发现书的？他们从哪儿弄来？这个女人可买不起昂贵的大部头。……她那敏捷的头脑为什么就认定书的艰涩会是一道屏障，而不明白这正是她等着的那一本，哪怕它是世上最艰涩最枯燥的？……可那书名是忘不掉的；总有一天她会在什么地方撞见这本书，用自己的路数弄懂它的意思。

===Original===
“Well; we may meet next week, if we are both early; I shall be early.” She rose enlivening her grey cloak with the swift grace of her movements and together they proceeded down the rapidly emptying room.

===Chinese===
「那么；下周我们也许会相见，倘若我们俩都早到；我会早到的。」她站起身，她动作的敏捷风姿把那件灰雨披都带得有了生气，两人一同顺着正迅速空下来的屋子往外走。

===Original===
“My name is Lucie Duclaux.”

===Chinese===
「我的名字是露西·迪克洛（Lucie Duclaux）。」

===Original===
The shock of this unexpected advance arrested Miriam’s rapid flight towards the harbour of solitude. She smiled a formal acknowledgment, unable and entirely unwilling to identify herself with a name. Her companion, remaining close in her neighbourhood as they threaded their way amongst talking groups along the corridor, said nothing more, and when they reached the doorway Miriam’s determination to be free, kept her blind and dumb. She was aware of an exclamation about the rain. That was enough. She would not risk a parting intimate enough to suggest another meeting, with anyone who at the sight of rain, belaboured the air and the people about her, with an exclamation that was, however gracious and elegant, a deliberate assault, condemning her moreover of the possession of two voices.﻿ ﻿… Gathering up her Lucie Duclaux cloak, the woman bowed swiftly and disappeared into the night.

===Chinese===
这番出乎意料的亲近之举一震，拦住了米丽亚姆朝独处之港疾飞的势头。她微笑着，规矩地致意，既不能、也全然不愿把自己同一个名字认同起来。她的同伴，在两人沿走廊从一堆堆谈话的人群中穿行时，紧挨在她身旁，再没说什么；到了门口，米丽亚姆那求自由的决心让她又盲又哑。她听见一声关于下雨的惊叹。这就够了。同一个告别时亲热到暗示再来相会的人，她可不愿冒这个险；何况此人一见下雨，便那样感叹着捶打空气和她周围的人，那声惊叹无论多么文雅优美，都是一次蓄意的袭击，而且还判她拥有两副嗓子。……那女人拢起她的露西·迪克洛式雨披，匆匆一躬，消失进了夜里。

===Original===
The girls had understood that the evening had been a vital experience. But they had sat far away, seeming to be more than ever enclosed in their attitude of tolerant amusement at her doings; more than ever supporting each other in a manner that told, with regard to herself, of some final unanimous conclusion reached and decision taken, after much discussion, once for all. In the old days they would have thought nothing of her dropping in at eleven o’clock at night, with no reason but that of just dropping in. But now, their armoury of detached expectancy demanded always that she should supply some pretext. Tonight, feeling that the pretext was theirs, everyone’s, news too pressing to wait, she had rushed in unprepared, with something of her old certainty of welcome. It was so simple. It *must* be important to Jan that what Hegel meant was only just beginning to be understood. If Jan’s acceptance of Haeckel made her sad, here was what she wanted; even though McHibbert said that we have no right to believe a theory because we could not be happy unless it were true.﻿ ﻿… All the same a theory that makes you miserable can’t be altogether true﻿ ﻿… *Miserable*; not sorry. Everything depends upon the kind of man who sets up the theory.﻿ ﻿… Pessimists can find as good reasons as optimists﻿ ﻿… but if the optimist is cheerful because he is healthy and the pessimist gloomy because﻿ ﻿… everything is a matter of temperament. Neither of them sees that the fact of there being anything anywhere is more wonderful than any theory about the fact﻿ ﻿… making optimists and pessimists look exactly alike﻿ ﻿… then why was philosophy so fascinating?

===Chinese===
姑娘们都明白，这个晚上是一场关乎生命的经历。可她们坐得远远的，看去比以往更深地封在她们那种对她言行宽容取乐的态度里；比以往更深地互相扶持着，那姿态说到她时，透出某种经多方讨论后达成的、一劳永逸的最终一致结论与决定。在从前，夜里十一点她顺路进来串个门，什么理由也没有，就只是进来串串门，她们根本不会当回事。可如今，她们那套超然期待的武库，总是要求她供应某种借口。今晚，她觉得这借口是她们的，是大家的——消息紧要，等不得——便带着几分旧日那种笃定受欢迎的把握，一头闯了进来，什么也没准备。事情就这么简单。黑格尔（Hegel）的意思才刚刚开始被人理解，这件事对简（Jan）必定是要紧的。如果说简接受海克尔（Haeckel）让她难过，那么这正是她想要的；哪怕麦克希伯特说过，我们无权因为「除非它是真的，否则我们便不能幸福」就去相信一个理论。……话虽如此，一个叫你痛苦的理论，总归不会全然是真的……*痛苦*；不是遗憾。一切取决于立起这个理论的是哪一种人。……悲观主义者找得出的理由，和乐观主义者的一样好……可倘若乐观主义者因为健康而快活，悲观主义者因为……而阴郁……那一切都是气质的事。他们俩谁都没看见：任何地方竟然存在任何东西，这一事实本身，比关于这事实的任何理论都更奇妙……这一来，乐观主义者和悲观主义者看上去一模一样……那么，哲学为什么还这样迷人呢？

===Original===
“You will lose your colour, my child, and get protuberances on your brow.”

===Chinese===
「你会失去血色的，我的孩子，还会在前额上鼓出包来。」

===Original===
“What then?”

===Chinese===
「那又怎样？」

===Original===
St. Pancras clock struck midnight as she reached home. The house was in darkness. She went noiselessly up the first two flights and forward, welcomed, towards the blue glimmer of street lamps showing through the open drawing-room door. It was long since she had seen the room empty. His absence had restored it to her in its old shadowy character; deep black shadows, and spaces of faint blue light that came in through the lace curtains, painting their patterned mesh on the sheen of the opposite walls. The old familiar presence was there in the hush of the night, dissolving the echoes of the day and promising, if she stayed long enough within it, the emergence of tomorrow, a picture, with long perspectives, seen suddenly in the distance, alone upon a bare wall. She stood still, moving rapidly into the neutral zone between the two days, further and further into the spaces of the darkness, until everything disappeared, and all days were far-off strident irrelevances, forever unable to come between her and the sound of the stillness and its touch, a cool breath, passing through her unimpeded.

===Chinese===
她到家时，圣潘克拉斯的钟敲响了午夜。屋子一片漆黑。她悄没声儿地上过头两段楼梯，往前，像是被迎接着，走向敞开的客厅门里透出的街灯的蓝色微光。她已久不见这房间空着的样子了。他的不在，把它旧日幽暗的品性还给了她：深深的黑影，和几方暗淡的蓝光，从蕾丝窗帘里进来，把带纹样的网印在对面墙壁的光泽上。那旧日熟稔的临在就在这夜的静谧里，消融着白昼的回声，并且许诺——只要她在它里面待得够久——明天的浮现：一幅画，透视悠长，忽然在远处、独自在一面空墙上看见。她伫立不动，迅速移进两个日子之间的中立地带，越移越深，移进黑暗的空间，直到一切都消失了，所有的日子都成了远处刺耳的不相干，永远插不进她和这静寂的声音、静寂的触碰——一缕清凉的呼吸，畅通无阻地穿过她——中间来。

===Original===
She could not remember whether she had first seen him rise or heard the deep tones coming out of the velvety darkness.

===Chinese===
她记不起自己先是看见他起了身，还是先听见那深沉的嗓音从天鹅绒般的黑暗里出来。

===Original===
“No, you did not startle me. I’ve been to a lecture,” she said sinking in a sleep-like stupor into a chair drawn up beyond the light of the window, opposite his own, across which there struck a shaft of light falling, now that he was again seated, only on his face. Miriam gazed at him from within the sheltering darkness, fumbling sleepily for the way back to some lucid recovery of the event of her evening.

===Chinese===
「不，你没吓着我。我去听了个讲座，」她说着，沉入一种睡眠般的恍惚，坐进一把拖在窗光之外、与他的椅子相对的椅子里；一道光柱斜过他那边，如今他重新坐下了，那光只落在他脸上。米丽亚姆从遮蔽的黑暗里凝望他，睡意昏沉地摸索着回去的路，想把今晚的事清明地收拢回来。

===Original===
“Ah. It is a pity I could not be there.” His words broke into the stillness, an immensity of communication, thrown forward through their unrestricted sitting, in the darkness, where, to bridge, before tomorrow, the gap made by his evening’s absence, he had waited for her. She sat silent, her days once more wound closely about her, an endless hospitable chain.

===Chinese===
「啊。真遗憾我不曾在场。」他的话破进静默，是一次浩大的交流，经由他们在黑暗中这场不设防的对坐，向前掷来；在这黑暗里，为了在明天之前搭桥跨过他今晚外出造成的豁口，他一直等着她。她坐着不出声，她的日子又一次紧紧密密地绕上她，一条无止尽的好客的链子。

===Original===
“Tell me of this lecture.”

===Chinese===
「给我讲讲这个讲座。」

===Original===
“Philosophy.”

===Chinese===
「哲学。」

===Original===
“Tsa. It is *indeed* a pity.”

===Chinese===
「啧。那*确实*可惜。」

===Original===
“It is a series.”﻿ ﻿… are you sitting there already involved in engagements﻿ ﻿… cut off; changed.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「是一个系列。」……你是否已坐在那里、缠进种种约会里去了……被隔断了；变了。……

===Original===
“Excellent. I shall most certainly come.” He was looking freely ahead. His evening had not interested him﻿ ﻿… he had gone and come back, his horizons unenlarged﻿ ﻿… but not seeing the impression he had made on those people; the steps they would take.

===Chinese===
「好极了。我一定会来。」他自在地望着前方。他的晚上不曾让他感兴趣……他去了又回来了，眼界未开……而且没看见他给那些人留下的印象；他们将要采取的步骤。

===Original===
“It would be splendid for you. The lecturer’s English *wonderful*. The way the close thought made his sentences, fascinated me so much, that I often missed the meaning in listening to the rhythm; like a fugue.” Aren’t you *glad* you’ve enlarged your horizons? Don’t you know what people are﻿ ﻿… what you, a person, are to people? Are you a person? In a blankness, life streamed up in spirals, vanishing, leaving nothing.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「那于你会是极好的。讲演者的英语*了不起*。那严密的思绪造出句子的方式，叫我着迷得很，以致我常常在听那节奏时漏掉了意思；像一首赋格。」你*扩了*眼界，难道不*高兴*吗？你难道不知道人们是什么……你这个人，对人们来说是什么？你是一个人吗？在一片空白中，生命盘旋着涌上来，消散着，什么也不留。……

===Original===
“That is not bad. Ah I should not have paid this visit. It was also in some respects most painful to me.” Poor little man, poor little lonely man white-faced and sensitive, in a world without individuals; grown and formed and wise without realising an individual; never to realise. Audible within the darkness was a singing, hovering on spaces of warm rosy light.

===Chinese===
「这不坏。啊，我本不该作这次拜访。它在某些方面于我也是十分痛心的。」可怜的小个子男人，可怜的小个子孤独男人，脸色苍白而敏感，活在一个没有个体的世界里；长大、成形、有了智慧，却从未意识到一个个体；永远也不会意识到了。黑暗中听得见一阵歌唱，悬在一片片温暖的玫瑰色光上。

===Original===
“You must not regret your visit.”

===Chinese===
「你可不要为这次拜访后悔。」

===Original===
“Regret no; it was much as I anticipated. But it is disheartening, this actual witnessing.” They were disposed of in some way; in one piece; he would have a formula.

===Chinese===
「后悔么，谈不上；事情大半如我所料。可这亲眼目睹，实在叫人灰心。」他们已以某种方式被处置完毕；整块地；他会有一个公式的。

===Original===
“What are they like?”

===Chinese===
「他们是什么样的人？」

===Original===
“Quite as I expected; good simple people, kind and hospitable. I have been the whole evening there. Ah but it is sad for me this first meeting with English Jews.”

===Chinese===
「全然如我所料；善良朴实的人，和气而好客。我整晚都在那里。啊，这第一次与英国犹太人相见，于我是悲伤的。」

===Original===
“Perhaps you can make Zionists of them.”

===Chinese===
「也许你能把他们变成犹太复国主义者。」

===Original===
“That is absolutely impossible.”

===Chinese===
「那是绝对不可能的。」

===Original===
“Did you talk to them about Zionism?”

===Chinese===
「你同他们谈犹太复国主义了吗？」

===Original===
“It is useless to talk to these people whose first pride is that they are *British*.”

===Chinese===
「与这些人谈是无用的，他们的第一骄傲，便是他们是*英国的*。」

===Original===
“But they’re *not*.”

===Chinese===
「可他们*并不是*啊。」

===Original===
“You should tell them so. They will tell you they are British of the Jewish persuasion. Ah it has revolted me to hear them talk of this war, the British Empire, and the subject races.”

===Chinese===
「你应当这样告诉他们。他们会向你说，他们是犹太信仰的英国人。啊，听他们谈这场战争、谈大英帝国、谈那些臣属种族，我实在感到憎恶。」

===Original===
“I know; disgusting; but very British. But the British Empire has done a good deal for the Jews and I suppose the Jews feel loyal.”

===Chinese===
「我知道；令人厌恶；可这正是十足的英国做派。不过大英帝国确实为犹太人做过不少事，我想犹太人也感到忠诚。」

===Original===
“That is true. But what they do not see is that they are not, and never can be, British; that the British do not accept them as such.”

===Chinese===
「那是真的。可他们没有看到的是：他们不是英国人，永远也成不了英国人；英国人并不把他们当英国人接纳。」

===Original===
“That’s true I know; the general attitude; but there are no disabilities. The Jews are free in England.”

===Chinese===
「这我知道，是真的；普遍的态度是这样；可并没有任何权利限制。犹太人在英国是自由的。」

===Original===
“They are free; to the honour of England in all history. But they are nevertheless Jews and not Englishmen. Those Jews who deny, or try to ignore this have ceased to be Jews without becoming Englishmen. The toleration for Jews, moreover, will last only so long as the English remain in ignorance of the immense and increasing power and influence of the Jew in this country. Once that is generally recognised, even England will have its antisemitic movement.”

===Chinese===
「他们是自由的；这是英格兰全部历史上的一桩光荣。但他们终究是犹太人，不是英国人。那些否认这一点、或试图无视这一点的犹太人，已不再是犹太人，却也并未变成英国人。再者，对犹太人的宽容，只能持续到英国人仍然不知道犹太人在这个国家里那巨大而日增的势力与影响之时。一旦此事被普遍认清，连英国也将有它的反犹运动。」

===Original===
“*Never.* England can assimilate anything. Look at the races that have been built into us in the past.”

===Chinese===
「*绝不会。*英格兰什么都能同化。看看过去那些已经筑进我们身体里的种族吧。」

===Original===
“No nation can assimilate the Jew.”

===Chinese===
「没有任何民族能同化犹太人。」

===Original===
“What about intermarriages?”

===Chinese===
「那通婚呢？」

===Original===
“That is the minority.”

===Chinese===
「那是少数。」

===Original===
“If it was right to make a refuge for the Jews here it is still right and England will never regret it.”

===Chinese===
「倘若当初在这里为犹太人立一个避难所是对的，那它如今仍然是对的，英格兰永远不会后悔。」

===Original===
“Believe me it is not so simple. Remember that British Jewry is perpetually and increasingly reinforced by immigration from those countries where Jews are segregated and ever more terribly persecuted. At present there is England, both for the Jewish speculator and the refugee pauper. But for those who look at *facts*, the end of this possibility is in sight. The time for the closing of this last door is approaching.”

===Chinese===
「相信我，事情并非如此简单。要记得，英国犹太人正不断被移民补充着，且日益增多——来自那些犹太人被隔离、被迫害得日益可怕的国家。目前还有英格兰在，既容犹太投机商，也容逃难的贫民。可在正视*事实*的人看来，这重可能的尽头已在望。这最后一扇门关闭的时辰正在临近。」

===Original===
“I don’t believe England will ever do it. How can they? Where will the Jews go? It’s impossible to think of. It will be the end of England if we begin that sort of thing.”

===Chinese===
「我不信英格兰会走到那一步。他们怎么能呢？犹太人将往哪里去？这连想都没法想。我们要是开了那种头，英格兰就完了。」

===Original===
“It may be the beginning of Jewish nationality. Ah at least this visit has reawakened all the Zionist in me.”

===Chinese===
「那也许是犹太民族性的开端。啊，至少，这次拜访把我身上的犹太复国主义全都唤醒了。」

===Original===
“It is a glorious idea.” His evening *had* been eventful; sending him back to the freshness of the days at Basel. It was then, she thought, at the moment he was bathed in the unceasing beauty of the surroundings, and immersed within it, in inextinguishable association with the students of the photographs, poised blissfully irresponsible in a permanent boundless beguilement, himself the most untouched of all, the most smoothly rounded, and elastically surrendered with his deep-singing, childlike confident face, that he had been touched and shaped and sent forth; his future set towards a single separate thing, the narrowest, strangest, most unknown of movements, far away from the wide European life that had flowed through his mind.

===Chinese===
「那是一个光辉的主意。」他的这个晚上*确实*多事；把他送回了巴塞尔（Basel）那些日子的清新里。就是那时，她想，在他沐浴于周遭不歇的美、又沉浸其中的那一刻，与照片上那些学生们结着扑不灭的交情，悬在一场永久的、无边的陶醉里，极乐而无所承担，他自己又是所有人里最未被触碰的、最光滑圆整的，带着他那张深沉歌唱着、孩子般自信的脸，弹性地顺从着——就在那时，他被触碰了、被塑了形、被打发出去了；他的未来被设定朝向一件单独孤立的东西，那最窄、最怪、最不为人知的运动，远离那曾经流过他头脑的宽广的欧洲生活。

===Original===
“It is a dream, far-off. In England hardly even that.” There was a blankness before him. Unconscious of his youth, and his radiating charm, distilled from the modern world; Frenchman, Russian, philosophical German-brained, he sat there white-faced, an old old Jew, immeasurably old, cut off, alone with his conviction, facing the blank spaces of the future. Why could he not be content to be a European? She swayed, dragging at the knot. In his deeply saturated intelligence there still was a balance on the side from which he had declared to his father, that he was first a man; then a Jew. By the accidents of living, this might be cherished. The voices of the night cried out against the treachery. She glanced remorsefully across at him and recognised with a sharp pang of pity, in his own eyes, the well-known eyes wide open towards the darkness where she sat invisible, the look he had described﻿ ﻿… *wehmütig*; in spite of his sheltered happy prosperous youth it was there; he *belonged* to those millions whose sufferings he had revealed to her, a shadow lying forever across the bright unseeing confidence of Europe, hopeless. And now, at this moment, standing out from their midst the strange beautiful Old Testament figure in modern clothes; the fine beautifully moulded Hebrew head, so like his own.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「那是一场梦，遥遥的。在英国，连梦都几乎算不上。」他面前是一片空白。他不知道自己的青春，不知道自己那份从现代世界里蒸馏出来、四下辐射的魅力；法国人、俄国人、德国哲学脑，他坐在那里，脸色苍白，一个老而又老的犹太人，无可计量的老，被隔绝，独自守着他的信念，面对着未来的空白地带。他为什么不能安于做一个欧洲人呢？她晃了一晃，拽着那个死结。在他那深深浸透的智识里，天平依然倾向他曾据以向父亲宣告的那一边：他先是人，然后才是犹太人。凭着生活的种种偶然，这一点或许还能珍护住。夜的各种声音呐喊着反对这背叛。她悔疚地瞥过他那边，带着一阵尖锐的怜惜，在他自己的眼睛里——那双朝她隐身坐着的黑暗睁大的、熟知的眼睛里——认出了他曾描述过的那种神情……*wehmütig*（德语：悒郁）；尽管他有荫庇、幸福而丰足的青春，它还是在那里；他*属于*那几百万人，属于他向她揭示过的那些苦难，一道永远横在明亮而不自知的欧洲信心上的阴影，无望。而此刻，从他们中间凸显出来的，是那个穿现代衣服的、奇异而美丽的《旧约》身影；那颗塑造精美的希伯来头颅，那么像他自己的。……

===Original===
“But it *is* extraordinary; that just when everything is at its worst, this idea should have arisen.﻿ ﻿… It’s all very well for people to laugh at Micawber.”

===Chinese===
「可这*确实*不寻常；偏偏在一切都糟到极点的时候，这个主意冒了出来。……人们尽管去笑米考伯（Micawber）好了。」

===Original===
“Who is this man?”

===Chinese===
「这个人是谁？」

===Original===
“The man who is always waiting for something to turn up. Things *do* turn up, exactly at the right moment. It doesn’t mean fatalism. I don’t believe in laisser-aller as a principle; but there is something *in* things, something the people who make plans and think they are thinking out everything in advance, don’t know; their oblivion of it, while they go busily on knowing exactly what they are going to do and why, even at picnics, is a terrible thing. And somehow they always fail.”

===Chinese===
「就是那个总在等着有什么事情自己冒出来的人。事情*确实*会冒出来，恰恰在最合适的时刻。这不等于宿命论。我不信把放任自流（laisser-aller）奉为原则；可事物里头*有*某种东西，某种那些制订计划、自以为把一切预先想周全的人所不知道的东西；他们一面忙忙碌碌往前赶，确切知道自己要做什么、为什么做——连野餐时都这样——一面却对它浑然不觉，这是件可怕的事。而他们不知怎么总要失败。」

===Original===
“They do not by any means always fail. In all concerted action there must be a plan. Herzl is certainly a man with a plan.”

===Chinese===
「他们绝非总是失败。凡协同的行动，必得有一个计划。赫茨尔（Herzl）无疑是一个有计划的人。」

===Original===
“Yes but it’s different; his idea *is* his plan. It isn’t *clever*. And now that it is here it seems so simple. Why was it never put forward before?”

===Chinese===
「是啊，可这不同；他的主意*就是*他的计划。它并不*高明*。可如今它既已在这里，又显得那么简单。为什么从前从来没人提出过？」

===Original===
“The greatest ideas are always simple; though not in their resultants. This dream however, has always been present with Jews.”

===Chinese===
「最伟大的主意总是简单的；虽然其后果不然。然而这个梦，始终都在犹太人中间。」

===Original===
“Of course. The Zionist Movement, coming now, when it is most wanted, is not altogether Herzl. It’s that strange thing, the thing that makes you stare, in history. A sort of shape﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「当然。犹太复国主义运动此刻到来，正当最需要它的时候，并不全是赫茨尔的功劳。它是那种怪东西，那种让你在历史面前瞠目的东西。一种形状……」

===Original===
“It is the collective pressure of life; an unseen movement. But if you feel this what now becomes of your individualism? Eh?” He chuckled his delight﻿ ﻿… passing so easily and leisurely to personal things.

===Chinese===
「这是生命的集体压力；一场看不见的运动。可你既感到了这个，你的个人主义如今又成了什么？嗯？」他咯咯地笑出他的欢喜……就这样轻易而从容地转到个人话题上去了。

===Original===
“Oh the shape doesn’t affect the *individual*, in himself. There’s something behind all those outside things that goes on independently of them, something much more wonderful.”

===Chinese===
「哦，那形状并不影响*个体*自身。在所有那些外部事物背后，有一样独立于它们继续走着的东西，一样奇妙得多的东西。」

===Original===
“You are wrong. What you call the shape, affects most profoundly every individual in spite of himself.”

===Chinese===
「你错了。你所称的那个形状，深深地影响着每一个个体，由不得他自己。」

===Original===
“But he must be an individual to be affected at all, and no two people are affected in the same way﻿ ﻿… after this evening I’m more of an individualist than before. It is *relief* to know that science is a smaller kind of truth than philosophy. The real difficulty is not between science and religion at all, but between religion and *philosophy*. Philosophy seems to think science assumes too much to begin with and can never get any further than usefulness.”

===Chinese===
「可他总得先是个个体，才会受影响，而没有两个人受影响的方式是相同的……经过这个晚上，我比从前更是个个人主义者了。得知科学是一种比哲学更小的真理，真是一种*宽慰*。真正的困难根本不在科学与宗教之间，而在宗教与*哲学*之间。哲学似乎认为，科学一开头就预设太多，永远走不出『有用』一步。」

===Original===
“Science can afford to smile at this.”

===Chinese===
「科学尽可以对此一笑置之。」

===Original===
“And that religion is philosophically unsound, though modern religious controversy is metaphysical.”

===Chinese===
「还有，说宗教在哲学上站不住脚——虽说现代的宗教论争全是形而上学的。」

===Original===
“*All* controversy depends from differences in estimation of term significations.”

===Chinese===
「*一切*论争，皆系于对词项意义之估量的分歧。」

===Original===
“That’s why arguments are so maddening; even small discussions; people go rushing on, getting angrier and angrier, talking about quite different things, especially men, because they never want to get at the truth, only to score a point.”

===Chinese===
「这就是为什么争起来那么叫人发疯；连小小的讨论也是；人们冲啊冲啊，火气越来越大，谈的全然是两码事——尤其是男人——因为他们从来不想弄清真相，只想赢一分。」

===Original===
“You are unjust; many men put truth before any other consideration whatsoever. It is not only unjust, it is most bad for you to hold this cynical estimation.”

===Chinese===
「你不公正；许多男人把真理置于其余一切考虑之上。持这种愤世的估量，不仅有失公正，而且于你大有害处。」

===Original===
“Well, men arguing always look like that to women. That’s why women always go off at a tangent; because they reply not to what men *say* but to what they *mean*, which is to *score a point*, which *anybody* can do, with practice, and while they hold on to the point they mean to score, they are revealed, under all sorts of circumstances, all sorts of things about them are as plain as a pikestaff, to a woman, and the results of these things; so that she suddenly finds herself saying something that sounds quite irrelevant, but isn’t.”

===Chinese===
「唉，男人争起来，在女人眼里永远是那副样子。这就是为什么女人总是岔到切线上去；因为她们回答的不是男人*说*的，而是他们*意指*的，也就是*赢一分*，这个*谁*都办得到，练练就行；而男人们一边死死咬住他们想赢的那一分，一边就被亮了相——种种处境之下，他们身上种种的事，连同这些事的后果，在女人看来都明晃晃像根竿子；于是她忽然发现自己说出的东西听上去全然不相干，其实不然。」

===Original===
“Nevertheless there is honourable controversy, and most fruitful.”

===Chinese===
「尽管如此，世上仍有光明正大的论争，而且极有成果。」

===Original===
“There are people here and there with open minds. Very few.”

===Chinese===
「这里那里总有些头脑敞开的人。很少。」

===Original===
“The point is not the few, but that they *are*.”

===Chinese===
「要点不在其少，而在他们*存在*。」

===Original===
“The few just men, who save the city.”

===Chinese===
「那少数的义人，他们救了那座城。」

===Original===
“Exactly.”

===Chinese===
「正是。」

===Original===
“But even existence is not quite certain.”

===Chinese===
「可就连存在，也并不十分确定。」

===Original===
“*What* is this?”

===Chinese===
「这*是*什么话？」

===Original===
“Descartes *said*, my existence is certain; that is a fallacy.”

===Chinese===
「笛卡尔*说过*，我的存在是确定的；那是个谬误。」

===Original===
“If this is a fallacy for metaphysic, so much the worse for metaphysic.”

===Chinese===
「若这在形而上学看来是谬误，那只能怪形而上学更糟。」

===Original===
“That is *argumentum ad hominem*.”

===Chinese===
「这是*诉诸人身的论证*（argumentum ad hominem）。」

===Original===
“I am not afraid of it.”

===Chinese===
「我不怕它。」

===Original===
“But what can you put in place of metaphysic?”

===Chinese===
「可你能把什么放到形而上学的位置上呢？」

===Original===
“Life is larger.”

===Chinese===
「生活更大。」

===Original===
“I know. I know. I know. Something exists. Metaphysic admits that. I nearly shouted when Dr. McHibbert said that. It’s enough. It answers everything. Even to have seen it for a moment is enough. The first time I thought of it I nearly died of joy. Descartes should have said ‘I am aware that there *is* something, therefore I am.’ If I am, other people are; but that does not seem to matter. That is their own affair.”

===Chinese===
「我知道。我知道。我知道。有东西存在。形而上学承认这一点。麦克希伯特博士说这话时，我差点喊出声来。这就够了。它回答了一切。哪怕只看见过它一刻，也够了。我头一回想到这一点时，差点欢喜得死过去。笛卡尔本该说：『我觉察到*有*东西存在，故我在。』倘若我在，别人也在；可这似乎无关紧要。那是他们自己的事。」

===Original===
“Beware of solipsism.”

===Chinese===
「当心唯我论。」

===Original===
“I don’t care what it is called. It is certainty. You *must* begin with the individual. There we are again.” There was an end to the conversation that could not be shared. The words of it already formed, intangibly, waited, ready to disappear, until she should be alone and could read them on a clear background. If she stayed they would disappear irrevocably. She rose, bidding him a hurried good night, suddenly aware of the busily sleeping household, friendly guardian of this wide leisurely nightlife. He too was aware and grateful, picking his way cautiously through the shadows of the large room, sheltered from his loneliness, invisibly enclosed by the waiting incommunicable statement that yet left him, accusing him of wilful blindness, so cruelly outside.

===Chinese===
「我才不管它叫什么名字。它是确定性。你*必须*从个体开始。我们又回到这儿了。」这段无法分享的谈话到了尽头。它的话已经成形，无形地候着，随时会消散，只等她独自一人，好把它们读到一块干净的底子上去。她若再待下去，它们就会不可挽回地消散。她起了身，匆匆向他道了晚安，忽然意识到那忙碌睡熟的一家人——这场宽绰从容的夜生活的友善守卫。他也有此意识，且心怀感激，小心翼翼地拣着路穿过大屋子的阴影，得了荫蔽、免于他的孤独，又被那句等候着的、无法传达的陈述无形地围拢——那陈述却仍把他撇在那样残忍的外面，指控他存心失明。

---

===Original===
“Materialism,” scribbled Miriam eagerly, “has the recommendation of being a Monism, and therefore a more perfect explanation of the universe than a Dualism can be.﻿ ﻿… And Matter forms one great whole, persisting through many ages. Mind appears in the form of separate individuals, isolated from each other by Matter, and each ceasing, so far as observation goes, after a very few years. Also the changes which we can observe Mind to make in Matter are comparatively insignificant, while a very slight change in Matter will either destroy mind, or, at least, remove it from the only circumstances in which we can observe its existence. All these characteristics make matter appear much more powerful and important than mind.”

===Chinese===
「唯物主义，」米丽亚姆急切地潦草记着，「有一条可取之处：它是一种一元论，因此对宇宙的解释比任何二元论都更完备。……而且物质构成一个巨大的整体，历经许多世代持存。心灵则以一个个分离的个体的形式出现，被物质彼此隔绝，而且——就观察所及——每一个都在短短几年后消逝。再者，我们所能观察到的心灵对物质作出的改变，相对而言微不足道；而物质极轻微的改变，就足以或者毁掉心灵，或者至少把它从我们唯一能观察其存在的境况中移走。这一切特性，都使物质显得比心灵强大得多、重要得多。」

===Original===
“I consider this a very strong reasoning,” muttered Mr. Shatov.

===Chinese===
「我认为这是一个十分有力的推理，」沙托夫先生低声道。

===Original===
“Ssh. Wait.” He was sitting intent, with an awakened youthful student’s face, meeting, through her agency, in England, a first-class intelligence. He would hear the beautiful building up, strophe and antistrophe, of the apparently unassailable argument, the pause, and then, in the same shapely cadences, its complete destruction, forever, the pleasant face smiling at the audience above the ruins, like a child who has just shattered a castle of bricks.

===Chinese===
「嘘。等等。」他专注地坐着，一张觉醒的青年学生式的脸，正借着她的引介，在英格兰会晤一流的智识。他将听到那条貌似坚不可摧的论证如何漂亮地搭建起来，正一折，反一折；听到那停顿，然后，仍用同样漂亮的音步，把它整个摧毁，永远摧毁，那张和善的脸在废墟之上朝听众微笑，像个刚推倒一座积木城堡的孩子。

===Original===
“Idealism was weakened by being supposed to be bound up with certain theological doctrines which became discredited. All these things account for the great strength of materialism some years ago. There has been a reaction against this, but the extent of the reaction has been exaggerated.”

===Chinese===
「唯心主义被削弱了，因为它被认为与某些后来声名扫地的神学学说捆在一起。这一切都解释了几年前唯物主义的强大。后来有了对此的反动，但反动的程度被夸大了。」

===Original===
“Quite so.”

===Chinese===
「正是如此。」

===Original===
“Wait, wait.”

===Chinese===
「等着，等着。」

===Original===
“It still remains the belief to which most people tend on first leaving an unreflecting position. And many remain there. Science is a large element in our lives now, and if we try to make science serve as metaphysic, we get materialism. Nor is it to be wished﻿—even by idealists﻿—that materialism should become too weak. For idealism is seldom really vigorous except in those who have had a serious struggle with materialism.﻿ ﻿… It would be very difficult to disprove materialism, if we once accepted the reality of matter as a thing in itself. But, as we saw when considering dualism, such a reality of matter is untenable. And this conclusion is obviously more fatal to materialism than it was to dualism. And again, if materialism is true, all our thoughts are produced by purely material antecedents. These are quite blind, and are just as likely to produce falsehood as truth. We have thus no reason for believing any of our conclusions﻿—including the truth of materialism, which is therefore a self-contradictory hypothesis.”

===Chinese===
「它仍然是大多数人初离不假思索的立场后倾向相信的东西。而许多人就停在那里。如今科学是我们生活中的一大成分，而如果我们试图让科学充任形而上学，我们得到的就是唯物主义。但唯物主义变得太弱，也并非可愿之事——哪怕对唯心主义者而言。——因为唯心主义很少真正旺盛，除了在那些与唯物主义认真搏斗过的人身上。……唯物主义会很难驳倒，倘若我们一旦接受物质作为自在之物的实在性。可是，正如我们考察二元论时看到的那样，物质的这样一种实在性是站不住的。而这个结论对唯物主义显然比对二元论更为致命。再者，倘若唯物主义为真，我们的一切思想便都由纯粹物质的前因产生。这些前因全然盲目，产生谬误与产生真理的可能性一样大。于是我们便没有理由相信我们的任何结论——包括唯物主义之真——因此它是一个自相矛盾的假设。」

===Original===
“I find this too easily stated.”

===Chinese===
「我觉得这陈述得未免太轻易。」

===Original===
Then God is *proved*.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
这么说，上帝就被*证明*了。……

===Original===
“You weren’t here *before*. Philosophy is not difficult. It is common sense systematised and clarified.”﻿ ﻿… wayfaring men, though fools, shall not err therein. It is not what people think but what they know. Thought is words. Philosophy will never find words to express life; the philosopher is the same as the criminal?

===Chinese===
「你*先前*不在这里。哲学并不难。它是有条理化、明晰化了的常识。」……行路的人虽愚昧，也不致失迷（wayfaring men, though fools, shall not err therein）。要紧的不是人们想什么，而是他们知道什么。思想即是词语。哲学永远找不到表达生活的词语；哲学家与罪犯是一样的？

===Original===
“He seems to say spirit when he means *life*.”

===Chinese===
「他指*生命*的时候，说的似乎总是精神。」

===Original===
“What *is* life?”

===Chinese===
「生命*究竟*是什么？」

===Original===
“Moreover presentationism is incompatible with the truth of general propositions﻿—and therefore with itself, since it can only be expressed by a general proposition. And closer analysis shows that it is incompatible even with particular propositions, since these involve both the union of two terms and the use of general ideas.” People know this faintly when they say things; not *why*; but faintly everyone knows that nothing can be said. Then why listen any more? Because if you know, exactly, that nothing can be said and the expert reasons for it, you know for certain in times of weakness, how much there is that might be expressed if there were any way of expressing it.﻿ ﻿… But there was no need to listen any more since God was proved by the impossibility of his absence, like an invisible star. No one seemed at all disturbed; the lecturer least of all. Perhaps he felt that the effects of real realisation would be so tremendous that he could not face them. The thought of no God made life simply silly. The thought of God made it embarrassing. If a hand suddenly appeared writing on the wall, what would he do? He would blush; standing there as a competitor, fighting for his theories, amongst the theories of other men. Yet if there were no philosophers, if the world were imagined without philosophy, there would be nothing but theology, getting more and more superstitious.

===Chinese===
「再者，呈现论与一般命题之真不相容——因而与它自身不相容，因为它只能用一个一般命题来表达。而更细的分析表明，它甚至与特殊命题也不相容，因为这些命题既涉及两个词项的结合，又涉及一般观念的使用。」人们开口说话时是隐隐知道这一点的；并不知*为什么*；但每个人都隐隐知道，什么也没法说。那为什么还要听下去？因为倘若你确切地知道没法说、也知道那些行家的理由，那么在软弱的时刻你就能确知：倘若真有表达的法子，该有多少东西可以被表达出来。……可如今再听下去已无必要，因为上帝已由他缺席之不可能得到证明，像一颗看不见的星。似乎谁也不为此不安；讲演者最不。也许他感到，真正觉悟的后果会大到无法面对。没有上帝这一想头，使生活简直无聊。有上帝这一想头，使生活尴尬。倘若忽然有一只手出现，在墙上写字，他会怎么办？他会脸红；站在那里当一名竞争者，在旁人的诸理论中间为自己的理论而战。然而，倘若没有哲学家，倘若把世界想象成没有哲学的样子，那就只剩神学了，越来越迷信的神学。

===Original===
Everybody was so *calm*. The calmness of insanity. Nobody quite all there. Yet intelligent. *What* were they all thinking about, wreathed in films of intelligent insanity; watching the performance in the intervals of lives filled with words that meant nothing﻿ ﻿… breath was more than words; the fact of breathing﻿ ﻿… but everyone was in such a hurry.

===Chinese===
人人都这么*平静*。疯狂的平静。没有一个人完完全全在场。却又聪明。他们个个都在想些什么呢，裹在一层聪明疯狂的薄膜里；在间隙里看着这场表演——那生活满是毫无意义的词语……呼吸比词语多；呼吸这一事实……可人人都那么匆忙。

===Original===
“I would ask”﻿ ﻿… one horrified glance revealed his profile quivering as he hesitated. A louder, confident, dictatorial English voice had rung out simultaneously from the other side of the hall. He would have to sit down, shaken by his brave attempt. But to the whole evening, the deep gentle tones had been added, welling through and beyond the Englishman’s strident, neat proclamation, and containing, surely everyone must hear it, so much of the answer to the essential question. The chairman hesitated, turned decisively and the other man sat down.

===Chinese===
「我想问」……惊惶的一瞥，看见他侧面的轮廓正颤抖着迟疑。一个更响亮、自信而专断的英国嗓音，同时从大厅另一侧轰然响起。他眼看只好坐下，被他那勇敢的尝试震得发颤。可是，对这整个晚上来说，那深沉温和的嗓音已被添了上来，涌出并漫过那英国人尖利利落的宣言，其中含有——想必人人都听得出——那么多对那个根本问题的回答。主席迟疑了一下，果断地转过身，另一个人坐下了。

===Original===
“What the lecturer makes of the psycho-physical parallelism?”

===Chinese===
「讲演者对于心身平行论，是作何见解的？」

===Original===
He drove home his question on a note of reproachful expostulation and sat down drawn together, with bent head and eye downcast, but listening intently with his serenely singing child’s brow. Miriam was instantly sorry that his words had got through, their naked definiteness changing the eloquent tone, sharpening it to a weapon, a borrowed weapon.

===Chinese===
他把问题钉在一声责怨诘问的音调上，坐下时身子缩作一团，垂着头，眼望着地，却仍在凝神细听，凭着那片安详歌唱的孩子般的额头。

===Original===
“That’s it,” she breathed, hoping the lecturer’s answer would throw some light on the meaning of the fascinating phrase, floating before her, fresh from far-off philosophical battlefields, bright from centuries of contemplation, flashing out now, today, in Europe triumphantly, in desperate encounters. The lecturer was on his feet, gleaming towards their centre of the audience his recognition of the clean thrust.

===Chinese===
「就是它，」她低呼一声，指望讲演者的回答能给这个迷人短语的意义照进一点光——它漂在她眼前，刚从遥远的哲学战场新鲜归来，被几百年的沉思磨亮，此刻、今天，在欧洲胜利地、在殊死的交锋中闪现。讲演者站起身，朝听众中他们这一角闪闪送出他的领情——领受那一记干净的突刺。

===Original===
“The correlation between physical and mental gives an empirical support to materialism.” That *couldn’t* be spirited away. The scientists *swore* there was no break; so convincingly; perhaps they would yet win and prove it. “But it is necessary to distinguish between metaphysic and psychology. Psychology, like physical science, is to be put to the score of our knowledge of matter.”

===Chinese===
「物理与心理之间的相关，给了唯物主义一个经验的支持。」这可是*打发*不掉的。科学家们*赌咒*说其间没有断裂；说得那样令人信服；也许他们终究会赢，会证明它。「但必须把形而上学与心理学区分开来。心理学，同物理科学一样，要记在我们对物质的知识账上。」

===Original===
“In which he doesn’t believe,” scoffed Miriam, distractedly poised between Mr. Shatov’s drama and the prospect opening within her mind.

===Chinese===
「而那个账，他是不信的，」米丽亚姆讥道，心神不定地悬在沙托夫先生的戏局与她头脑里正展开的前景之间。

===Original===
“I find this a most arbitrary statement.”

===Chinese===
「我以为这是一个最为专断的断言。」

===Original===
“Yes, *rather*” murmured Miriam emphatically, and waited for a moment as if travelling with him along his line of thought. But he was recovering, had recovered, did not seem to be dwelling or moving in any relation to what he had said, appeared to be disinterestedly listening to the next question.

===Chinese===
「是啊，*可不*，」米丽亚姆加重语气喃喃道，还等了片刻，仿佛陪他沿着他的思路走了一程。可他正在恢复，已经恢复了，似乎并不在他说过的话里盘桓或移动，倒像是不偏不倚地在听下一个问题。

===Original===
“Besides,” she said, “the empirical method is a most important method, and jolly.”﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「再说，」她说，「经验方法是个非常重要的方法，而且痛快。」……

===Original===
“Poor chap; what a stupidity is this question.” Miriam smiled solicitously, but she had travelled back enraptured across nine years to the day, now only yesterday, of her first meeting with her newly recovered word. Jevons. From the first the sienna brown volume had been wonderful, the only one of the English books that had any connection with life; and that day, Sunday afternoon prep in the dining-room, with the laburnum and pink may outside the window changing as she read from a tantalising reproach to a vivid encirclement of her being by all the spring scenes she had lived through, coming and going, the sight and scent and shimmering movement of them, as if she moved, bodiless and expanded, about in their midst. Something about the singing, lifting word appearing suddenly on the page, even before she had grasped its meaning, intensified the relation to life of the little hard motionless book, leaving it, when she had read on, centred round the one statement; the rest remaining in shadow, interesting but in some strange way ill-gotten.

===Chinese===
「可怜的家伙；这问题多蠢哪。」米丽亚姆关切地笑着，可她已欢然神驰，跨过九年，回到那一天——如今只是昨天——她初遇那个新近寻回的词的日子。杰文斯（Jevons）。从起初，那本赭褐色的小书就是奇妙的，是那些英文书里唯一与生活有干系的一本；而那一天，礼拜天下午在餐室里做功课，窗外的金链花与粉红山楂花，随着她的阅读，从一种撩人的责备，变成她经历过的全部春景对她存在的一场鲜活围拥——来来去去，它们的形、香与闪烁的动静，仿佛她无形无骸、胀大了身子，在它们中间走动。那个歌唱着、扬起来的词忽然出现在页面上，甚至在她抓住它的意思之前，就加深了这本小小的、坚硬的、一动不动的书与生活的关系；待她读下去，全书便围着那一个论断定了轴心；其余的都留在阴影里，有趣，却不知怎么是来路不正的。

===Original===
The recovery of the forgotten word at the centre of “the philosophical problems of the present day” cast a fresh glow of reality across her schooldays. The efforts she had so blindly made, so indolently and prodigally sacrificing her chances of success in the last examination, to the few things that had made the world shine about her, had been in some way right, with a shapeliness and fruitfulness of their own. Her struggles with Jevons had been bread cast upon the waters﻿ ﻿… how differently the word now fell into her mind, with “intuition” happily at home there to keep it company. If materialism could be supported empirically, there was something in it, something in matter that had not yet been found out.﻿ ﻿… Meantime philosophy proved God. And Hegel had not brushed away the landscape. There was God *and* the landscape.

===Chinese===
把「当世哲学诸问题」中心那个被遗忘的词寻了回来，给她的学生时代投上一道新鲜的实在之光。她曾那么盲目地作过的努力——那么懒散而奢靡地，牺牲掉她在最后一场考试里成功的机会，去换那几件曾让世界在她四周发光的东西——在某种意义上原是对的，自有其端整与丰饶。她与杰文斯的苦斗，原是撒在水上的面包……如今这个词落进她心里，落得多么不同了——「直觉」正安然住在那里，与它作伴。倘若唯物主义竟能得到经验的支持，那它里面就有点什么，物质里就有点什么还没被找出来。……与此同时，哲学证明了上帝。而黑格尔并未把风景扫掉。上帝*和*风景，都在。

===Original===
“Materialism isn’t dead *yet*,” she heard herself say recklessly.

===Chinese===
「唯物主义*还*没死呢，」她听见自己鲁莽地说。

===Original===
“More. Chemistry will yet carry us further than this kind of metaphysical surmising.”

===Chinese===
「更有甚者。化学终将把我们带到比这类形而上学臆测更远的地方。」

===Original===
Taking part, even being with someone who took part in the proceedings, altered them. Some hidden chain of evidence was broken. Things no longer stood quietly in the air for acceptance or rejection. The memory of the evening would be a memory of social life, isolated revelations of personality.

===Chinese===
参与了进去——哪怕只是同一个参与了进去的人坐在一起——就改变了这一切。某条隐藏的证据之链断了。事物不再静静地悬在半空，听凭接受或拒斥。对今晚的回忆，将是一段社交生活的回忆，一段段孤立的人格启示。

## VIII / VIII

===Original===
