When they emerged from the dusty shabbiness of the Euston Road it was suddenly a perfect June morning. Now was the moment. She opened the letter unnoticed, with her eyes on the sunlit park-lined vista … “London owes much to the fact that its main thoroughfares run east and west; walk westward in the morning down any one of them, or in the afternoon towards the east and whenever the sun shines you will see” … and without arousing his attention hurriedly read the few lines. Was that man still in London, trying to explain it to himself, or had he been obliged to go away, or perhaps to die? London is heaven and can’t be explained. To be sent away is to be sent out of heaven.

===Chinese===

他们从尤斯顿路尘垢狼藉的寒碜里走出来时，眼前忽地是一个完美无瑕的六月清晨。就是此刻。她趁人不留意拆开了信，眼睛望着阳光满泻、公园夹峙的远景……「伦敦之所以有如此气象，多亏它的几条干道皆作东西走向；清晨沿随便哪一条向西行，或是午后向东去，只要太阳露脸，你便会看见」……她不曾惹起他的注意，匆匆读完了那几行字。那个人还在伦敦么，还在设法向自己把它解说清楚么，还是已经不得不远走，抑或，已经死了？伦敦是天堂，无从解说。被送走，便是被逐出天堂。

===Original===

“I’ve been telling,” useless words, coming thin and helpless out of darkness and pressing against darkness … a desperate clutching at a borrowed performance to keep alive and keep on … “my employers what I think of them just lately.”

“Excellent. What have you told?”

His unconscious voice steadied her; as the darkness drove nearer bringing thoughts that must not arrive. The morning changed to a painted scene, from which she turned away, catching the glance of the leaves nearby, trickily painted, as she turned to steer the eloquence flowing up in her mind.

===Chinese===

「我一直在告诉，」无用的话语，从黑暗里出来时又细又弱、全无依傍，又抵在黑暗上……绝望地攫住一场借来的表演，好活下去、好继续下去……「我的雇主们，我近来对他们的看法。」

「好极了。你都告诉了些什么？」

他那浑然不觉的声音使她稳住了；黑暗却越逼越近，载来那些决不容到来的念头。清晨化作一幅画就的布景，她从中转过脸去，瞥见近旁树叶递来的眼色，也画得刁钻，一面转舵去驾驭心中正涌上来的滔滔言辞。

===Original===

“Well, it was a whole point of view I saw suddenly in the train coming back after Easter. I read an essay, about a superannuated clerk, an extraordinary thing, very simple and well written, not in the least like an essay. But there was something in it that was horrible. The employers gave the old man a pension, with humorous benevolence. He is so surprised and so blissfully happy in having nothing to do but look at the green world for the rest of the time, that he feels nothing but gratitude. That’s all right, from his point of view, being that sort of old man. But how dare the firm be humorously benevolent? It is no case for *humour*. It is *not* funny that prosperous people can use up lives on small fixed salaries that never increase beyond a certain point, no matter how well the employers get on, even if for the last few years they give pensions. And they don’t give pensions. If they do, they are thought most benevolent. The author, who is evidently in a way a thoughtful man, ought to have known this. He just wrote a thing that looks charming on the surface and is beautifully written and is really perfectly horrible and disgusting. Well, I suddenly thought employers ought to know. I don’t know what can be done. *I* don’t want a pension. I hate working for a salary as it is. But employers ought to *know* how fearfully unfair everything is. They ought to have their complacency smashed up.” He was engrossed. His foreign intelligence sympathised. Then she was right.

===Chinese===

「是这样，复活节后搭火车回来的时候，我忽然看出了整整一套观点。我读到一篇随笔，写一个到了年纪被辞退的老职员，一件出奇的作品，写得极简净漂亮，一点也不像随笔。可里头有一样东西很可怕。雇主们带着打趣的仁慈，给了那老头儿一笔养老金。他又是惊讶又是极乐地幸福——往后再没有别的事可做，只消望着绿色的世界——心里只剩下感激。从他那个观点看，这没什么不好，他就是这样一种老人。可那家商号怎么敢打趣地行善？这根本不是*打趣*的事。兴旺的人们尽可以拿小小的固定薪水把一条条性命用旧用尽——那薪水永不会涨过某一点，无论雇主们的日子过得多么发达，哪怕最后几年发给养老金——这*并不*可笑。何况他们并不发养老金。当真发了，就要被人看作天大的善人。那位作者，分明也算个有思想的人，本该明白这一层。他偏偏写出一件东西，表面看来动人，文笔也漂亮，骨子里却可怕恶心到了家。于是我突然想，雇主们应当知道。我不知道能有什么办法。*我*不要养老金。我本来就恨为薪水做工。可雇主们应当*知道*，一切是何等骇人的不公。他们那份自满，应当被砸个粉碎。」他听得出神。他那外国的智识起了共鸣。那么她是对的。

===Original===

“Anyhow. The worst of it is that my employers are so frightfully nice. But the principle’s the same, the frightful unfairness. And it happened that just before I went away, just as Mr. Hancock was going off for his holiday, he had been annoyed by one of his Mudie books going back before he had read it, and no others coming that were on his list, and he suddenly said to me in a grumbling tone ‘you might keep an eye on my Mudie books.’ I was simply furious. Because before I began looking after the books—which he had never asked me to do, and was quite my own idea—it was simply a muddle. They all kept lists in a way, at least put down books when they hit upon one they thought they would like, and then sent the whole list *in*, and never kept a copy, and of course forgot what they’d put down. Well, I privately took to copying those lists and crossing off the books as they came and keeping on sending in the rest of the list again and again till they had *all* come. Well, I know a wise person would not have been in a rage and would meekly have rushed about keeping more of an eye than ever. But I can’t stand unfairness. It was the principle of the thing. What made it worse was that for some time I have had the use of one of his books myself, his idea, and of course most kind. But it doesn’t alter the principle. In the train I saw the whole unfairness of the life of employees. However hard they work, their lives don’t alter or get any easier. They live cheap poor lives in anxiety all their best years and then are expected to be grateful for a pension, and generally get no pension. I’ve left off living in anxiety; perhaps because I’ve forgotten how to have an imagination. But that is the principle and I came to the conclusion that no employers, however generous and nice, are entitled to the slightest special consideration. And I came back and practically said so. I told him that in future I would have nothing to do with his Mudie books. It was outside my sphere. I also said all sorts of things that came into my head in the train, a whole long speech. About unfairness. And to prove my point to him individually I told him of things that were unfair to me and their other employees in the practice; about the awfulness of having to be there first thing in the morning from the country after a weekend. *They* don’t. They sail off to their expensive weekends without even saying goodbye, and without even thinking whether we can manage to have any sort of recreation at all on our salaries. I said that, and also that I objected to spend a large part of a busy Monday morning arranging the huge bunches of flowers he brought back from the country. That was not true. I loved those flowers and could always have some for my room; but it *was* a frightful nuisance sometimes, and it came into the principle, and I wound up by saying that in future I would do only the work for the practice and no odd jobs of any kind.”

===Chinese===

「不管怎样。最糟的是，我的雇主们为人好得吓人。可原则是同一个，那可怕的不公。碰巧在我动身之前，正当汉考克先生要启程度假，他为一本穆迪（Mudie）租书处的书还没读就被收了回去、单子上旁的书又一封不来而着恼，忽然用咕哝的口气对我说：『你可以替我照看着点儿我的穆迪租书处的书。』我简直气坏了。因为在我着手照看这些书以前——他从来没有托付过我，全然是我自己的主意——那简直是笔糊涂账。他们几个各人也照样开单子，至少碰上一本心想会喜欢的就记下来，然后把整张单子*送进去*，从不留个副本，自然把记过的忘个精光。于是我私下动手誊抄那些单子，书一到就逐本划掉，再把余下的单子一而再、再而三地送进去，直到*全部*到齐。我知道，一个明白人是不会动这样火的，只会乖乖地跑得更勤、看得更紧。可我受不了不公。这是原则问题。更糟的是，有些日子我自己一直借着读他的一本书，那是他的主意，自然好意得很。可这改变不了原则。在火车上，我看清了受雇人生全部的不公。无论他们怎样苦干，他们的生活不会改变，也不会轻松半分。他们最好的年华在忧惧中过着廉价贫寒的日子，到头来还指望他们对一笔养老金感激涕零，而通常连养老金也没有。我已经不过忧惧的日子了；也许因为我忘了怎样去拥有想象力。可原则就在这儿，于是我得出结论：天下的雇主，无论多么慷慨多么和气，都不配得到一星半点的特殊体谅。于是我回来了，也差不多就这么说了。我告诉他，往后他的穆迪租书处的书我一概不沾。那在我的职分之外。我还把火车上涌进头脑的种种一气说了，一整篇长长的陈词。关于不公。为了向他本人证明我的论点，我对他讲了诊所里加在我和其他雇员身上的种种不公；讲周末在乡间过罢、星期一一早就得从乡下赶到诊所头一个到班的可怕。*他们*用不着。他们扬帆驶向他们花费不赀的周末，连再见也不道一声，甚至连想也不想，凭我们的薪水，我们究竟腾不腾得出任何一种消遣。我说了这些，还说我反对把忙乱的星期一上午的一大段，花在安插他从乡间带回的一抱抱大束的鲜花上。那不是真话。我爱那些花，自己房里也总插得上几枝；可这有时*确实*是可怕的烦累，也归得进原则里去，末了我宣布，往后我只做诊所分内的事，任何零活杂差一概不承。」

===Original===

“What was his reply?”

“Oh well, I’ve got the sack.”

“Are you serious,” he said in a low frightened tone. The heavens were clear, ringing with morning joy; from far away in the undisturbed future she looked back smiling upon the episode that lay before her growing and pressing.

===Chinese===

「他怎么答复？」

「哦，这个么，我被解雇了。」

「你是当真的么，」他低声说，语调里带着惊惶。天宇澄澈，鸣响着清晨的欢悦；从远远的、未受惊扰的未来，她含笑回望着那桩横在眼前、正生长着压迫过来的事端。

===Original===

“I’m not serious. But they are. This is a solemn, awfully nice little note from Mr. Orly; he had to write, because he’s the senior partner, to inform me that he has come to the conclusion that I must seek a more congenial post. They have absolutely made up their minds. Because they know quite well I have no training for any other work, and no resources, and they would not have done this unless they were absolutely obliged.”

“Then you will be obliged to leave these gentlemen?”

===Chinese===

「我不是当真。他们是。这是奥利先生一封郑重其事、和气得不得了的小简；他是资深合伙人，不得不写，来通知我说，他已得出结论，我必须另觅一个更相宜的职位。他们是铁了心的。因为他们十分清楚，我没有受过任何别种工作的训练，也没有任何依靠，若非万不得已，他们绝不会出此一着。」

「那么，你将不得不离开这几位先生了？」

===Original===

“Of course long before I had finished talking I was thinking about all sorts of other things; and seeing all kinds of points of view that seemed to be stated all round us by people who were looking on. I always do when I talk to Mr. Hancock. His point of view is so clear-cut and so reasonable that it reveals all the things that hold social life together, and brings the ghosts of people who have believed and suffered for these things into the room, but also all kinds of other points of view. … But I’m not going to leave. I can’t. What else could I do? Perhaps I will a little later on, when this is all over. But I’m not going to be dismissed in solemn dignity. It’s too silly. That shows you how nice they are. I know that really I must leave. Anyone would say so. But that’s the extraordinary thing; I don’t believe in those things; solemn endings; being led by the nose by the necessities of the situation. That may be undignified. But dignity is silly; the back view. Already I can’t believe all this solemnity has happened. It’s simply a most fearful bother. They’ve managed it splendidly, waiting till Saturday morning, so that I shan’t see any of them again. The Orlys will be gone away for a month when I get there today and Mr. Hancock is away for the weekend and I am offered a month’s salary in lieu of notice, if I prefer it. I had forgotten all this machinery. They’re perfectly in the right, but I’d forgotten the machinery. … I knew yesterday. They were all three shut up together in the den, talking in low tones, and presently came busily out, each so anxious to pass the dismissed secretary in hurried preoccupation, that they collided in the doorway, and gave everything away to me by the affable excited way they apologised to each other. If I had turned and faced them then I should have said worse things than I had said to Mr. Hancock. I *hated* them, with their resources and their serenity, complacently pleased with each other because they had decided to smash an employee who had spoken out to them.”

===Chinese===

「当然，话还没说完，我早已在想种种别的事情了；看见各式各样的观点，仿佛正由旁观的人们围在我们四周一一陈述出来。我跟汉考克先生说话时总是这样。他的观点那样分明、那样在理，以致把维系社会生活的一切都照了出来，把那些曾为这些东西信过、苦过的人们的魂灵请进屋来，可也照出各式各样别的观点。……但我不打算走。我不能。我还能去做什么呢？也许稍过些时，等这一切都过去了，我会走的。可我不要这样一本正经、道貌岸然地被辞退。太可笑了。这正见出他们有多好。我知道，实际上我必须走。谁都会这么说。可这正是那不可思议之处；我不信那一套；庄严的收场；被时势的必然牵着鼻子走。那也许有失体面。可体面是蠢东西；不过是从背后看去的光景。此刻我已经不能相信，这全部的庄严当真发生过。这不过是一桩天大的麻烦。他们安排得漂亮极了，专等星期六早上动手，叫我从此再见不着他们任何一位。等我今天到那儿，奥利夫妇已动身外出一月，汉考克先生也去度他的周末，还提给我一个月薪水以代通知期——倘若我宁可这样。这一套程序我全忘了。他们完全在理，可我把这程序忘了。……我昨天就知道了。他们三位一道关在那间里屋，低声计议，随即匆匆忙忙一齐出来，一个个急煎煎、心事满腹，只想着赶快从被辞退的秘书身边挤过去，竟在门口撞作一团，又那样一团和气、兴奋仓皇地互相道歉，倒把什么都泄露给了我。我当时若转身面对他们，说出口的话会比先前对汉考克先生说的更难听。我*恨*他们，恨他们的财源与安闲，恨他们因为议定碾碎一个敢对他们直言的雇员而彼此得意洋洋。」

===Original===

“This was indeed a scene of remarkable significance.”

“I don’t know. … I once told Mr. Hancock that I would give notice every year, because I think it must be so horrible to dismiss anybody. But I’m not going to be sent away by machinery. In a way it is like a family suddenly going to law.”

===Chinese===

「这确乎是一幕意味深长的场景。」

「我不知道。……有一次我跟汉考克先生说过，我情愿年年由我提出辞呈，因为我想，辞退任何人必定可怕得很。可我不要被一套机器打发走。在某种意义上，这就像一家人忽然彼此打起官司来。」

===Original===

But with the passing of the park and the coming of the tall houses on either side of the road, the open June morning was quenched. It retreated to balconies, flower-filled by shocked condemning people, prosperously turned away towards the world from which she was banished. Wimpole Street, Harley Street, Cavendish Square. The names sounded in her ears the appeal they had made when she was helplessly looking for work. It was as if she were still waiting to come. …

===Chinese===

可是随着公园退去、大路两侧高高的房屋逼来，开阔的六月清晨熄灭了。它退避到阳台上去了，那些阳台满陈着花木，花木的主人惊愕而谴责，富泰地把脸扭向她被放逐出来的那个世界。温波尔街，哈利街（Harley Street），卡文迪什广场（Cavendish Square）。这些名字在她耳边重新响起，正是当年她走投无路寻职时它们发出的吁请。仿佛她仍旧在等候着进来。……

===Original===

Within the Saturday morning peace of the deserted house lingered the relief that had followed their definite decision. They were all drawn together to begin again, renewed, freshly conscious of the stabilities of the practice; their enclosed cooperating relationship. …

===Chinese===

空宅星期六清晨的安宁里，萦回着他们定下明确决断之后随之而来的宽解。他们重新聚拢，准备从头再来，焕然一新，清清新新地意识到诊所的种种稳固；他们那闭锁而协作的关系。……

===Original===

She concentrated her mental gaze on their grouped personalities, sharing their long consultations, acting out in her mind with characteristic gesture and speech, the part each one had taken, confronting them one by one, in solitude, with a different version, holding on, breaking into their commonsense finalities. … It was all nothing; meaningless … like things in history that led on to events that did not belong to them because nobody went below the surface of the way things appear to be joined together but are not … but the words belonging to the underlying things were far away, only to be found in long silences, and sounding when they came out into conversations, irrelevant, often illogical and self-contradictory, impossible to prove, driving absurdly across life towards things that seemed impossible, but were true … there were two layers of truth. The truths laid bare by common sense in swift decisive conversations, founded on apparent facts, were incomplete. They shaped the surface, made things go kaleidoscoping on, recognisable, in a sort of general busy prosperous agreement; but at every turn, with every application of the commonsense civilised decisions, enormous things were left behind, unsuspected, forced underground, but never dying, slow things with slow slow fruit … the surface shape was powerful, everyone was in it, that was where free will broke down, in the moving on and being spirited away for another spell from the underlying things, but in everyone, alone, often unconsciously, was something, a real inside personality that was turned away from the surface. In front of everyone, away from the bridges and catchwords, was an invisible plank, that would bear … always … forgotten … nearly all smiles were smiled from the bridges … nearly all deaths were murders or suicides. …

===Chinese===

她把内心的注视聚在他们成群的品性上，分享他们漫长的合议，在心里用各人特有的手势与言辞，演出每人充当的角色，在孤独中一个一个面对他们，端出另一个版本来，死死撑住，闯进他们常识的种种定论里去。……这一切全不算什么；毫无意义……像历史上的那些事物，引出并不属于它们的后续事件，因为没有人肯沉到表面之下去，看一看事物那貌似相连、实则不连的相连之法……但属于底下那些事物的言词远在他方，唯有在长久的沉默里方能寻得，一旦流出到谈话之中，听着便离题，常常不合逻辑、自相矛盾，无从证明，荒唐地横冲直撞地掠过生活，扑向那些看似不可能、却真实存在的事物……真理有两层。常识在快捷决绝的谈话里剥露出来的那些真理，奠基于表面的事实，是不完全的。它们塑造表面，使事物万花筒般翻演下去，可以辨认，处在一种普遍忙碌而兴旺的一致之中；可每转一个弯，每运用一回常识的文明的裁断，便有庞然巨物被留在身后，无人疑心，被逼入地下，却永不死去，迟缓的事物结着迟缓迟缓的果实……表面的形制强大有力，人人都嵌在里头，自由意志正是在那里败退——在那随波推进、又被神差鬼使带离底下事物又一阵子的进程里；可在每个人身上，独自一人时，常常并不自知，都存着一点什么，一个真实的内在人格，背转身去，不对表面。在每个人面前，离开那些桥梁与口头禅，有一块看不见的木板，它承受得住……永远……被遗忘……几乎所有的微笑都是从桥上绽出来的……几乎所有的死亡，不是他杀，便是自杀。……

===Original===

It would be such an awful labour … in the long interval the strength for it would disappear. Thoughts must be kept away. Activities. The weekend would be a vacuum of tense determination. That was the payment for headlong speech. Speech, thought-out speech, does nothing but destroy. There had been a moment of hesitation in the train, swamped by the illumination coming from the essay. …

===Chinese===

那会是何等可怕的劳作……在那长长的间歇里，做它的气力会消失殆尽。念头必须挡在外面。活动。周末将是一段紧张决心的真空。那便是信口直陈的代价。言语，想定了的言语，只会破坏。火车上有过一刻犹豫，被那篇随笔投来的光照淹没了。……

===Original===

The morning’s letters lay unopened on her table. Dreadful. Dealing with them would bring unconsciousness, acceptance of the situation would leap upon her unawares. She gathered them up conversationally, summoning presences and the usual atmosphere of the working day, but was disarmed by the trembling of her hands. The letters were the last link. Merely touching them had opened the door to a withering pain. When the appointments were kept, she would no longer be in the house. The patients crowded through her mind; individuals, groups, families, the whole fabric of social life richly unrolled day by day, for her contemplation; spirited away. Each letter brought the sting of careless indifferent farewell.

===Chinese===

早晨的信件原封未拆地躺在她桌上。可怕。处理它们会带来无意识，对处境的接受会趁她不备一跃而上。她带着应酬似的神气把它们拢起，召来种种人影与工作日惯常的气氛，却被自己双手的颤抖解除了武装。信件是最后一环。仅仅一碰，便推开了一扇通向枯萎之痛的门。等这些预约一一了结，她便再不在这宅子里了。病人们从她头脑里挤过；个人，群体，家庭，社会生活的整幅织锦日复一日丰饶地铺展，供她静观；被神差鬼使地卷走了。每一封信都带来那漫不经心、漠然作别的蜇痛。

===Original===

At the hall door James was whistling for a hansom; it was a dream picture, part of the week that was past. A hansom drew up, the abruptly reined-in horse slipping and scrabbling. Perhaps there *was* a patient hidden in Mr. Leyton’s quiet sounding surgery. Once more she could watch a patient’s departure; the bright oblong of street … he was away for the weekend. There was no patient. It was a dream picture. Dream figures were coming downstairs. … Mrs. Orly, Mr. Orly, not yet gone; coming hurriedly straight towards her. She rose without thought, calmly unoccupied, watching them come, one person, swiftly and gently. They stood about her, quite near; silently radiating their kindliness.

===Chinese===

门厅前，詹姆斯（James）正打着唿哨唤一辆双轮马车（hansom）；那是一幅梦中的图画，属于已然过去的那一周。一辆双轮马车靠边停住，猛地受缰的马打滑刨蹄。说不定莱顿先生那听去静悄悄的诊室里*当真*藏着一位病人。她又能目送一位病人的离去；街面上那一方明亮的矩形……他度周末去了。没有病人。那是一幅梦中的图画。梦中的人影正下楼来。……奥利太太，奥利先生，还没有动身；匆匆地径直朝她走来。她不假思索地站起身，安闲地无所事事，望着他们合成一个人似的走来，又轻快又温柔。他们立在她近旁，站得很近；默默辐射着他们的和善。

===Original===

“I suppose we must say goodbye,” said Mrs. Orly. In her sweet little sallow face not a shadow of reproach; but lively bright sorrow, *tears* in her eyes.

“I say, we’re awfully sorry about this,” said Mr. Orly gustily, shifting his poised bulk from one foot to the other.

“So am I,” said Miriam seeking for the things they were inviting her to say. She could only smile at them.

===Chinese===

「我想我们总得道个别了，」奥利太太说。她那张甜甜的小小的灰黄脸上，没有一丝责怪的影子；只有活泼明亮的哀愁，眼里含着*泪*。

「我说，这档子事我们真是过意不去，」奥利先生的话一阵一阵迸着出来，把他那端凝庞大的身躯从一只脚倒到另一只脚上。

「我也一样，」米丽亚姆说，一面搜寻着他们正引她出口的那些话。她只能对他们微笑。

===Original===

“It *is* a pity,” whispered Mrs. Orly. *This* was the Orlys; the reality of them; an English reality; utterly unbusinesslike; with no codes but themselves; showing themselves; without disguises of voice or manner, to a dismissed employee; the quality of England; old-fashioned.

===Chinese===

「这*确实*可惜，」奥利太太悄声说。*这*才是奥利夫妇；他们的本相；一种英国的本相；全然不合生意规矩；除自身之外别无法典；向一个被辞退的雇员袒露自身；声音与举止不设半点伪装；英格兰的质地；老派的。

===Original===

“I know.” They both spoke together and then Mrs. Orly was saying “No, Ro can’t bear strangers.”

“If *you* don’t want me to go I shall stay,” she murmured. But the sense of being already half reinstated was driven away by Mrs. Orly’s unaltered distress.

“Ungrateful?” The gustily panting tones were the remainder of the real anger he had felt, listening to Mr. Hancock’s discourse. They had no grievance and they had misunderstood his.

===Chinese===

「我知道。」两人异口同声，随后奥利太太又说道：「不成，罗（Ro）见不得生人。」

「倘若*你们*不要我走，我就留下，」她低声说。可那种已被恢复了一半职位似的感觉，被奥利太太分毫未减的愁苦赶散了。

「忘恩负义？」那阵阵喘动的语调，是他听汉考克先生那番陈述时动了真怒的余烬。他们本没有委屈，却错会了他的委屈。

===Original===

“No,” she said coldly, “I don’t think so.”

“Hang it all, excuse my language, but y’know he’s done a good deal for ye.” “All expectation of gratitude is meanness and is continually punished by the total insensibility of the obliged person.” … “we are lucky; we ought to be grateful;” meaning, to God. Then unlucky people ought to be ungrateful. …

===Chinese===

「不，」她冷冷地说，「我不这么想。」

「该死，恕我出言无状，可你晓得，他为你出过不少的力哪。」「一切对感激的期许都是刻薄，并且不断受着受惠人全然麻木的惩罚。」……「我们有福气；我们应当感恩；」意思是，向上帝感恩。那么，倒霉的人就理应忘恩。……

===Original===

“Besides,” the same gusty tone “it’s as good as telling us we’re not gentlemen; y’see?” The blue eyes flashed furiously.

Then all her generalisations had been taken *personally*. … “Oh well,” she said helplessly.

“We shall be late, laddie.”

“Surely that can be put right. I must talk to Mr. Hancock.”

===Chinese===

「再说，」还是那股一阵一阵的火气，「这就等于说我们不是绅士；你明白么？」蓝眼睛狂怒地闪着光。

原来她那些一概而论，全被*记到了个人头上*。……「唉，好吧，」她无可奈何地说。

「我们要迟到了，小家伙。」

「那总还有挽回的余地吧。我得跟汉考克先生谈一谈。」

===Original===

“Well, to tell y’honestly I don’t think y’ll be able to do anything with Hancock.” Mrs. Orly’s distressed little face supported his opinion, and her surprising sudden little embrace and Mr. Orly’s wringing handshake meant not only the enduring depths of their kindliness but their pained dismay in seeing her desolate and resourceless, their certainty that there was no hope. It threw a strong light. It would be difficult for him to withdraw; perhaps impossible; perhaps he had already engaged another secretary. … But she found that she had not watched them go away and was dealing steadily with the letters, with a blank mind upon which presently emerged the features of the coming weekend.

===Chinese===

「唉，跟你说句老实话，我看你在汉考克身上是使不出什么劲来的。」奥利太太那张愁苦的小脸附和着他的见解，而她那出人意料、突然的一抱，奥利先生那紧拧的一握，不只意味着他们和善的深沉经久，也意味着他们眼见她孤零无靠时痛楚的惶惑，意味着他们断定再无指望。这投下一道强光。要他收回成命会很难；也许全然不能；也许他早已聘定了另一位秘书。……可她发觉，自己并没有目送他们离去，手底下正一封封安稳地料理着信件，头脑一片空白，不多时，行将到来的那个周末的眉目，便在空白上浮现出来。

---

===Original===

“Well as I *say*—” Miriam followed the lingering held-in cold vexation of the voice, privately prompting it with informal phrases fitting the picture she held, half-smiling, in her mind, of a moody, uncertain, door-slamming secretary, using the whole practice as material for personal musings, liable suddenly to break into long speeches of accusation. But if they were spoken, they would destroy the thing that was being given back to her, the thing that had made the atmosphere of the room. “It will be the most unbusinesslike thing I’ve ever done; and I doubt very much whether it will answer.”

===Chinese===

「这个么，正像我*所说*的——」米丽亚姆跟随着那嗓音里迟迟不散、强自按捺的冷冷愠怒，私下里用随口的字句给它提词，那些字句合着她心中半含笑意擎着的一幅图景：一个喜怒无常、拿不定主意、动辄摔门的秘书，把整间诊所用作个人遐思的材料，随时会突然迸出长篇的控诉。可这些话若当真说出口，便会毁掉那件正被交还给她的东西，那件曾经造出这间屋子气氛的东西。「这将是我此生做过的最不合生意规矩的事；而且我很怀疑它行不行得通。」

===Original===

“Oh well. There’s not any reason why it shouldn’t.” She smiled provisionally. It was not yet quite time to rise and feel life flowing about her in the familiar room, purged to a fresh austerity by the coming and passing of the storm. There was still a rankling, and glorious as it was to sit talking at leisure, the passing of time piled up the sense of ultimate things missing their opportunity of getting said. She could not, with half her mind set towards the terms, promising a laborious future, of her resolution that he should never regret his unorthodoxy, find her way to them. And the moments as they passed gleamed too brightly with confirmation of the strange blind faith she had brought as sole preparation for the encounter, hovered with too quiet a benediction to be seized and used deliberately, without the pressure of the sudden inspiration for which they seemed to wait.

===Chinese===

「哦，这又有什么行不通的道理。」她姑且一笑。还不到起身的时候，还不到在这间熟悉的屋子里、感到生活重新在周身流转的时候——这屋子经那场风暴的来去，涤净出一种新的简素。这里仍有一处隐痛，而闲坐对谈固然荣耀，时间的流逝却渐渐堆起一种感觉：种种终极的东西，正错过被说出的机会。她半个心思系在那条件上——她立意决不叫他为他的不循常轨而后悔，那条件许下了一个辛苦的未来——在这情形下，她寻不出通往它们的路径。而逝去的时刻闪着过分明亮的光，证实着她为这场会面携来的唯一准备——那奇异而盲目的信念——又萦回着过分安静的祝福，教人无从蓄意地去抓住它们、使用它们，除非有那突然灵感的催迫，而它们仿佛正在等它。

===Original===

“Well, as I say, that depends entirely on yourself. You must clearly understand that I expect you to fulfil all reasonable requests whether referring to the practice or no, and moreover to fulfil them *cheerfully*.”

===Chinese===

「这个么，正像我说的，那全然取决于你自己。你必须明白懂得，我期望你履行一切合理的要求，无论关乎诊所与否，而且要*高高兴兴*地履行。」

===Original===

“Well, of course I have no choice. But I can’t promise to be *cheerful*; that’s impossible.” An obstinate tightening of the grave face.

“I think perhaps I might manage to be *serene*; generally. I can’t pretend to be cheerful.” “Assume an air of cheerfulness, and presently you will be cheerful, in spite of yourself.” Awful. To live like that would be to miss suddenly finding the hidden something that would make you cheerful forever.

===Chinese===

「这个么，我当然无可选择。可我不能保证做到*高兴*；那不可能。」那张严肃的脸执拗地绷紧了一瞬。

「我想，我或许办得到*宁静*；大致上。我装不出高兴来。」「且摆出一副高兴的神气，不多时你便会当真高兴起来，由不得你。」可怕。那样活着，便会错过忽然寻见那件藏着的东西——那件能叫你从此永远高兴的东西。

===Original===

“Well as I say.”

===Chinese===

「这个么，正像我说的。」

===Original===

“You see there’s always the awful question of right and wrong mixed up with everything; all sorts of rights and wrongs, in the simplest things. I can’t think how people can go on so calmly. It sometimes seems to me as if everyone ought to stop and do quite other things. It’s a nightmare, the way things go on. I want to stay here, and yet I often wonder whether I ought; whether I ought to go on doing this kind of work.”

===Chinese===

「你瞧，对与错这可怕的问题，永远搅和在一切事情里；各式各样的对错，就在顶简单的事情里。我想不通，人们怎么能这样心平气和地过下去。有时在我看来，仿佛人人都该停下手里的事，去做截然不同的营生。事情这样一路下去，是一场噩梦。我想留在这里，可我又常常怀疑，我究竟该不该；究竟该不该继续做这一类工作。」

===Original===

“Well as I say, I know quite well the work here leaves many of your capabilities unoccupied.”

“It’s not that. I mean everything in general.”

===Chinese===

「这个么，正像我说的，我很清楚，这里的工作让你许多的才具闲置未用。」

「不是那个。我是笼统地指一切。」

===Original===

“Well—if it is a question of right and wrong, I suppose the life here like any other, offers opportunities for the exercise of the Christian virtues.”

===Chinese===

「这个么——倘若那当真是对与错的问题，那么我想，这里的生活同任何别处的一样，都提供了践行基督教诸美德的机会。」

===Original===

Resignation; virtues deliberately set forth every day like the wares in a little shop; and the world going on outside just the same. A sort of sale of mean little virtues for respectability and a living; the living coming by amiable cooperation with a world where everything was wrong, turning the little virtues into absurdity; respectable absurdity. He did not think the practice of the Christian virtues in a vacuum was enough. But he had made a joke, and smiled his smile. … There was no answer anywhere in the world to the question he had raised. Did he remember saying why shouldn’t you take up dentistry? Soon it would be too late to make any change; there was nothing to do now but to stay and justify things … it would be impossible to be running about in a surgery with grey hair; it would make the practice seem dowdy. All dental secretaries were young. … The work … nothing but the life all round it; the existence of a shadow amidst shadows unaware of their shadowiness, keeping going a world where there were things, more than people. The people moved sunlit and prosperous, but not enviable, their secrets revealed at every turn, unaware themselves, they made and left a space in which to be aware. …

===Chinese===

顺从；种种美德每天像小铺子里的货色一般，郑重其事地陈列出来；外面的世界照旧运转。一种拿卑微的小美德换取体面与生计的交易；生计靠的是与一个事事皆错的世界和气合作，而这合作又把小美德变成荒唐；体面的荒唐。他本不以为，在真空里践行基督教美德便已足够。可他方才说了句笑话，露出了他那种微笑。……他提起的那个问题，世上没有任何地方有答案。他还记得自己说过「你何不也从事牙医」么？不久便要来不及改行；如今别无他路，只有留下来，替一切辩白……顶着一头灰发在诊室里跑进跑出是不成的；那会叫诊所显得寒酸。牙医诊所的秘书个个年轻。……这工作……不过是被围绕在它四围的生活；一个影子置身众影之间、而众影不自知其为影的存在，维持着一个「物」重于「人」的世界的运转。人们行动在阳光里，兴旺昌隆，却并不足羡，他们的秘密处处显露而不自知，他们造出又留下一个空间，好教人有所知觉。……

===Original===

“I want to say that I think it is kind of you to let me air my grievances so thoroughly.”

“Well, as I *say*, I feel extremely uncertain as to the advisability of this step.”

===Chinese===

「我想说，你容我把一肚子的委屈这样淋漓尽致地吐了个痛快，实在是你的好意。」

「这个么，正像我*所说*，对这一步是否得宜，我感到极其没有把握。」

===Original===

“You needn’t,” she said rising as he rose, and going buoyantly to move about in the neighbourhood of the scattered results of his last operation, the symbols of her narrowly rescued continuity. She was not yet free to touch them. He was still, wandering about the other part of the room, lingering with thoughtful bent head in the mazes of her outrageous halting statements. But a good deal of his resentment had gone. It was something outside herself, something in the world at large, that had forced him to act against his “better judgment.” He was still angry and feeling a little shorn, faced, in the very presence of the offender, with the necessity of disposing of the fact that he had been driven into inconsistency.

===Chinese===

「你用不着，」她说着，随他一同起身，轻快地走动到他上一场手术散落的成果近旁去——那是她险险挽回的连续性的表征。她还不能自由去触碰它们。他还在屋子另一头徘徊，垂着头若有所思，流连在她那些放肆而期期艾艾的声明的迷宫里。可他的怨愤已消去了大半。是某种外在于她的东西、某种广布于世的东西，迫使他行事违背了自己的「明断」。他仍有愠意，自觉威仪稍折，当着冒犯者的面，还须处置一个事实：他已被逼得自相矛盾了。

===Original===

Miriam drew a deep sigh, clearing her personal air of the burden of conflict. Was it an affront? It had sounded to her like a song. His thoughts must be saying, well, there you are, it’s all very well to throw it all off like that. His pose stiffened into a suggested animation with regard to work delayed. If only now there could be an opportunity for one of his humorous remarks so that she could laugh herself back into their indestructible impersonal relationship. It was, she thought, prophetically watching his gloriously inevitable recovery, partly his unconscious resentment of the blow she had struck at their good understanding that had made him so repeatedly declare that if they started again it must be on a new footing; that all possibility of spontaneity between them had been destroyed.

===Chinese===

米丽亚姆深深叹出一口气，把纷争的重负从她个人的气氛里清除出去。那算是种冒犯么？在她听来，它像一支歌。他心里一定在说：喏，你瞧，像那样把一切一掸而尽，倒也轻巧。他的姿态绷紧起来，化作一种对于延宕了的工作的跃跃然。此刻若能有个机会，让他说上一句俏皮话，她便可借着笑一笑，回到他们那段摧折不了的非个人的关系里去。她一面如预言般注视着他那光彩而必然的复原，一面想：一部分正是他对她击在他们良好谅解上的那一记所怀的无意识的怨愤，才使他再三申明：倘若他们重新开始，必须立于新的基础之上；他们之间一切自发的可能，都已毁尽了。

===Original===

How could it be, with the events of daily life perpetually building it afresh?

===Chinese===

那怎么可能呢？日常生活的种种事件，不是时时刻刻都在把它重新筑起来么？

## IX / IX

===Original===

The power of London to obliterate personal affairs depended upon unlimited freedom to be still. The worst suffering in the days of uncertainty had been the thought of movements that would make time move.﻿   … Now that the stillness had returned, life was going on, dancing, flowing, looping out in all directions able to bear its periods of torment in the strength of its certainty of recovery, so long as time stayed still. Life *ceased* when time moved on. Out in the world life was ceasing all the time. All the time people were helplessly doing things that made time move; growing up, old people growing onwards, with death suddenly in sight, rushing here and there with words that had lost their meaning, dodging and crouching no matter how ridiculously, to avoid facing it. Young men died in advance; it was visible in their faces, when they took degrees and sat down to tasks that made time begin to move; never again free from its movement, always listening and looking for the stillness they had lost.﻿   … But why is the world which produces them so fresh and real and free, and then seizes and makes them dead old leaves whirled along by time, so different to people alone in themselves when time is not moving? People in themselves want nothing but reality. Why can’t reality exist in the world? All the things that happen produce friction because they distract people from the reality they are unconsciously looking for. That is why there are everywhere torrents of speech. If she had not read all those old words in the train and had been silent. Silence is reality. Life ought to be lived on a basis of silence, where truth blossoms. Why isn’t such an urgent thing known? Life would become like the individual; alive﻿   … it would show, inside and out, and people would leave off talking so much. Life does show, seen from far off, pouring down into stillness. But the contemplation of it, not caring for pain or suffering except as part of a picture, which no one who is in the picture can see, seems mean. Old women sitting in corners, suddenly making irrelevant remarks and chuckling, see; they make a stillness of reality, a mind picture that does not care, out of the rush of life. Perhaps they do not fear death. Perhaps people who don’t take part don’t fear death﻿   … the outsider sees most of the game; but that means a cynical man who does not care for anything; body and mind without soul. Lying dead at last, with reality left unnoticed on his dressing-table, along the window sill, along the edge of things outside the window.﻿   …

===Chinese===

伦敦抹去私人心事的力量，全系于不受拘限的静止自由。在那些悬而未决的日子里，最重的苦楚，便是想到会有种种动静，叫时间动起来。……如今静止回来了，生活继续着，舞蹈着，流淌着，向四面八方绕出环弧，凭着它对复原的确信之力，经得住一阵阵的折磨——只要时间停驻不动。时间一旦前行，生活便*止息*了。外面的世界上，生活无时无刻不在止息。人们无时无刻不无可奈何地做着叫时间动起来的事；长大，成人，老人们继续老下去，死亡蓦然在望，于是带着失了意义的字句东奔西突，闪躲蜷伏——无论那姿势多么可笑——只求不去面对它。年轻人预先死掉了；这看得见，在他们取得学位、坐下来做起叫时间开始移动的营生时，便显在脸上；从此再脱不开它的移动，永远谛听着、寻望着他们失掉的静止。……可是，为什么生出他们的这世界这般新鲜、真实而自由，随即却攫住他们，把他们变作被时间卷着打旋的枯死老叶，与时间静止时、独自守在自己里面的人们判然两样？人们在自己里面，要的唯有真实。为什么真实不能存在于世上？种种发生的事之所以生出摩擦，是因为它们把人从他不知不觉寻望着的真实那里岔开。所以到处是滔滔的言语。倘若她在火车上不曾读那些陈旧的字句，而是沉默着。沉默即是真实。生活应当立在沉默的根基上过活，真理便在那里开花。这样一件紧迫的事，为什么无人知晓？生活会变得像个体一样；活的……它会里里外外显露出来，人们也会省去这许多的话。从远处望去，生活确乎显露着，倾泻而下，注入静止。可这样静观它——除却当作一幅画的一部分，便不把疼痛苦难放在心上，而这幅画，身在画中的人谁也看不见——未免卑薄。坐在角落里的老妇人，忽然说句不着边际的话，咯咯地笑，她们看得见；她们从生活的奔涌里，造出一种真实的静止，一幅满不在乎的心中之画。也许她们不怕死。也许不参与其间的人们不怕死……旁观者清；可那说的是一个什么都不挂心的冷嘲者；有身有心，却没有灵魂。到头来直挺挺躺着死了，真实被撇在梳妆台上无人理会，沿着窗台，沿着窗外万物的边沿。……

===Original===

But one day in the future time would move, by itself, not through anything one did, and there would be no more life.﻿   … She looked up hurriedly towards the changing voice. He was no longer reading with a face that showed his thoughts wandering far away.

“The thought of death is, throughout life, entirely absent from the mind of the healthy man.” His brilliant thought filled eyes shone towards her at the end of the sentence.

“There is *indeed* a vulgarity in perfect health,” he exclaimed.

===Chinese===

但是将来总有一天，时间会自己动起来，不因任何人做的任何事，而生活便再没有了。……她急忙抬眼，朝那变换了的嗓音望去。他不再诵读了，方才脸上那副思绪飘向远处的神气也不见了。

「对死亡的念头，在人的一生中，全然不在健康人的心里。」一句既终，他那双神采焕发、盛满思绪的眼睛向她闪着光。

「完美的健康里*确实*有一种俗气，」他喊道。

===Original===

“Yes,” she said hurriedly, carrying off the statement for examination, as peacefully he went on reading. What did vulgarity mean, or perfect health? Nobody knew. Dante ennobled the vulgar tongue.﻿   … People went on forever writing books using the same words with different meanings. Her eyes returned to the relaxed unconscious form. He thought too much of books. Yet it did not appal him to think of giving up his free intellectual life and taking to work. “I shall still be an interested amateur.”﻿   … He would go on reading, all his life, sitting as he was sitting now, grave and beautiful; with a mind outspread in a mental experience so wide that he was indifferent to the usual ideas of freedom and advantage. Yet he did not seem to be aware how much the sitting like this, linked to the world by its deep echo in the book, was a realisation of life as he saw it. It did not occur to him that this serenity, in which were accumulated all the hours they had passed together, *was* realisation, the life of the world in miniature, making a space where everything in human experience could emerge like a reflection in deep water, with its proportions held true and right by the tranquil opposition of their separate minds. She summoned onlookers, who instantly recognised themselves in this picture of leisure. It was in every life that was not astray in ceaseless movement. It was the place where everything was atoned. He fitted placed thus, happy, without problems or envies, in possession of himself and his memories in the room where he had voiced them, into the centre of English life where all turned to good, in the last fastness of the private English mind where condemnation could not live. He reinforced it with a consciousness that was not in the English, making it show as an idea, revealing in plain terms their failure to act it out.﻿   … Thus would his leisure always be. But it was no part of her life. In this tranquillity there was no security﻿   … we will always sit like this; we must, she said within herself impatiently towards his unconsciousness. Why did he not *perceive* the life there was, the mode of life, in this sitting tranquilly together? Was he thinking of nothing but his reading? She listened for a moment half carried into the quality of the text. There was reality there, Spinoza, by himself, sounding as if the words were being traced out now, for the first time. One day in a moment of blankness, she would read it and agree and disagree and carry away some idea and lose and recover it and go on, losing and recovering, agreeing and disagreeing.﻿   …

===Chinese===

「是的，」她急忙说，把这句话接过去留待细究，他则安安静静地继续诵读。俗气是什么意思，完美的健康又是什么？没有人知道。但丁（Dante）令俗语变得高贵。……人们永远在写着一部部的书，用同样的字眼，装着不同的意思。她的目光回到那放松的、浑然不觉的身影上。他把书看得太重了。然而一想到要放弃他自由的思想生活、去就一份工作，他并不惊骇。「我仍将是个兴致不减的业余爱好者。」……他会一直读下去，读一辈子，就像他此刻这样坐着，庄重而俊美；一颗心智铺展在如此宽广的精神阅历里，以至于对寻常的自由与利害的观念无动于衷。然而他似乎并不觉得，像这样坐着——借着书里深沉的回响与世界相连——在多大程度上正是照他的眼光看去、生活的实现。他想不到，这份安宁——其中积攒着他们一同度过的全部时辰——*本就是*实现，是世上生活的一个缩影，辟出一方空间，人类阅历中的一切都能在其中浮现，宛如映在深水里的倒影，各部分的比例，由他们两颗心智静穆的对峙把持得端正妥帖。她召来一众旁观者，他们立时在这幅闲居的图画里认出了自己。它存在于每一种不曾迷失于无休无止的奔动里的生活。它是一切皆得补赎的地方。他这样安放着——快活的，没有难题，没有艳羡，守着他自己和他的记忆，在这间他曾倾吐它们的屋子里——正嵌进英国生活的中心，那里一切都转向善，是英国人私密心智最后的堡垒，谴责在那里无法存活。他以一种英国人身上没有的意识将它加固，使它显形为一个观念，用浅白的话揭出他们未能将它行出来的失败。……他的闲暇将永远是这样。可它不是她生活的一部分。在这份安恬里没有保障……我们会永远这样坐着；我们必须，她朝着他的浑然不觉，在心里不耐烦地说。他为什么就*感知*不到这里头有的那种生活、那种生活方式——这样安安静静地同坐？他心里想的莫非就只有他的诵读？她听了一阵，半个心被卷进了文本的质地。那里头有真实在，斯宾诺莎，独自一人，听上去仿佛那些字句此刻才头一回被描画出来。有朝一日，在一阵空白的间隙里，她会去读它，同意又不同意，携走某个念头，失了又寻回，一路下去，失而复得，是而又非。……

===Original===

When he went away her life would be swept clear of intelligently selected books and the sting of conflict with them﻿   … that would not matter; perhaps; books would come, somehow, in the unexpected way they always did. But it was impossible to face the ending of these settled tranquil elderly evenings of peaceful unity, the quiet dark-bearded form, sitting near, happily engrossed.﻿   …

===Chinese===

等他走了，她的生活会被清扫一空，不再有慧心拣选的书籍，不再有与它们交锋的刺痛……那不要紧；也许；书总会来的，不知怎的，以它们一贯那不期而至的方式。可是要面对这一个个安顿、恬静、老成而和洽如一的夜晚的终结——那安静的黑胡子的身影，坐在近旁，欣然入神——却是不可能的。……

===Original===

“Well, what do you think of this?”

“I haven’t been attending. But I will read it﻿   … some time.”

“Ah, it is a pity. But tell me your thoughts at least.”

“Oh, I was thinking of my sisters.”

“Ah. You must tell me,” and again with unrelaxed interest he was listening to story after story, finding strange significances, matter for envy and deep chuckles of appreciative laughter.

===Chinese===

「那么，你觉得这个怎么样？」

「我方才没有留心听。不过我会读的……改天。」

「啊，这真可惜。可至少，把你的想法讲给我听吧。」

「哦，我在想我的姐妹们。」

「啊。你一定要讲给我听，」于是他又怀着毫不松懈的兴致，听着一个又一个的故事，从里头发现种种奇异的意趣，发现教人艳羡的题材，发出一阵阵深深的、会心的轻笑。

## X / X

===Original===

With a parting glance at Mr. Shatov’s talked-out indolent vacuity, she plunged, still waiting in the attitude of conversation, into a breathless silence. She would make no more talk. There should be silence between them. If he broke it, well and good; in future she would take measures to curtail the hours of conversation leading, now that she was at home in possession of the Russian life and point of view, only to one or other of his set of quoted opinions, beyond which he refused to move. If not, the quality of their silence would reveal to her what lay behind their unrelaxed capacity for association. The silence grew, making more and more space about her, and still he did not speak. It was dismantling; unendurable. With every moment they both grew smaller and smaller, moving quickly towards the quenching of all their interchange. But there was no doubt now. The question was there between them, for equal contemplation. His easy indolence had fled; his usual pallor heightened, and he sat regarding her with an unhesitating personal gaze. Her determination closed about him, blocking his way, filling the room. He *must* emerge, admit. He must at least *see*, as she saw, if it were only the extent of their dependence on each other. He knew his need. Perhaps she fulfilled it less than she thought? Perhaps it was hers alone.﻿ ﻿… His multiplied resources made hers humiliatingly greater. The shrine of her current consciousness stood before her; the roots of her only visible future planted forever within it. Losing it, she would be left with her burden of being once more scattered and unhoused.

===Chinese===

朝沙托夫先生那已被谈话掏空的、怠惰的空茫投去一瞥作别，她——还端着交谈的姿态等候着——一头坠入了叫人屏息的沉默。她再不要多说话了。他们之间就该有沉默。倘若由他来打破，那也好；往后她要设法裁减那些谈话的钟点——如今她既已安然握有俄国的生命与观点，那些谈话到头来无非引到他那一套引来的意见的这一条或那一条上，越过这些，他执意不肯挪动一步。若不由他打破，那么他们沉默的质地，便会向她揭示：在他们那从不松弛的相与的能力背后，究竟沉着什么。沉默生长起来，在她四周撑出越来越多的空间，而他依旧不作一声。它在把一切都拆了下去；叫人无法忍受。时时刻刻，他们两个越缩越小，飞快地走向他们全部往来的熄灭。可此刻再没有疑问了。那问题就横在他们之间，供两人同等地静观。他那份从容的怠惰已然遁去；他惯常的苍白深了一层，他坐在那儿端详她，目光是个人的，毫不迟疑。她的决心围拢住他，堵住他的去路，灌满了整间屋子。他*必须*现身，承认。他至少必须*看见*，像她看见的那样——哪怕只看见他们彼此倚赖到了什么地步。他知道自己的需要。也许她填补它，还及不上她自己以为的那样多？也许那需要单单是她的。……他那成倍丰盈的资源，使她的那一份羞辱地愈发庞大。她当下意识的圣殿立在眼前；她唯一望得见的未来，根子永远栽在其中。失去它，她便只剩存在的重负，再一次四散飘零、无所栖身。

===Original===

He rose, bringing her to her feet, and stood before her ready to go or stay as she should choose, heaping up before her with an air of gently ironic challenge, the burden of responsibility; silently offering her one of his borrowed summaries, some irrelevant and philosophic worldly wisdom. But it was what he felt. There was something he feared. Alone, he would not have initiated this scene. She faltered, driven back and disarmed by the shock of an overwhelming pity﻿ ﻿… unexpected terrible challenge from within, known to no one, to be accepted or flouted on her sole eternal responsibility.﻿ ﻿… In a torture of acceptance she pressed through it and returned remorseless to her place, flooded as she moved by a sudden knowing of wealth within herself now being strangely quarried.

The long moment was ending; into its void she saw the seemings of her grown life pass and disappear. His solid motionless form, near and equal in the twilight, grew faint, towered above her, immense and invisible in a swift gathering swirling darkness bringing him nearer than sight or touch. The edges of things along the margin of her sight stood for an instant sharply clear and disappeared leaving her faced only with the swirling darkness shot now with darting flame. She ceased to care what thoughts might be occupying him, and exulted in the marvel. Here already rewarding her insistence, was payment in royal coin. She was at last, in person, on a known highway, as others, knowing truth alive. She stared expostulation as she recognised the celebrated nature of her experience, hearing her own familiar voice as on a journey, in amazed expostulation at the absence everywhere of simple expression of the quality of the state﻿ ﻿… a voyage, swift and transforming, a sense of passing in the midst of this marvel of flame-lit darkness, out of the world in glad solitary confidence with wildly, calmly beating morning heart.

The encircling darkness grew still, spread wide about her; the moving flames drew together to a single glowing core. The sense of his presence returned in might. The rosy-hearted core of flame was within him, within the invisible substance of his breast. Tenderly transforming his intangible expansion to the familiar image of the man who knew her thoughts she moved to find him and marvel with him.

===Chinese===

他站起身，带得她也起立，立在她面前，去留听凭她选择，以一种温和而讥诮的挑战神气，把责任的重担在她面前高高堆起；又默默递上他那一套借来的总结中的一条，某种不相干的、哲学气的处世明哲。可那正是他所感到的。有一样东西叫他害怕。若是他独自一人，决计不会挑起这一幕。她踉跄了，被一阵铺天盖地的怜悯的震击打退、卸了武装……一场自内而来的、意想不到的可怕挑战，无人知晓，要凭她独自一人的永恒责任去承受或嘲弃。……她在承受的酷刑里从中间挤了过去，毫无悔意地回到自己的位置上，一举一动之间被一种突然的知晓漫过——她自身之内，正有一种财富被奇异地开采出来。

那漫长的时刻正在收束；在它的空虚里，她眼见自己长成的生命的种种表象一一过去、消隐。他那沉实不动的身形，在暮色里又近、又与她平等，渐渐淡去，又巍然高耸在她之上，在一阵迅速聚拢、回旋而来的黑暗里庞大而不可见，那黑暗把他带得比目见与抚触更近。她视界边缘上各物的棱线，锋锐清晰地立了一瞬，随即消失，只留她独自面对那回旋的黑暗，此刻黑暗里已射出窜动的火焰。她不再管有什么念头正占据着他，只在这奇迹里欢腾。就在此地，她的坚持已得了偿付，以帝王金币偿付。她终于亲身在一条人人识得的大道上，同旁人一样，识得了活的真理。她认出了这段经历素负盛名的性质，瞪目抗辩着，听见自己那熟悉的嗓音，如在旅途上一般，惊愕地抗辩这境界之质地竟处处都没有简单的表达……一场迅疾而蜕变的航行，一种穿行在这焰照黑暗的奇迹正中间的感觉，怀着欢欣而孤独的信心离尘而去，带着一颗狂野而又平静地搏动的清晨的心。

四合的黑暗静了下来，向她周围铺展开去；窜动的火焰聚拢成一颗灼灼的核心。对他的临在的知觉挟着威势归来了。那颗玫瑰芯子的火焰核心就在他里面，在他胸中那不可见的实质里。她温柔地把他那无形的弥漫，变回那个熟悉的形象——那个识得她心思的男人——她移步去寻他，要与他一同惊叹。

===Original===

His voice budded gently, but with the same quality that had flung her back solid and alone into the cold gloom.

“We must consider”﻿ ﻿… what did he think had happened? He had kissed a foreign woman. Who did he think was hearing him?﻿ ﻿… “what you would do under certain circumstances.” The last words came trembling, and he sat down clearly visible in the restored blue twilight; waiting with willing permanence for her words.

===Chinese===

他的声音轻轻绽出，却带着先前那同一种质地——正是它，把她囫囵而孤单地掷回了寒冷的幽暗。

「我们必须考虑」……他以为发生了什么？他吻了一个外国女人。他以为此刻在听他的是谁？……「在某些情形之下，你会怎么做。」末几个字颤着出来；他在复原的蓝色暮光里坐下来，轮廓分明，甘愿永久地等候她的话。

===Original===

“I should do nothing at all, under any circumstances.”

“Do not forget that I am Jew.”

===Chinese===

「无论在什么情形之下，我都会什么都不做。」

「不要忘记，我是犹太人。」

===Original===

Looking at him with the eyes of her friends Miriam saw the Russian, standing free, beyond Europe, from the stigma of “foreigner.” Many people would think, as she had in the beginning, that he was an intellectual Frenchman, different to the usual “Frenchman”; a big-minded cosmopolitan at any rate; a proud possession. The mysterious fact of Jewishness could remain in the background﻿ ﻿… the hidden flaw﻿ ﻿… as there was always a hidden flaw in all her possessions. To her, and to her adventure, its first step now so far away, an accepted misery powerless to arrest the swift rush of the transforming moments, it need make no difference.

===Chinese===

用她朋友们的眼睛看他，米丽亚姆看见一个俄国人，立在欧洲之外，脱然于「外国人」的污名。许多人会以为——像她起初那样——他是个有智识的法国人，不同于寻常的「法国人」；无论如何是个胸襟宏阔的世界主义者；一件足以为傲的占有。犹太身份这桩神秘的事实可以留在背景里……那隐匿的瑕疵……正如她一切的占有里，总有一处隐匿的瑕疵。对她，对她的这场奇遇——它的第一步如今已那样遥远，成了一桩领受下来的苦难，拦不住蜕变时分迅疾的奔流——它尽可以毫无分别。

===Original===

“Perhaps it shall be better I should go away.”

Where? Into the world of people, who would seem to him not different to herself, see his marvellous surrendered charm, catch him, without knowing who or what he was. Who else could know “Mr. Shatov”?

“Do you want to go away?”

“I do not. But it must be with you to decide.”

“I don’t see why you should go *away*.”

“Then I shall stay. And we shall see.”

===Chinese===

「说不定还是我走开去，会更好一些。」

去哪儿？去到人群的世界里。那些人在他看来将与她自己并无不同，会看见他那奇异的、倾诚的魅力，会把他捉住，却不知道他是谁、是什么。此外还有谁能识得「沙托夫先生」？

「你想走开么？」

「我不想。但这必须由你来决定。」

「我看不出你为什么非要*走开*不可。」

「那我就留下。往后我们且看。」

===Original===

The summer lay ahead, unaltered; the threat of change gone from their intercourse. Tomorrow they would take up life again with a stability; years at their disposal. The need for the moment was to have him out of sight, kill the past hour and return to the idea of him, already keeping her standing, with relaxed power of attention to his little actual pitiful obstructive form, in an independent glow, an easy wealth of assurance towards life whose thronging images, mysteries of cities and crowds, single fixed groups of known places and inexorable people were alight and welcoming with the sense of him.

===Chinese===

夏天横陈在前，原封未动；变化的威胁已从他们的往来中退去。明天他们将带着一种安稳把生活重新拾起；经年的岁月，尽他们支取。眼下的需要，是让他在视野里消失，杀死过去这一小时，回到关于他的观念那儿去——那观念已经支撑着她站立，使她对眼前他那小小的、实实在在、可怜巴巴、碍手碍脚的形骸放松了注意，独立焕发着光，带着一种从容富足的、朝向生活的确信——生活的种种拥攘的意象，城市与人群的诸般神秘，已知的地方与不容分说的人们那一组组固定的群，都因着对他的感觉点亮了、迎上来。

===Original===

Putting on her outdoor things next morning, left in the drawing-room while she snatched her breakfast, she was immensely embarrassed to find him standing silently near. The woman facing her in the mirror as she put on her hat was the lonely Miriam Henderson, unendurably asked to behave in the special way. For he was standing eloquently silent and the hands arranging her hat trembled reassuringly. But what was she to do? How turn and face him and get back through the room and away to examine alone the surprises of being in love? Her image was disconcerting, her clothes and the act of rushing off to tiresomely engrossing work inappropriate. It was paralysing to be *seen* by him struggling with a tie. The vivid colour that rushed to her cheeks turned her from the betraying mirror to the worse betrayal of his gaze. But it was enough for the moment, which she faced out, downcast, yet joyful in giving what belonged to his grave eyes.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，她匆匆吞早餐的当儿，他给撂在客厅里；待她穿戴出门的衣物，却发现他默默立在近旁，窘得她无地自容。她戴帽子时，镜中迎面望着她的那个女人，是孤身的米丽亚姆·亨德森，被无可容忍地要求以那特殊的方式行事。因为他正一言不发而雄辩地立着，而理着帽子的那双手，正叫人安心地颤抖。可她该怎么办？怎样转过身去面对他，怎样退过这间屋子、走出门去，独自去查验恋爱的种种惊奇？她的镜像令人狼狈，她的衣裳，连同飞奔去赴那烦人心神、缠住人不放的营生的这番举动，都不合时宜。被他*看着*自己跟一根领带纠缠不清，简直叫人瘫软。涌上双颊的鲜艳血色，使她从那面泄露心迹的镜子转开，转向更糟的泄露——他的凝视。但这一下尽够当下之用；她垂着眼把这片刻撑了过去，而在把他那双严肃的眼睛所应得的东西交付出去时，满心欢悦。

===Original===

“We cannot be as boy and girl,” he said gently, “but we may be very happy.”

Overwhelmed with the sense of inadequate youth Miriam stared at his thought. A fragment of conversation flashed into her mind. Jewish girls married at eighteen, or never. At twenty-one they were old maids.﻿ ﻿… He was waiting for some sign. Her limbs were powerless. With an immense effort she stretched forth an enormous arm and with a hand frightful in its size and clumsiness, tapped him on the shoulder. It was as if she had knocked him down, the blow she had given resounding through the world. He bent to catch at her retreating hand with the attitude of carrying it to his lips, but she was away down the room, her breath caught by a little gurgle of unknown laughter.

===Chinese===

「我们不能像少男少女那样，」他温柔地说，「但我们可以非常幸福。」

被那份青春不足之感压倒，米丽亚姆怔怔地瞪着他的这个念头。一段谈话的碎片闪进她心里。犹太女孩十八岁出嫁，不然就永不。到了二十一岁，她们便是老姑娘了。……他在等候某个示意。她的四肢毫无气力。她费了巨大的努力，伸出一条硕大无朋的胳膊，用一只大得吓人、笨得吓人的手，在他肩上轻轻一拍。那就好像她把他打倒在地了似的，她给的那一记，轰响着传遍世界。他俯下身去捉她退走的手，摆出要把它送到唇边的姿势，可她已经掠过房间远去了，一口气被一串小小的、素不相识的咯咯笑声截住了。

===Original===

He was at the end of the street in the evening, standing bright in the golden light with a rose in his hand. For a swift moment, coming down the shaded street towards the open light she denied him, and the rose. He had bought a rose from some flower-woman’s basket, an appropriate act suggested by his thoughts. But his silent, most surrendered, most childlike gesture of offering, his man’s eyes grave upon the rose for her, beneath uplifted childlike plaintive brows, went to her heart, and with the passing of the flower into her hand, the gold of the sunlight, the magic shifting gleam that had lain always day and night, yearlong in tranquil moments upon every visible and imagined thing, came at last into her very hold. It *had* been love then, all along. Love *was* the secret of things.

They wandered silently, apart, along the golden-gleaming street. She listened, amidst the far-off sounds about them, to the hush of the great space in which they walked, where voices, breaking silently in from the talk of the world, spoke for her, bringing out, to grow and expand in the sunlight, the thoughts that lay in her heart. They had passed the park, forgetting it, and were enclosed in the dust-strewn narrowness of the Euston Road. But the dust grains were golden, and her downcast eyes saw everywhere, if she should raise them, the gleam of roses flowering on the air, and when, their way coming too soon towards its familiar end, they turned, with slow feet, down a little alley, dark with voices, the dingy house-fronts gleamed golden about her, the narrow strip of sky opened to an immensity of smiling spacious blue, and she still saw, just ahead the gleam of flowers and heard on a breath purer than the air of the open country, the bright sound of distant water.

===Chinese===

傍晚，他立在街的尽头，沐在金色的光里，手里拈着一支玫瑰，通体明亮。有一刹那，沿着蔽荫的街朝那敞开的亮处走来，她不肯认他，也不肯认那玫瑰。他从哪个卖花女人的篮子里买了一支玫瑰，一个由他的心思提点出来的、得体的举动。可他那沉默的、倾诚到极处、孩子气到极处的呈献姿势，他那双男人的眼睛沉沉地注视着给她的玫瑰，扬起的眉宇间一副孩子气的哀恳，直抵她的心；而随着那支花渡到她手里，日光之金——那一道昼夜长年躺在每一件可见与可想之物的静谧时刻上的、变幻不定的魔光——终于真真切切落入她的掌握。那么，那*一直*就是爱，自始至终。爱*正是*万物的秘密。

他们默默漫游，彼此隔开一段，沿着金光闪烁的街。她听着——在四围遥远的声响中间——听他们行走于其间的那个浩大空间的静默；在那里，种种声音从世界的谈论中悄然破入，替她开口，把她心中躺着的那些思绪带将出来，在阳光里生长、舒张。他们走过了公园，把它忘了，被裹进尤斯顿路那撒满尘土的窄狭里。可尘粒是金色的，她低垂的眼睛看得见——倘若抬起来——到处是玫瑰在空气里开放的微光；而他们的路太快地临近那熟惯的尽头时，他们折转，迈着缓慢的脚步，拐下一条小径，那巷子因人声而幽暗，黑黢黢的临街门面在她四周泛着金光，窄窄的一线天敞开成一浩片微笑的、空阔的蓝；她仍看见，就在前方，花朵的闪光，并在一口比旷野的空气更纯净的气息上，听见远处水声的明亮。

## XI / XI

===Original===
For many days they spent their leisure wandering in the green spaces of London, restored to Miriam with the frail dreamlike wonder they had held in her years of solitude, deepened to a perpetual morning brightness. She recalled, in the hushed reconciliation of the present, while they saw and thought in unison, breaking their long silences with anecdotes, reliving together all they could remember of childhood, their long exhausting, thought-transforming controversies. And as her thoughts had been, so now, in these same green places were her memories transformed.

===Chinese===
好多天来，他们的闲暇都消磨在伦敦一片片绿地间的漫游里；那些绿地重新回到了米丽亚姆面前，带着她独居岁月里它们曾怀有的那种脆弱的、梦一般的惊奇，而且加深成了一种恒久的晨光。她回想起——在眼前这场静默的和解之中，他们看着、想着，如出一辙，用一段段轶闻趣事打破长长的沉默，一同把记忆所及的童年重新过一遍——回想起他们那些漫长的、耗人心力、改造思想的论争。正如她的思想当年曾被改造，如今，在这些同样的绿地里，她的记忆也被改造着。

===Original===
She watched, wondering, while elderly relatives, hated and banished, standing, forgotten like past nightmares, far away from her independent London life, but still powerful in memory to strike horror into her world, came forth anew, food as she breathlessly spoke their names and described them, for endless speculation. With her efforts to make him see and know them, they grew alive in her hands, significant and attractive as the present, irrecoverable, gone, lonely and pitiful, conquered by her own triumphant existence in a different world, free from obstructions, accompanied, understood. Between the movements of conversation from figure to figure, a thread of reflection wove itself in continuous repetition. Perhaps to all these people, life had once looked free and developing. Perhaps, if she went their way, she might yet share their fate. Never. She was mistress of her fate; there was endless time. The world was changed. They had never known freedom or the endlessness of the passing moment. Time for them had been nothing but the continuous pressure of fixed circumstances.

===Chinese===
她看着，惊奇着，那些年长的亲戚——被憎恨、被放逐，站着，像过去的噩梦一般被遗忘，远离她独立的伦敦生活，却在记忆里仍有力量把恐怖打进她的世界——这时一个个重新走了出来，趁她气喘吁吁说着他们名字、描画着他们模样的当儿，成了供无穷揣想咀嚼的食粮。凭着她要让他看见他们、认识他们的种种努力，他们在她手里活了起来，像眼前一样意味深长而动人，不可挽回，逝去了，孤单而可怜，被她自己在一个不同世界里得胜的存在所征服——那世界没有梗阻，有人陪伴，被人理解。在谈话从一个身影移到另一个身影的动作之间，一条思绪的线反复不停地织着自身。也许对这一切人来说，生活都曾一度显得自由而发展着。也许，她若走了他们的路，也可能仍要分得他们的命运。决不。她是自己命运的主妇；时间无穷无尽。世界已经变了。他们从来不曾认识自由，不曾认识流逝时刻的无穷无尽。对他们来说，时间无非是固定境况连绵不断的重压。

===Original===
Distant parts of London, whither they wandered far through unseen streets, became richly familiar, opening, when suddenly they would realise that they were lost, on some scene, stamped as unforgettably as the magic scenes of holiday excursions. They lingered in long contemplation of all kinds of shop windows, his patient unmoved good-humour while she realised his comparative lack of tastes and preferences, and held forth at length on the difference between style and quality, and the products of the markets, his serene effrontery in taking refuge at last behind the quaintest little tales, satirical, but dreadfully true and illuminating, disarmed her impatience and sent her forward in laughter. He seemed to have an endless supply of these little tales, and told them well, without emphasis, but each one a little drama, perfectly shaped and staged. She collected and remembered and pondered them, the light they shed on unfamiliar aspects of life, playing comfortingly over the future. If Judges and Generals and Emperors and all sorts of people fixed and labelled in social life were really absurd, then social life, with him, might be not merely unaffrighting, but also amusing. At the same time she was affronted by his inclusion of English society in his satirical references. There were, she was sure, hidden and active, in all ranks in England, a greater proportion of people than in any country of his acquaintance, who stood outside his criticism.

===Chinese===
伦敦那些遥远的地界——他们穿过一条条不见于眼底的街巷远远漫游到的地方——变得丰足地熟悉了；每当他们蓦然发觉迷了路，眼前便展开某一处景象，像假日远足中那些魔幻的景象一样，盖下不可磨灭的印记。他们在形形色色的橱窗前流连谛视，他那份耐心的、不动声色的好脾气——这时她发觉他相对地缺少趣味与偏好——而她就风格与品质之别、就市场的各色出产大发议论；他末了竟从容无耻地躲到一个个最古怪的小故事背后去，那些故事带讥讽，却真实得可怕、照彻人心，卸下了她的不耐，送她笑着往前走。他仿佛有这些小故事的无穷储备，而且讲得好，不着重音，可每一个都是一出小戏，情节场面都完美。她把它们收集、记取、玩味，它们照亮的那些人生不熟的面，令人安慰地铺展在未来之上。倘若法官们、将军们、皇帝们、社会生活里一切被固定被贴了标签的人们果真荒唐，那么同他一起，社交生活就不仅可以不骇人，还可以有趣。与此同时，他把英国社会也收进他讥讽的射程里，这叫她受了冒犯。她确信，在英国的各个阶层，藏着、活跃着一大批人，比他相熟的任何一国比例都大，是站在他的批评之外的。

===Original===
She avoided the house, returning only when the hour justified a swift retreat from the hall to her room; escape from the dimly-lit privacy of the deserted drawing-room. Not again could she suffer his nearness, until the foreigner in him, dipped every day more deeply into the well of English feeling, should be changed. When she was alone, she moved, thoughtless, along a pathway that led backwards towards a single memory. Far away in the distance, coming always nearer, was the summer morning of her infancy, a permanent standing arrested, level with the brilliance of flower-heads motionless in the sunlit air; no movement but the hovering of bees. Beyond this memory towards which she passed every day more surely, a marvellous scene unfolded. And always with the unfolding of its wide prospects, there came a beautifying breath. The surprise of her growing comeliness was tempered by a sudden curious indifference. These new looks of hers were not her own. They brought a strange publicity. She felt, turned upon her, the welcoming, approving eyes of women she had contemptuously neglected, and upon her own face the dawning reflection of their wise, so irritating smile. She recognised them, half fearfully, for they alone were the company gathered about her as she watched the opening marvel. She recognised them for lonely wanderers upon the earth. They, these women, then were the only people who *knew*. Their smile was the smile of these wide vistas, wrought and shaped, held back by the pity they turned towards the blind life of men; but it was *alone* in its vision of the spaces opening beyond the world of daily life.

===Chinese===
她避开那所房子，只在钟点容许她从门厅急急退回自己房里的时候才回来；逃离那无人光顾的客厅里灯光昏暗的幽私。她不能再经受他的迫近——直到他里面的那个外国人，一天天更深地浸入英国感情的井里，被改变过来为止。独自一人时，她便无知无觉地沿着一条小径移动，那小径向后通向单一的记忆。远远的天边，总在靠近的，是她婴孩时代的一个夏日清晨：一场永久凝止的伫立，与一簇簇花头齐平，在阳光照耀的空气里纹丝不动；除了蜂儿的悬停，再无动静。越过这段她一天天更笃定地走近的记忆，一幅奇景展开了。而每当它宽阔的远景铺展开来，总有一阵美化之息随之而来。她自己日渐动人的容色带来的惊奇，被一阵突发的古怪淡漠冲淡了。她这副新面目不是她自己的。它们带来一种古怪的公开。她感觉到那些被她轻蔑冷落过的女人们的眼睛转到她身上，迎纳着、赞许着，还感觉到她们那种聪慧得恼人的微笑，正在她自己脸上初现微光。她认出了她们，半带惊惧，因为在她凝望那展开的奇景时，聚在她身边作陪的唯有她们。她认出她们是世上孤独的漂泊者。她们，这些女人，便是唯一*知道*的人。她们的微笑正是这些辽阔远景的微笑，锤炼而成、塑造而定，因她们投向男人们盲目生活的怜悯而有所收敛；可是在望见日常生活世界之外展开的空阔这一点上，这微笑是*孤独无伴*的。

===Original===
The open scene, that seemed at once without her and within, beckoned and claimed her, extending forever, without horizons, bringing to her contemplating eye a moving expansion of sight ahead and ahead, earth and sky left behind, across flower-spread plains whose light was purer and brighter than the light of day. Here was the path of advance. But pursuing it she must be always alone; supported in the turmoil of life that drove the haunting scene away, hidden beyond the hard visible horizon, by the remembered signs and smiles of these far-off lonely women.

===Chinese===
那开阔的景象，仿佛既在她外又在她内，招引着她，认领着她，永远延展，没有地平线，给她凝望的眼睛带来前方复前方、移动着扩展的视界，天与地被抛在后面，越过繁花铺展的平原，那里的光比日光更纯粹、更明亮。这便是前进的路径。可循着它走，她必须永远独自一人；在那把萦绕的景象驱走、藏到坚硬可见的地平线之外的生活喧嚣里，凭这些遥远孤独的女人们被记起的示意与微笑，她得到支撑。

---

===Original===
Between them and their second week stood a promised visit to the Brooms; offering itself each time she surveyed it, under a different guise. But when, for their last evening together, he surprised her, so little did he ever seem to plan or reflect, with stall tickets for the opera she was overwhelmed by the swift regardless pressure of events. Opera, forever outside her means and forgotten, descending thus suddenly upon her without space for preparation of mind, would seem to be wasted. Not in such unseemly haste could she approach this crowning ornament of social life. She was speechless, too, before the revelation of his private ponderings. She knew he was indifferent, even to the theatre, and that he could not afford this tremendous outlay. His recklessness was selfless; a great planning for her utmost recreation. In her satisfaction he was to be content. Touched to the heart she tried to express her sense of all these things, much hampered by the dismayed anticipation of failure, on the great evening, to produce any satisfying response. She knew she would dislike opera; fat people, with huge voices, screaming against an orchestra, in the pretence of expressing emotions they had never felt. But he assured her that opera was very beautiful, Faust perhaps the most beautiful and charming of all, and drew her attention to the massed voices. To this idea she clung, in the interval, for enlightenment.

===Chinese===
横在他们与第二周之间的，是一次应承下的对布鲁姆家（the Brooms）的造访；她每回盘点它，它都以一副不同的面目现身。可是，为了他们相聚的最后一晚，他——这个看去多么少作打算、少费思量的人——竟拿出歌剧的正厅票来叫她吃惊，事情这股迅疾无顾的冲力把她压倒了。歌剧，永远在她的财力之外、早被忘怀的东西，这样突兀地降临到她头上，不给心智留一点准备的余地，眼看要白白糟蹋了。以这样失礼的仓促，她无法去接近社交生活的这顶冠冕。而且，他私下这番筹思的披露，也叫她无言以对。她知道他对戏剧都无动于衷，更知道他负担不起这笔惊人的开销。他的挥霍是无私的；一场为她最大消遣而作的大筹划。她满足了，他便知足。她心里感动，想把这一切都道出感激，却又被一种沮丧的预感重重牵绊——预感在那个盛大的夜晚，她拿不出任何像样的回应。她知道自己会讨厌歌剧；胖人们，顶着巨大的嗓门，对着乐队嘶喊，假装表达着他们从不曾感到的激情。可他向她保证，歌剧非常美，《浮士德》（Faust）也许是其中最美最迷人的，又把她的注意力引到合唱的众声上去。幕间休息时，她便抱着这个念头，指望开窍。

===Original===
But after spending all her available funds on an evening blouse and borrowing a cloak from Jan she found herself at the large theatre impressed only by the collected mass of the audience. The sense of being small and alone, accentuated by the presence of little Mr. Shatov, neatly in evening dress at her side, persisted, growing, until the curtain rose. So long as they had wandered about London and sat together in small restaurants, the world had seemed grouped about them, the vast ignored spectator of a strange romance. But in this huge enclosure, their small, unnoticed, unquestioned presences seemed challenged to account for themselves. All these unmoved people, making the shut-in air cold with their unconcern, even when they were hushed with the strange appealing music of the overture, were moving with purpose and direction because of their immense unconsciousness. Where were they going? What was it all about? What, she asked herself, with a crowning pang of desolation, as the curtain went relentlessly up, were he and she to be or do in this world? What would they become, committed, identified, two small desolate, helpless figures, with the crowding mass of unconscious life?

===Chinese===
可是在把全部可动用的钱花在一件晚装短衫上、又向简（Jan）借了一件斗篷之后，她到了那座大剧院，印象里却只剩聚拢成一大团的观众。渺小与孤独之感——又因小小的沙托夫先生一身齐整的晚礼服侍立在她身旁而愈发加深——持续着，生长着，直到幕启。只要他们还在伦敦四处漫游、还一同坐在小饭馆里，世界便仿佛围聚在他们四周，是一场奇特罗曼史那庞大而被忽视的看客。可是在这巨大的围场里，他们两个小小的、无人留意、无人过问的存在，倒像是被喝令起来，要为自家的来路申辩。所有这些不动声色的人，以他们的漠然把围拢的空气变冷，即便在序曲那奇异的恳切音乐叫他们安静下来的时候，也因着他们巨大的无意识而怀着目的和方向移动着。他们要去哪里？这一切究竟是为了什么？当大幕无情地升起时，她带着登峰造极的荒凉之痛问自己：他和她在这世上究竟要做什么、成为什么？他们将会变成什么——这两个渺小、凄凉、无助的身影，一旦被托付、被认同于这拥挤成团的无意识的生活？

===Original===
“I find something of grandeur in the sober dignity of this apartment. It is medieval Germany at its best.”

“It is very dark.”

“Wait, wait. You shall see life and sunshine, all in the most beautiful music.”

===Chinese===
「我觉得这间屋子素穆的庄严里有一种宏大。这是中世纪德国最好的样子。」

「这里头很暗。」

「等等，等等。你会看见生活与阳光，全都裹在最美的音乐里。」

===Original===
The sombre scene offered the consolation, suddenly insufficient, that she had found in the past in sliding idly into novels, the restful sense of vicarious life. She had heard of a wonderful philosophy in Faust, and wondered at Mr. Shatov’s claim for its charm. But there was, she felt, no space, on the stage, for philosophy. The scene would change, there was “charm” and sunshine and music ahead. This scene itself was changing as she watched. The old man talking to himself was less full of meaning than the wonderful German interior, the pointed stonework and high, stained windows, the carved chairs and rich old manuscripts. Even as he talked, the light from the night-sky, pouring down outside on a beautiful old German town, was coming in. And presently there would be daylight scenes. The real meaning of it all was scenes, each with their separate, rich, silent significance. The scenes were the story, the translation of the people the actual picture of them as they were by themselves behind all the pother.﻿ ﻿… She set herself, drifting in solitude away from the complications of the present, to watch Germany. The arrival of Mephistopheles was an annoying distraction suggesting pantomine. His part in the drama was obscured by Mr. Shatov’s whispered eulogies of Chaliapin, “the only true Mephistopheles in Europe.” It certainly seemed right that the devil should have “a most profound bass voice.” The chanting of angels in Paradise, she suggested, could only be imagined in high clear soprano, whereat he maintained that women’s voices unsupported by the voices of men were not worth imagining at all.

===Chinese===
这素暗的场面所提供的慰藉——她过去懒懒滑进小说里时寻到的那种代他人生活的安适感——忽然显得不够了。她听说过《浮士德》里有一套奇妙的哲学，对沙托夫先生对它魅力的称许颇感诧异。可是她觉得，舞台上没有哲学的位置。场面会换的，前方有「魅力」、有阳光、有音乐。这眼前的场面本身，就在她注视下变化着。那自言自语的老人，比起这美妙的德国室内景——尖拱的石作、高高的彩绘窗、雕花的椅子和华贵的旧抄本——意义反倒单薄。就在他说话的当儿，夜空的光，在外头倾泻在一座美丽的老德国城上，正透进来。而顷刻之间便会有白昼的场景了。这一切真正的意义在于场面，每一个场面各有其单独的、丰盈而无声的意味。场面即是故事，是人物的译文，是他们抛开一切喧嚣、独处时的真容实照。……她把自己安顿下来，独自漂离眼前的种种纠葛，去看德国。梅菲斯托费勒斯（Mephistopheles）的登场是一段恼人的岔戏，令人想起童话哑剧。他在戏里的份量，被沙托夫先生对夏里亚宾（Chaliapin）的低声颂扬盖过了——「欧洲唯一真正的梅菲斯托费勒斯」。魔鬼理当有一副「最深沉的低音」，这看上去确实是对的。而乐园里众天使的吟唱，她提出，只能想象成高亢清亮的女高音；对此他坚称，没有男声托底的女声根本不值得想象。

===Original===
“Pippa passes. It is a matter of opinion.”

“It is a matter of fact. These voices are without depth of foundation. What is this Pippa?”

“And yet you think that women can rise higher, and fall lower, than men.”

===Chinese===
「《皮帕走过》（Pippa Passes）。这问题是见仁见智的。」

「这是个事实问题。这些声音没有根基的深度。这皮帕是什么？」

「可你却认为，女人能升得比男人高，也落得比男人低。」

===Original===
She walked home amidst the procession of scenes, grouped and blending all about her, free of their bondage to any thread of story, bathed in music, beginning their life in her as memory, set up forever amongst her store of realities. It *had* been a wonderful evening, opera *was* wonderful. But the whole effect was threatened, as it stood so lovely all about her in the night air, by his insistence upon a personal interpretation, surprising her in the midst of the garden scene and renewed now as they walked, by little attempts to accentuate the relationship of their linked arms. Once more she held off the threatened obliteration. But the scenes had retreated, far away beyond the darkness and light of the visible street. With sudden compunction she felt that it was she who had driven them away, driven away the wonders that were after all his gift. If she had softened towards him, they would have gone, just the same.﻿ ﻿… It was too soon to let them work as an influence.

===Chinese===
她在那一列场面的行进中走回家去；它们成群结队、交融混合在她四周，摆脱了对任何故事线索的役属，浸在音乐里，开始作为记忆在她之内过起日子，永远安设进她那一库真实之中。这*的确*是一个奇妙的夜晚，歌剧*确实*美妙。可是，当这一切在夜气里如此可爱地立在她四周时，整体的效果却受到了威胁——威胁来自他坚持的个人解说，在花园一场的中途惊过她，此刻走着路又添了新的，小小心心地着力渲染他们相挽手臂的关系。她再一次挡开了那场威胁着的抹除。可是场面们已经退走了，远远退到可见街道的明暗之外。她蓦然生出悔意：是她把它们赶走的，赶走了那些归根到底本是他馈赠的奇迹。哪怕她对他软下来，它们也照样会走的。……要让它们作为影响力发挥作用，还为时过早。

===Original===
Absurd, too, to try to invent life which did not come of itself. He had desisted and was away, fallen into his thoughtful forgetful singing, brumming out shreds of melody that brought single scenes vividly penetrating the darkness. She called him back with a busy repentance, carelessly selecting from her thronging impressions a remark that instantly seemed meaningless.

===Chinese===
况且，去编造一段不请自来的生活，也是荒唐。他罢了手，人已走远，落进他那沉思忘形的哼唱里，哼出一丝丝旋律，把一个个场面鲜活地送进黑暗。她怀着忙碌的悔意唤他回来，从纷至沓来的印象里随手拈出一句话，那话立时便显得毫无意义。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said heartily, “there is, absolutely, something *echt, kern-gesund* about these old-German things.”

===Chinese===
「是的，」他恳切地说，「这些老德国的东西里头，绝对有一种*地道、芯子里健康*（echt, kern-gesund）的东西。」

===Original===
That was it. It had all meant, really, the same for him; and he knew what it was that made the charm; admitting it, in spite of his strange deep dislike of the Germans. *Kern-Gesundheit* was not a sufficient explanation. But the certainty of his having been within the charm made him real, a related part of the pageant of life, his personal engaging small attribute her own undivided share. On the doorstep, side by side with his renewed silent appeal, she turned and met, standing free, his gentle tremulous salutation.

===Chinese===
就是这话了。这一切对他来说，真正意味的原是同一个东西；而且他知道魅力之所由来；尽管他对德国人怀着古怪而深沉的厌恶，他还是承认了它。*芯子里的健康*（Kern-Gesundheit）不是充分的解释。可是他确曾身在那魅力之内，这份确定使他成为真实的，成为生活盛典上一个沾亲带故的部分，他那点个人的、讨喜的小特质，成了她独享的一份。在门阶上，与他那重新抬头的无声恳求并排，她转过身，自由地站着，迎受他那温柔而颤动的致意。

===Original===
For a moment the dark silent house blazed into light before her. She moved forward, as he opened the door, as into a brightness of light where she should stand visible to them both, in a simplicity of golden womanhood, no longer herself, but his Marguerite, yet so differently fated, so differently identified with him in his new simplicity, going forward together, his thoughts and visions as simple as her own in the life now just begun, from which their past dropped away grey and cold, the irrelevant experience of strangers.

===Chinese===
有一瞬间，那黑暗沉寂的房子在她眼前骤亮起来。他开门时，她向前走去，仿佛走进一片明亮的光，她将在那里站定，为他们两人所见，带着一种金色女性单纯的素朴，不再是她自己，而是他的玛格丽特（Marguerite）——然而命运何其不同，又何其不同地与他相认同，认同于他的新的单纯里，一同向前去，他的思想与幻象同她的一样单纯，在那刚刚开始的生活里，他们的过去从那里灰灰冷冷地脱落下去，像陌生人不相干的阅历。

===Original===
But the hall was dark and the open dining-room door showed blank darkness. She led the way in; she could not yet part from him and lose the strange radiance surrounding herself. They ought to go forward now, together, from this moment, shedding a radiance. To part was to break and mar, forever, some essential irrecoverable glory. They sat side by side on the sofa by the window. The radiance in which she sat crowned, a figure visible to herself, recognisable, humble and proud and simple, back in its Christian origin, a single weak small figure, transfixed with light, dreadfully trusted with the searing, brightly gleaming dower of Christian womanhood, was surrounded by a darkness unpenetrated by the faint radiance the high street lamps must be sending through the thick lace curtains. This she thought is what people mean by the golden dream; but it is not a dream. No one who has been inside it can ever be the same again or quite get out. The world it shows is the biggest world there is. It is *outer* space where God is and Christ waits. “I am very happy, do you feel happy?” The small far-off man’s voice sounded out, lost in the impenetrable darkness. Yet it was through him, through some essential quality in him that she had reached this haven and starting place, he who had brought this smiting descent of certainties which were to carry her on her voyage into the unknown darkness, and since he could not see her smile, she must speak.

===Chinese===
可是门厅是黑的，敞开的餐室门里是一色空茫的黑暗。她引路走了进去；她还不能与他分手，还不能失去环绕着她自己的那圈奇异的辉光。此刻他们理当一同向前去，从这个瞬间起，披洒着辉光。分手便是打碎、便是永远毁伤某种根本的、不可挽回的荣光。他们并肩坐在窗前的沙发上。她坐在其中、加冕于其中的那辉光——一个她自家看得见的身影，可辨认的，谦卑而骄傲而单纯，退回到它基督教的本源里，一个单薄弱小的身影，被光洞穿，被可怕地托付了基督教女性那灼人的、明亮闪耀的嫁资——被一团黑暗围着，高高的街灯透过厚厚挑花窗帘必会送来的微光，也透不进那黑暗。这，她想，便是人们所说的金色的梦；可它不是梦。进到过它里面的人，永远不会再是原样，也永远出不干净。它显示的世界是所有世界里最大的。那是*外的*空阔，上帝在的地方，基督守候的地方。「我很幸福，你觉得幸福吗？」那小小的、遥远的男人嗓音响起来，迷失在不可穿透的黑暗里。然而正是通过他，通过他身上某种本质的东西，她才抵达了这港湾、这起程之地；正是他带来了这场确信的劈头降落，它们将载着她航行进入未知的黑暗；而既然他看不见她的微笑，她便必须开口。

===Original===
“I think so,” she said gently. She must, she suddenly realised, never tell him more than that. His happiness was, she now recognised, hearing his voice, different to hers. To admit and acclaim her own would be the betrayal of a secret trust. If she could dare to lay her hand upon him, he might know. But they were too separate. And if he were to touch her now, they would again be separated for longer than before, for always. “Good night,” she said, brushing his sleeve with the tips of her fingers, “dear, funny little man.”

===Chinese===
「我想是的，」她温柔地说。她突然明白，她绝不能对他说出比这更多的话。听着他的嗓音，她此刻认出，他的幸福与她的不同。承认并颂扬她自己的幸福，便是背叛一桩秘密的信托。倘若她敢把手放上去、放在他身上，他或许会知道。可他们隔得太开了。而倘若他此刻碰了她，他们便会再度分离，比先前更久，永远。「晚安，」她说，指尖轻轻拂过他的衣袖，「亲爱的、有趣的小小人儿。」

===Original===
He followed her closely but she was soon away up the familiar stairs in the darkness, in her small close room, and trying to chide herself for her inadequate response, while within the stifling air the breath of sunlit open spaces moved about her.

===Chinese===
他紧紧跟着，可她很快便没入黑暗里熟悉的楼梯，进了她那间小而闭塞的屋子，试着责备自己回应得不够——而在这窒闷的空气里，阳光照耀的空阔之地的一息，正绕着她流动。

===Original===
But in the morning when the way to King’s Cross Station was an avenue of sunlight, under a blue sky triumphant with the pealing of church bells, his sole conversation was an attempt to induce her to reproduce the epithet. The small scrap of friendliness had made him happy! No one, it seemed, had ever so addressed him. His delight was all her own. She was overcome by the revelation of her power to bless without effort. The afternoon’s visit now seemed a welcome interval in the too swift succession of discoveries. In the cool noisy shelter of the station, Sunday holidaymakers were all about them. He was still charmingly preening himself, set off by the small busy crowd, his eye wandering with its familiar look, a childlike contemplation of the English spectacle. To Miriam’s unwilling glance it seemed for observation a fruitless field; nothing exhibited there could challenge speculation.

===Chinese===
可是到了早晨，通向国王十字车站（King's Cross Station）的路成了一条阳光的大道，蓝天下教堂钟声齐鸣、意气扬扬，他一路唯一的话题，便是想法子逗她再把那句称呼说一遍。那一小片友爱竟叫他快乐起来！看样子，从来没有人这样称呼过他。他的欢喜全成了她自己的。她被这个启示压倒了：她竟拥有不费力气便赐福于人的力量。下午的那次造访，如今倒像是这一连串过于急骤的发现之间一个受欢迎的间歇。在车站清凉而嘈杂的荫庇里，星期天的度假人潮把他们围在当中。他还在讨人喜欢地顾影自赏，被这一小片忙碌的人群一衬，他的眼睛带着那熟悉的神情四处游移，孩子气地观照着英国的景观。在米丽亚姆那不情愿的一瞥里，这片地方作为观察的对象是不毛之地；那里陈列的任何东西都挑动不起思索。

===Original===
On each face, so naively engrossed with immediate arranged circumstance, character, opinion, social conditions, all that might be expected under the small tests of small circumstances, was plainly written in monotonous reiteration. Moving and going, they could go, with all their busy eagerness, no further than themselves. At their destinations other similar selves awaited them, to meet and send them back, unchanged; an endless circling. Over their unchanging, unquestioned world, no mystery brooded with black or golden wings. They would circle unsurprised until for each one came the surprise of death. It was all they had. They were dreadful to contemplate because they suggested only death, unpondered death. Her eye rested for relief upon a barefooted newspaper boy running freely about with his cry, darting head down towards a shouted challenge.

===Chinese===
每一张脸上——都那样天真地埋头于眼下安排好的境况——性格、见解、社会地位，还有在小境况的小考验下可望出现的一切，都以单调的反复明明白白写着。他们动着、去着，可凭他们全部的忙碌热切，也走不出他们自己半步。在他们的目的地，另一些相似的自家等着他们，迎一迎，再原样把他们送回来；一个无尽的循环。他们那不变更、不遭疑问的世界之上，没有神秘张开黑色或金色的翅膀盘旋。他们会毫不诧异地兜着圈子，直到死亡那份诧异临到每个人头上。那是他们的全部。他们望着叫人心里发寒，因为他们只令人想到死，不经思量的死。她的目光落在一个赤脚的报童身上，得一点宽解；他喊着号外，自由地跑来跑去，闻一声吆喝，便低头扎过去。

===Original===
“Before you go,” Mr. Shatov was saying. She turned towards his suddenly changed voice, saw his pale face, grave, and working with the determination to difficult speech; saw him, while she stood listening to the few tense phrases in painful admiration of his courage, horribly transformed, by the images he evoked far away, immovable in the sunshine of his earlier days. The very trembling of his voice had attested the agonising power of his communication. Yet behind it all, with what a calmness of his inner mind, had he told her, now, only now, when they were set in the bright amber of so many days, that he had been lost to her, forever, long ago in his independent past. The train was drawing in. She turned away speechless.

===Chinese===
「在你走之前，」沙托夫先生正说着。她朝他陡然变了的声音转过脸去，看见他苍白的脸，凝重，正为难言的话语而抽动着；她站着，听那几句紧绷的短语，痛苦地赞叹他的勇气，同时看见他可怕地变了样——变于他所唤起的那些形象，它们远在他早年时光的阳光里，纹丝不动。他声音里的颤抖，便已见证了他这番剖白惨痛的力量。然而在这一切背后，他怀着内心何等的平静，告诉了她——此刻，偏偏是此刻，正当他们双双安坐在这么多天明亮琥珀色的时间里——他早就，在他那段独身过去的遥远年月里，永远地失落了，失落给她。列车正在进站。她无言地转过身去。

===Original===
“Miriam, Miriam,” he pleaded in hurried shaken tones close at her side, “remember, I *did not know* that you would come.”

===Chinese===
「米丽亚姆，米丽亚姆，」他紧挨在她身旁，用仓皇震颤的声调恳求道，「记住，我*那时并不知道*你会来。」

===Original===
“Well, I must go,” she said briskly, the words sounding out to her like ghostly hammer-blows upon empty space. Never again should her voice sound. The movement of getting into the train brought a nerve-crisping relief. She had taken the first step into the featureless darkness where, alone, she was to wait, in a merciful silence, forever.

===Chinese===
「好了，我该走了，」她利落地说，那几个字在她听来像幽灵的锤击，敲在空茫之上。她的声音再也不该响起了。登上列车的动作带来一阵激得神经发麻的宽解。她已迈出第一步，进入那无面目的黑暗，在那里她要独自等待，在一种慈悲的沉默里，直到永远。

===Original===
“I shall meet you this evening,” said his raised voice from the platform. He stood with bowed head, his eyes gravely on her unconsidering gaze, until the train moved out. She set her teeth against the slow movement of the wheels, grinding it seemed, smoke-befouled, deliberate, with awful circling relentlessness over her prostrate body, clenched together for the pang, too numb to feel it if only it would come, but left untouched.

===Chinese===
「今晚我去接你，」他从月台上提高了嗓音说。他垂头站着，双眼凝重地对着她那不曾思量的凝视，直到列车开出。她咬紧牙关，抵住车轮缓慢的转动——那转动仿佛在碾磨，被烟雾污损着，不慌不忙，带着骇人的、周而复始的无情，碾过她扑倒的身体；她为那一阵剧痛浑身紧缩，麻木得即便痛真来了也感觉不到，却始终没有被碰着。

===Original===
The crushing of full realisation, piling up behind her numbness, must pass over her. There was not much time. The train was carrying her steadily onward, and towards conversation with the unconscious Brooms. She tried to relax to its movement, to hold back from the entanglements of thought and regard the day as an interval outside the hurrying procession of her life. A way opened narrowly ahead, attainable by one rending effort, into a silence, within which the grey light filtering through the dingy windows on to the grime-greyed floor offered itself with a promise of reassurance. It was known to her; by its unvexed communion with her old self. One free breath of escape from the visions she was holding clutched for inspection, and herself would be given back to her. This awful journey would change to an eternity following serenely on a forgotten masquerade. She would not lose her knowing that all solitary journeys go on forever, waiting through intervals, to renew themselves. But the effort, even if she could endure the pain of it, would be treachery until she had known and seen without reservations the whole meaning of the immovable fact. The agony within her must mean that somewhere behind the mere statements, if she could but get through and discover it, there must be a revelation that would set the world going again; bring back the vanquished sunlight. Meanwhile life must pause, humanity must stay hushed and waiting while she thought. A grey-shod foot appeared on her small empty patch of floor. With the fever of pain that flooded her she realised that she could go neither forward nor back. Life pinned her motionless, in pain. Her eye ran up and found the dreaming face of a girl; the soft fresh lineaments of childhood, shaped to a partial awareness by some fixed daily toil, but still, on all she saw, the gleam she did not know could disappear, did not recognise for what it was, priceless and enough. She would never recognise it. She was one of those women men wrap in lies, persisting unchanged through life, revered and yet odious in the kindly stupidity of thoughts fixed immovably on unreality, the gleam gone, she knew not why, and yet avenged by her awful unconscious production of the kind of social life to which men were tied, compelled to simulate life in her obstinate, smiling fool’s﻿ ﻿… hell. The rest of the people in the carriage were aware, in the thick of conscious deceits; playing parts. The women, strained and defaced, all masked watchfulness, cut off from themselves, weaving romances in their efforts to get back, the men betraying their delight in their hidden opportunities of escape by the animation behind the voice and manners they assumed for the fixed calculable periods of forced association; ready to distract attention from themselves and their hidden treasures by public argument, if accident should bring it about, over anything and everything.

===Chinese===
