全然领悟的碾压，在她的麻木后面越积越高，必将从她身上碾过去。时间不多了。列车载着她平稳前行，载着她走向与浑然不觉的布鲁姆家的交谈。她试着随着车行松弛下来，从思绪的缠结里退出来，把这一天看作她生命匆匆行进之外的一个间歇。前方窄窄地开着一条路，凭一记撕裂般的努力便可抵达，通入一片静默；静默之中，透过肮脏车窗滤在积垢成灰的地板上的灰色光线，正带着抚慰的许诺自荐于她。她认得它；凭它同她旧日自我的不受搅扰的交融。只需从那些她攥着检视的幻象那里逃出自由的一息，她自己便会归还给她。这可怕的旅程便会化作一段永恒，安然接续在一场忘却的假面戏之后。她不会失去她的这份知晓：一切孤独的旅程都永远继续着，穿过一个个间歇等待，以更新自身。可是那努力，纵然她受得住它的痛，也是背信——直到她毫无保留地认识并看清那桩不可移易的事实的全部意义。她体内的苦痛必得意味着：在那些干巴巴的陈述后面某处，只要她能穿过去、把它找出来，必定藏着一场启示，会叫世界重新运转起来；把那被击溃的阳光带回来。其间生活必须停顿，人类必须屏息等待，等她想。一只灰鞋的脚出现在她面前那一小块空地板上。随着涌上来的痛楚的热，她意识到她既不能向前，也不能退后。生活把她钉住不动，钉在疼痛里。她的目光向上跑，寻见一个姑娘出神的脸；童年柔软新鲜的眉目，被某种固定的日常劳作塑成半开的知觉，可在她所见的一切上面，仍有那点微光——她不知道它会消失，认不出它是什么，无价而足够。她永远不会认出它来。她是男人们用谎言包裹起来的那类女人中的一个，一生不改地持续着，在那把思想一成不变钉在虚妄上的好心的愚蠢里，受尊崇而又可憎；微光去了，她不知为何，却又被她那可怕的无意识的生产所报偿——生产出男人们被拴在其上的那种社交生活，被迫在她的固执的、微笑的愚人……地狱里佯装生活。车厢里其余的人都心里有数，身陷有意识欺瞒的重围；都在扮演着角色。女人们，紧绷而破相，全是面具式的警觉，与自家隔断，在找回自家的努力里编织罗曼史；男人们则泄露着他们对偷来的逃逸机会暗藏的欢喜——那欢喜泄在他们为着强制交际的、可计算的固定时段而装出的嗓音与举止背面的那点活泼里；只要凑巧，随时预备用一场当众的争论，就随便什么、一切什么，把注意力从他们自己和他们的暗藏财宝上岔开。

===Original===
At least she saw. But what was the *use* of not being deceived? How in the vast spread of humanity expose the sham? How escape, without surrendering life itself, treacherous countenancing of the fiendish spectacle? What good would death do? What did “*Eine fur Viele*” do? Brought home the truth to one man, who probably after the first shock, soon came to the conclusion that she had been mad.

===Chinese===
至少她看得见。可是，不受欺骗又有什么*用处*？在人类茫茫的铺展里，怎样揭穿这假面？怎样脱身——而不交出生活本身——不去奸险地纵容这恶魔的景观？死又有什么好处？「*一个为众人*」（Eine fur Viele）又成就了什么呢？不过是把真相送进一个男人心里，那人多半在最初的震动过后，很快就断定她疯了。

===Original===
She talked through lunch to the Brooms with such an intensity of animation that when at last the confrontation was at an end and the afternoon begun in the shelter of the dim little drawing-room, she found Grace and Florrie grouped closely about her, wrapped and eager for more. She turned, at bay, explaining in shaken unmeditated words that the afternoon must be spent by her in thinking out a frightful problem, and relapsed, averted swiftly from their sensitive faces, suddenly pale about eyes that reflected her distress, towards the open door of the little greenhouse leading miserably into the stricken garden. They remained motionless in the chairs they had drawn close to the little settee where she sat enthroned, clearly prepared so to sit in silent sympathy while she gazed at her problem in the garden. She sat tense, but with their eyes upon her she could not summon directly the items of her theme. They appeared transformed in words, a statement of the case that might be made to them, “anyone’s” statement of the case, beginning with “after all”; and leaving everything unstated. Applied to her own experience they seemed to have no meaning at all. Summaries were no good. Actual experience must be brought home to make anything worth communicating. “When he first kissed me,” started her mind “those women were all about him. They have come between us forever.” She flushed towards the garden. The mere presence in her mind of such vileness was an outrage on the Broom atmosphere. She could not again face the girls. For some time she sat, driving from point to point in the garden the inexorable fact that she had reached a barrier she could not break down. She could, if she were alone, face the possibility of dashing her life out against it. If she were to turn back from it, she would be rent in twain, and how then, base and deformed could she find spirit to face anyone at all? At last, still with her eyes on the garden, she told them, she must go and think in the open air. They cherished and indulged her in their unaltered way and she escaped, exempted from coming back to tea.

===Chinese===
午饭桌上她对布鲁姆家谈笑得那样起劲，等到那场对坐终于结束、下午在昏暗小客厅的荫庇里开始时，她发现格蕾丝（Grace）和弗洛丽（Florrie）紧紧围坐在她身旁，裹着期待的急切，还想要更多。她穷于应付，转过身，用发颤的、未及思量的话语解释说，这个下午她必须用来想通一个可怕的问题，随即颓然不再言语，急急避开她们敏感的脸——那眼睛周围忽然发白，映着她的苦楚——朝向小温室敞开的门，门凄凄地通向那遭了灾的园子。她们一动不动坐在各自拖近小睡榻的椅子里——她端坐在睡榻上——分明准备好就这样默默同情地坐着，等她把她的难题望进园子里去。她绷紧地坐着，可她们的眼睛落在她身上，她没法把题目的条目直接召唤出来。它们一现身便变了形，化作一篇可以说给她们听的案情陈述，一篇「谁都能作」的案情陈述，开头是「毕竟」；而什么都没有说出来。一用到她自己的经历上，它们仿佛全无意义。摘要没有用。必须把实况本身送进人心里，才谈得上有什么值得传达。「他头一回亲我的时候，」她的心里开了头，「那些女人正围满他全身。她们已经永远插到我们中间来了。」她朝着园子涨红了脸。这样污秽的念头单是出现在她心里，就是对布鲁姆家空气的亵渎。她没法再面对姑娘们。有一阵子她坐着，在园子里从一点驱赶到另一点，驱赶着那桩无可移易的事实：她抵达了一道她打不破的屏障。倘若独自一人，她能够面对把生命朝它撞个粉碎的可能。可倘若从它面前退回去，她便会被撕作两半，那时，卑污而变形的她，又哪里找得到精神去面对任何人呢？末了，眼睛仍望着园子，她告诉她们，她必须出去，到露天里想一想。她们以她们一成不变的方式疼她纵她，她逃脱了，还获准不必回来用茶。

===Original===
Suppose, said the innumerable voices of the road, as she wandered down it relieved and eager in the first moments of freedom, he had not told you? It was sincere and fine of him to tell. Not at all. He wanted to have an easy mind. He has only explained what it was that came between us at the first, and has been waiting ever since to be there again.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
倘使他当初没有告诉你呢？——大路上无数的声音说；她沿着大路走去，在自由最初的一刻里松了口气、跃跃然。他告诉你，是真诚而高尚的。全然不是。他不过是想求个心安。他只是解释了起初插在我们中间的是什么，而且从那以后一直在等着回到那里去。……

===Original===
“Remember; I *did not know* you would come.”

===Chinese===
「记住；我*那时并不知道*你会来。」

===Original===
Why did men not know? That was the strange thing. Why did they make their first impressions of women such as would sully everything that came after? That was the extraordinary thing about the average man and many men who were not average at all. Why?

===Chinese===
男人们为什么不知道？这才是怪事。为什么他们留给女人的第一印象，竟是足以玷污此后一切的东西？这在寻常男人身上、也在许多绝不寻常的男人身上，都是非同寻常的事。为什么？

===Original===
The answer must be there if she could only get through to it. Some immovable answer. The wrong one perhaps, but sufficient to frame an irreversible judgment. There was an irreversible judgment at the heart of it all that would remain, even if further fuller truer reasons were reached later on. Anything that could take the life out of the sunlight was wrong. Every twist and turn of the many little side roads along which she made her way told her that. It was useless to try to run away from it. It remained, the only point of return from the wilderness of anger into which with every fresh attempt at thought, she was immediately flung. The more angry she grew the further she seemed to move from the possibility of finding and somehow expressing, in words that had not sounded in her mind before, the clue to her misery.

===Chinese===
答案必在那里，只要她能穿到它跟前。某个不可移易的答案。也许是错的那一个，却足够铸成一桩不可逆转的判决。这一切的中心有一桩不可逆转的判决，即便日后抵达更远、更全、更真的理由，它也会留存。凡是能抽走阳光里生命的，就是错的。她一路走去的那许多小岔道的每一个转折都这样告诉她。想逃开它是没有用的。它留在那里，是她从愤怒的荒野唯一可以折返的地点；而每一次重新试图思考，她便立即被掷回那荒野。她越愤怒，便仿佛离那种可能越远——找到她的苦难的线索、并用某种方式把它表达出来的可能，用那些还从不在她心里响过的言语。

===Original===
She reached the park at teatime. Its vistas were mercifully empty. She breathed more freely within its greenery. Hidden somewhere here, was relief for the increasing numbness of her brain and the drag of her aching heart. The widening sky understood and would presently, when she had reached the statement that lay now, just ahead, offer itself in the old way, for companionship. Wandering along a little path that wound in and out of a thicket of shrubs, she heard a subdued rumble of voices and came in a moment upon two men, bent-headed in conversation side by side on a secluded seat. They looked up at her and upon their shiny German faces, and in the cold rheumy blue eyes beneath their unconscious intelligent German foreheads, was the horrible leer of their talk. Looking up from it, scanning her in the spirit of the images of life they had evoked in their sequestrated confidential interchange, they identified her with their vision. She turned back towards the wide empty avenues. But there was no refuge in them. Their bleak emptiness reflected the thoughtless lives of English men. Behind her the two Germans were immovably there, hemming her in. They were the answer. Sitting hidden there, in the English park, they were the whole unconscious male mind of Europe surprised unmasked. Thought out and systematised by them, openly discussed, without the cloudy reservations of Englishmen, was the whole masculine sense of womanhood. One image; perceived only with the body, separated and apart from everything else in life. Men were *mind* and *body*, separated mind and body, looking out at women, below their unconscious men’s brows, variously moulded and sanctified by thought, with one unvarying eye. There was no escape from its horrible blindness, no other life in the world to live﻿ ﻿… the leer of a prostitute was﻿ ﻿… reserved﻿ ﻿… beautiful, suggesting a daily life lived independently amongst the impersonal marvels of existence, compared to the headlong desirous look of a man. The greed of men was something much more awful than the greed of a prostitute. She used her last strength to wrench herself away from the hopeless spectacle and wandered impatient and thoughtless in a feverish void. Far away from this barren north London, the chosen perfect stage for the last completion of a misery as wide as the world, was her own dream world at home in her room, her strange unfailing self, the lovely world of lovely things seen in silence and tranquillity, the coming and going of the light, the myriad indescribable things of which day and night, in solitude, were full, at every moment; the marvellous forgetfulness of sleep, followed by the smiling renewal of inexhaustible *sameness*﻿ ﻿… thought flashed in, stabbing her weakness with the reminder that solitude had failed and from its failure she had been saved by the companionship of a man; of whom until today she had been proud in a world lit by the glory and pride of achieved companionships. But it was an illusion, fading and failing more swiftly than the real things of solitude﻿ ﻿… there was no release save in madness; a suddenly descending merciful madness, blotting everything out. She imagined herself raging and raving through the park, through the world, attacking the indifferent sky at last with some final outbreaking statement, something, somewhere within her she *must* say, or die. She gazed defiance upwards at the cloudless blue. The distant trees flattened themselves into dark clumps against the horizon. Swiftly she brought her eyes back to the diminishing earth. Something must be said; not to the sky, but in the world. She grew impatient for Mr. Shatov’s arrival. If only she could convey to him all that was in her mind, going back again and again endlessly to some central unanswerable assertion, the truth would be out. Stated. At least one man brought to book, arrested and illuminated. But what was it? That men are not worthy of women. He would agree, and remain pleading. That men never have, never can, understand the least thing about even the worst woman in the world? He would find things to say. She plunged back groping for weapons of statement, amongst the fixities of the world, there from the beginning, and pressing at last with their mocking accomplishment, against her small thread of existence. Long grappling in darkness against the inexorable images, she fell back at last upon wordless repudiation, and again the gulf of isolation opened before her. The struggle was not to be borne. It was monstrous, unforgivable, that it should be demanded of her. Yet it could not be given up. The smallest glance in the direction of even the simulation of acceptance, brought a panic sense of treachery that flung her back to cling once more to the vanishing securities of her own untouched imagination.

===Chinese===
下午茶时分她到了公园。它的条条远景慈悲地空着。她在它的绿荫里呼吸得自由了些。这里某处藏着解救，解她脑中日渐加重的麻木和她那颗作痛的心的坠拉。那越展越宽的天空懂得她，不久——等她抵达此刻就在前方的那个陈述——便会以旧日的方式自荐于她，作个伴。她沿一条小径游走，小径在一丛灌木里绕进绕出，她听见一阵压低的语声的滚雷，顷刻间便撞见两个男人，并肩坐在僻静的座位上，俯着头交谈。他们抬头看她；在他们油光光的德国人脸上，在他们那不自觉而聪慧的德国人额头底下冷淡、水汪汪的蓝眼睛里，是他们谈话那可怕的淫笑。从那淫笑里抬起眼来，以他们在这场与人隔绝的密谈中唤起的生活形象的精神打量她，他们把她认同进他们的幻象里去了。她折回宽而空的大道。可大道里没有庇护。它们荒凉的空洞映出英国男人们不假思索的生活。在她背后，那两个德国人纹丝不动地在着，把她围在当中。他们就是答案。坐在英国公园里那藏匿之处，他们就是整个欧洲无意识的男性心智，被撞见、被摘了面具。被他们想透、被他们系统化、被他们公开讨论、不带英国人那种含混保留的，是关于女性的整个男性意识。一个形象；只用身体去感知，同生活中其余的一切分离、隔绝。男人是*心*也是*身*，分离的心与身，从他们不自觉的男人眉骨底下望出来，望着女人们——那眉骨曾被思想各式各样地塑造和圣化——用的却是一只从不更改的眼。从它可怕的盲目里无路可逃，世上没有别的生活可过……一个妓女的淫笑倒是……有所保留……美的，暗示着一种独立过活的日子，活在那生存的非个人的诸般奇迹中间——比起男人那不管不顾、欲焰腾腾的一望。……男人的贪婪是比妓女的贪婪可怕得多的东西。她用尽最后的力气，把自己从这无望的景观上拧开，不耐地、无知无觉地在一团热病的虚空里游荡。远离这片贫瘠的北伦敦——它被选中作这场与世界一样宽的苦难最后完成的完美舞台——是她自己房里家一般安顿着的梦的世界，是她那个奇怪的、从不失效的自我，是静默与安宁中看见的可爱事物的可爱世界，是光的来来去去，是独处时昼夜无时无刻不充盈着的那无数不可言说的东西，是睡眠奇妙的忘却，和随后那取之不尽的*同一*微笑着的新生……思想闪进来，带着提醒刺她的软弱：孤独早已败绩，而她正是被一个男人的陪伴从那败绩里救出来的；直到今天，在那个被修成的伴侣关系的荣耀与骄傲照亮的世界里，她还为他骄傲过。可那是幻觉，比孤独的那些真实之物凋谢得、败坏得更快的幻觉……除了疯狂没有解脱；一场突然降临的慈悲的疯狂，把一切抹掉。她想象自己狂怒地喊骂着穿过公园、穿过世界，末了以某句最终迸出的宣言去攻击那冷漠的天空——某句在她体内某处她*必须*说出、否则便死的话。她向着无云的碧空仰目瞪视，满眼不屈。远处的树把自己压平成一块块暗色，抵着地平线。她倏地把眼睛收回这越缩越小的地面。总得有什么被说出来；不是对天空，而是在世上。她等沙托夫先生到来，等得不耐烦了。倘若她能把心里的一切都传达给他，无穷无尽地一遍遍回到某个中心的、无可置答的断言上，真相便会出来。被陈述出来。至少有一个男人被传到案前，被截住，被照亮。可那断言是什么？男人配不上女人。他会同意，然后继续恳求。男人从不曾、也永远不能理解世上哪怕最坏的女人的最微小的一点？他会有话可说。她重新扎回去，摸索陈述的武器，在世界的诸种既定之间——它们从起初就在那里，此刻正以它们嘲弄的圆熟，抵着她那一小股存在的细线。在黑暗中同那些无可移易的形象长久角力之后，她末了退回到无语的否认，于是在她面前，隔绝的深渊又一次张开。这斗争熬不下去。竟要她来承受，这是荒唐的，不可饶恕的。可它又放弃不得。哪怕朝接受的样子那边瞥去最小的一眼，也会带来一阵背信的惊惶，把她掷回去，再度抱住她自己那未曾触动的想象正在消逝的凭依。

===Original===
When at last he appeared, the sight of the familiar distinctive little figure plunging energetically along, beard first, through the north London Sunday evening crowd drifting about the park gates, their sounds quenched by the blare of the Salvation Army’s band marching townwards along the battered road, for one strange moment while a moving light came across the gravel pathway at her feet, decking its shabby fringe of grass with the dewy freshness of some remembered world far away and unknown to this trampling blind north London, she asked herself what all the trouble was about. What after all had changed? Not herself, that was clear. Walking in fevered darkness had not destroyed the light. But he had joined her, pulling up before her with white ravaged face and hands stretched silently towards her.

===Chinese===
待他终于出现——那熟悉的、格局独特的小小身影一路奋力冲撞而来，胡子先着，穿过北伦敦星期天晚上在公园门口游荡的人群，人声都被救世军乐队那沿砸坏的马路朝镇上行进的号音淹熄了——有一个奇怪的时刻，一道移动的光掠过她脚前的碎石小径，给它那圈寒碜的草边缀上某个遥远的记忆之世界的新鲜露意，那世界是这践踏着一切的、盲目的北伦敦所不识的，她问自己，这一切烦恼究竟是为了什么。究竟有什么变了呢？不是她自己，这清楚得很。在发热的黑暗里走路，并没有毁掉光。可是他到了她跟前，在她面前收住脚，脸色被摧毁般苍白，双手无声地朝她伸过来。

===Original===
“For pity’s sake don’t touch me,” she cried involuntarily and walked on, accompanied, examining her outcry. It was right. It had a secret *knowledge*. They rode in silence on tram and bus. Below them on the dimly-lit pavements people moved, shadows broken loose and scattered in the grey of night. Gaslit, talking faces succeeded each other under the street lamps; not one speaking its thoughts; no feeling expressed that went even as deep as the screening chatter of words in the mind. But presently all about her, as she sat poised for the length of the journey between the dead stillness within her and the noise of the silence without, a world most wonderful was dawning with strange irrelevance, forcing her attention to lift itself from the abyss of her fatigue. Look at us, the buildings seemed to say, sweeping by massed and various and whole, spangled with light. We are here. We, are the accomplished marvel. Buildings had always seemed marvellous; and in their moving, changing aspects an endless fascination, except in North London, where they huddled without distinction, defaced in feature and outline by a featureless blind occupancy. But tonight, it was North London that was revealing the marvel of the mere existence of a building. North Londoners were not under the spell; but it was there. Their buildings rising out of the earth where once there had been nothing, proclaimed it as they swept dreaming by, making roadways that were like long thoughts, meeting and crossing and going on and on, deep alleyways and little courts where always was a pool of light or darkness, pouring down from their secret communion with the sky a strange single reality upon the clothed and trooping multitude below. And all the strange unnoticed marvel of buildings and clothes, the even more marvellously strange unnoticed clothing of speech, all existing alone and independent outside the small existence of single lives and yet proclaiming them﻿ ﻿… an exclamation of wonder rose to her lips, and fell back checked, by the remembered occasion, to which for an instant she returned as a stranger seeing the two figures side by side chained in suspended explanations that would not set them free, and left her gazing again, surrendered, addressing herself with a deepening ease of heart to the endless friendly strength flowing from things unconsciously brought about. It brought a balm that lulled her almost to sleep, so that when at last their journey was at an end she found herself wordless and adrift in a tiresome pain, that must be removed only because it blotted out marvels.

===Chinese===
「看在老天的份上别碰我，」她不由自主地喊出来，继续朝前走，有他陪着，一面检视自己这声喊。它是对的。它怀着一种秘密的*知道*。他们乘电车、乘公共汽车，一路无言。下方灯光昏暗的人行道上人们移动着，是挣脱开、散落在夜的灰色里的影子。煤气灯照亮的一张张说着话的脸在街灯下相继而过；没有一张说出它的思想；没有一种感情被表达出来，深到哪怕及得上心里那层起遮蔽作用的词语絮谈。可是不多时，在她四周——她为整段旅程端坐着，悬在她体内死一般的静寂与体外沉默的喧嚣之间——一个无比奇妙的世界正带着古怪的不相干破晓，迫使她的注意力从她疲惫的深渊里抬升起来。看我们呀，那些楼房仿佛在说，成群地、多样地、完整地掠过，缀满了灯光。我们在这里。我们，是修成了的奇迹。楼房一向显得奇妙；在它们移动变换的诸貌里有一种无尽的魅惑——除了在北伦敦，那里它们挤作一团，毫无分别，眉目轮廓都被一种无面目的盲目占用毁坏了。可是今晚，偏偏是北伦敦在揭示一幢楼房仅仅存在着这件奇事。北伦敦人不在咒力之下；可咒力就在那里。他们的楼房从一度空无一物的地面上立起来，一边宣告着这奇事，一边载着他们梦一般掠过，把马路修成一条条长长的思想，相遇、交叉、绵延不断，深深的巷道和小小的院落，那里总有一泓光或一泓暗，从楼房与天空秘密的交融里，把一种奇异的单一真实，倾泻到下方衣着包裹、成群结队的大众身上。而这一切奇异的、未被注意的奇迹——楼房与衣裳，还有那更加奇异得奇妙、同样未被注意的衣裳——言语——全都独自而独立地存在着，在一条条单独生命的微小存在之外，却又宣告着它们……一声惊奇的呼叹升到她唇边，又咽了回去，被记起的那个场合拦住；有一瞬她像个陌生人回到那场合里，看见那两个并肩的身影，锁在悬而不决的解说里，那解说并不放他们自由；然后留下她再度凝望，降服了，怀着越来越安心的一颗心，把自己交给那从无心成就的事物里流出来的无尽的友善力量。它带来一剂香膏，哄得她几乎睡着，以致旅程终于到头时，她发觉自己无言地漂在一种恼人的疼痛里——那疼之所以必须除去，只因它抹掉了奇迹。

===Original===
He began at once, standing before her, relating in simple unbroken speech the story of his student days, without pleading or extenuation; waiting at the end for her judgment.

===Chinese===
他立刻开了口，站在她面前，用简单而不断续的言语，讲述他学生时代的故事，不求饶，不开脱；讲完了，等着她的判决。

===Original===
“And that first photograph that I liked, was before; and the other, after.”

“That is so.”

“In the first there is someone looking out through the eyes; in the other that someone has moved away.”

“That is so. I agree.”

“Well, can’t you see? Never to come back. Never to come back.”

“Miriam. Remember I am no more that man. I was in suffering and in ignorance. It would have been better otherwise. I agree with you. But that is all past. I am no more that man.”

“Can’t you see that there is no past?”

“I confess I do not understand this.”

“It is crowding all round you. I felt it. Don’t you remember? Before I knew. It comes between us all the time. I know now. It’s not an idea; or prudishness. It’s more solid than the space of air between us. I can’t get through it.”

===Chinese===
「我先前喜欢的那头一张照片，是在那之前照的；另一张，是在那之后。」

「是这样。」

「头一张里，有一个人从眼睛里朝外望着；另一张里，那个人已经走了。」

「是这样。我同意。」

「那么，你难道看不见吗？再也不会回来了。再也不会回来了。」

「米丽亚姆。记住，我已经不是那个人了。我那时在受苦，在无知之中。若是换一种样子本该更好。我同意你。可那一切都过去了。我已经不是那个人了。」

「你难道看不见，没有什么过去吗？」

「我承认我不明白这个。」

「它正四面八方地挤着你。我感觉到过它。你不记得吗？在我知道以前。它时时刻刻插在我们中间。我现在知道了。这不是一个念头；也不是假正经。它比我们之间这段空气还结实。我穿不过去。」

===Original===
“Remember I was suffering and *alone*.” Somewhere within the vibrating tones was the careless shouting of his boyhood; that past was there too; and the eager lifting voice of his earlier student days, still sometimes alive in the reverie of his lifted singing brows. The voice had been quelled. In his memory as he stood there before her was pain, young lonely pain. Within the life thrown open without reservation to her gaze, she saw, confronting her determination to make him suffer, the image of unhealed suffering, still there, half stifled by his blind obedience to worldly ignorant advice, but waiting for the moment to step forward and lay its burden upon her own unwilling heart, leaving him healed and free. Tears sprang to her eyes, blotting him out, and with them she sprang forth into a pathless darkness, conscious far away behind her, soon to be obliterated on the unknown shores opening ahead, but there gladly in hand, of a debt, signed and to be honoured even against her will, by life, surprised once more at this darkest moment, smiling at her secretly, behind all she could gather of opposing reason and clamourous protests of unworthiness. “Poor boy,” she gasped, gathering him as he sank to his knees, with swift enveloping hands against her breast. The unknown woman sat alone, with eyes wide open towards the empty air above his hidden face. This was man; leaning upon her with his burden of loneliness, at home and comforted. This was the truth behind the image of woman supported by man. The strong companion was a child seeking shelter; the woman’s share an awful loneliness. It was not fair.

===Chinese===
「记住，我那时在受苦，*孤身一人*。」在这震颤的音色深处某处，有他少年时代无所挂心的呼喊；那段过去也在那里；还有他早先学生时代那急切上扬的嗓音，至今有时还在他扬起的、歌唱着的眉宇的遐想里活着。那嗓音曾被压服。他站在她面前，他记忆里的是痛，年轻而孤独的痛。在这条毫不保留地向她的凝望敞开的生命里，她看见——正对着她要叫他受苦的决心——那个未愈之痛的形象，还在那里，被他对外间无知劝告的盲目服从半掩着，只等那一刻走上前来，把它的重负放上她自己不甘愿的心，留下他痊愈而自由。泪水涌上她的眼睛，把他抹掉了；她随着泪水一跃而出，跃进无路的黑暗，意识到身后远远的一件东西——它很快就会被前方展开的未知海岸抹去，此刻却乐于握在手中——那是一宗债，签了字的，哪怕违逆她的意愿也须偿付，由生活来偿付；生活在这最黑暗的时刻又一次吃了一惊，在她能召集的一切反对的理由和一连串不配的吵嚷抗议背后，朝她秘密地微笑。「可怜的孩子，」她喘着气说，在他跪下去的时候接住他，用一双飞快环拢的手，把他拢向自己的胸口。那个无名的女人独自坐着，眼睛大大地睁着，望着他埋下去的脸之上的空茫。这就是男人；带着他孤独的重负偎着她，安了家，得了安慰。这就是男人支撑女人的那个形象背后的真相。那强壮的伴侣是个寻庇护的孩子；女人的份是一种可怕的孤独。这不公平。

===Original===
She moved to raise and restore him, at least to the semblance of a supporting presence. But with a sudden movement he bent and caught a fold of her dress to his lips. She rose with a cry of protest, urging him to his feet.

===Chinese===
她动了动，想把他扶起来、还给他——至少还给他一副支撑者的样貌。可他猛地一俯身，抓住她裙子的一褶，印在唇上。她喊出一声抗议站起身，催他也站起来。

===Original===
“I know now,” he said simply, “why men kneel to women.” While in her heart she thanked heaven for preserving her to that hour, the dreadful words invested her in yet another loneliness. She seemed to stand tall and alone, isolated for a moment from her solid surroundings, within a spiral of unconsuming radiance.

===Chinese===
「我现在知道了，」他简单地说，「男人为什么向女人下跪。」她心里一面感谢上苍把她保全到这个时辰，那可怕的话语一面又给她披上又一重孤独。她仿佛高大地独自站着，有一瞬间从她坚实的环境里被隔绝出来，立在一柱不噬人的辉光的螺旋里。

===Original===
“No one ought to kneel to anyone,” she lied in pity, and moved out restlessly into the room. We are real. As others have been real. There is a sacred bond between us now, ratified by all human experience. But oh the cost and the demand. It was as if she were carrying in her hands something that could be kept safe only by a lifelong silence. Everything she did and said in future must hide the sacred trust. It gave a freedom; but not of speech or thought. It left the careless dreaming self behind. Only in ceaseless occupation could it hold its way. Its only confidant would be God. Holding to it, everything in life, even difficulties, would be transparent. But seen from the outside, by the world, an awful mysteriously persistent commonplace. It was not fair that men did not know the whole of this secret place and its compact. Why was God in league only with women?

===Chinese===
「谁也不该向谁下跪，」她出于怜悯撒了谎，随即不安地踱进屋子里去。我们是真实的。正如别人也曾真实过。我们之间如今有了一条神圣的纽带，由全部人类的经验批准。可是啊，这代价，这索求。仿佛她双手捧着一样东西，只有靠一生的沉默才能保它无恙。她今后的一言一行，都必须把这神圣的信托藏起来。它给了一种自由；却不是言语或思想的自由。它把那无忧无虑、爱做梦的自我撇在了后面。只有在不停歇的操劳里，它才能守得住自己的路。它唯一的知己会是上帝。守着它，生活里的一切，连艰难在内，都会变得透明。可从外面、从世界看来，却是一件可怕的、神秘地执拗的平凡事。男人们不知道这秘境与这契约的全部，这不公平。为什么上帝只与女人结盟？

## XII / XII

===Original===

It’s not altogether personal. … Until it is understood and admitted, there is a darkness everywhere. The life of every man in existence, who does not understand and admit it, is perfectly senseless. Until they know they are all living in vain.

===Chinese===

这并非全然是私人的事。……在它得到理解与承认之前，处处都是一片黑暗。世上每一个不曾理解它、承认它的人，其生命都全然无谓。在他们知道之前，他们全都活得枉然。

===Original===

“What on earth did you mean?” she said as soon as the omnibus had started.

He turned a startled musing face. He had *forgotten*.

“What have I said?”

“Kindly think.”

“Really I am at a loss.”

“When that woman collided with me, crossing the road.”

“Ah, ah, I remember. Well?”

“You pronounced an opinion.”

“It is not my opinion. It is a matter of ascertained fact.”

“Facts are invented by people who start with their conclusions arranged beforehand.”

“Perhaps so.”

“Ah well; that is an admission.”

“The conclusion is amply verified.”

“Where?”

“I speak only of women in the mass. There are of course exceptions.”

“Go on, go on.”

“I see you are annoyed. Let us leave this matter.”

“Kindly go on.”

“There is nothing more to say.” He *laughed*. He was not even being aware that it was a matter of life and death. He could go on serenely living in an idea, that turned life into a nightmare.

===Chinese===

「你到底是什么意思？」公共马车一开动，她就开了口。

他转过一张吃惊的、出神的脸。他已经*忘了*。

「我说了什么？」

「劳驾想想。」

「我实在摸不着头脑。」

「过马路的时候，那个女人撞上我的时候。」

「啊，啊，我想起来了。怎么样？」

「你发表了一个意见。」

「那不是我的意见。那是一件确证了的事实。」

「事实是那些先安排好结论的人发明出来的。」

「也许如此。」

「啊哈；这就算承认了。」

「这结论是充分验证过的。」

「在哪儿验证的？」

「我只说群体意义上的女人。当然是有例外的。」

「往下说，往下说。」

「我看你恼了。我们还是撇开这事吧。」

「劳驾往下说。」

「再没有什么可说的了。」他*笑了*。他甚至没有意识到，这是一件生死攸关的事。他可以安然继续活在一个观念里，那观念把生活变成了一场噩梦。

===Original===

“Oh if it amuses you.” He was silent. The moments went beating on. She turned from him and sat averted. She would go now onward and onward till she could get away over the edge of the world. There was nothing else to do. There were no thoughts or words in which her conviction could take shape. Even looking for them was a degradation. Besides, argument, if she could steady herself to face the pain of it, would not, whatever he might say, even dislodge his satisfied unconcern. He was uneasy; but only about herself, and would accept reassurance from her, without a single backward glance. But what did their personal fate matter beside a question so all-embracing? What future could they have in unacknowledged disagreement over central truth? And if it were acknowledged, what peace?

===Chinese===

「哦，要是这能逗你开心的话。」他沉默了。时辰一刻一刻敲打着过去。她从他那边转过身，侧身坐着。她如今要一路向前、再向前，直到能翻出世界的边缘脱身而去。此外再没有别的可做。没有任何念头或字句，能让她的确信在其中成形。连去寻找它们，都是一种自贬。况且，争辩——纵使她能稳住自己去抵挡它的痛——无论他说什么，也撼不动他那份心满意足的无动于衷。他不安；却只是为她而不安，并且会从她这里接受宽慰，一次也不回头。可面对一个如此包罗一切的问题，他们两人的私人命运又算得了什么？在核心真理上各怀分歧而互不承认，他们还能有什么将来？倘若承认了，又有什么安宁？

===Original===

The long corridor of London imprisoned her. Far away beneath her tumult it was making its appeal, renewing the immortal compact. The irregular façades, dull greys absorbing the light, bright buffs throwing it brilliantly out, dadoed below with a patchwork of shops, and overhead the crisscross of telephone wires, shut her away from the low-hung soft grey sky. But far away, unfailing, retreating as the long corridor telescoped towards them, an obliterating saffron haze filled the vista, holding her in her place.

===Chinese===

伦敦长长的走廊囚禁着她。在她的纷乱底下，它远远地发出着自己的召唤，续订着那不朽的契约。参差不齐的临街立面，暗灰的吞着光，亮土黄的把光灿灿地掷出，底下让七零八落的店铺拼缀出一道墙裙，头顶上电话线纵横交错，把她同那低垂柔和的灰天隔开。可远处，从不爽约，随着长廊朝它们节节套缩而一路退让，一片抹平一切的番红花色薄霭充满远景，把她牢牢按在她的位置上。

===Original===

The end of the journey brought them to grey streets and winding alleys where the masts and rigging that had loomed suddenly in the distance, robbing the expedition of its promise of ending in some strange remoteness with their suggestion of blind busy worlds beyond London, were lost to sight.

===Chinese===

旅程的尽头把他们带到灰色的街道和蜿蜒的巷陌里；先前远远隐现的桅杆与索具——它们暗示着伦敦之外还有盲目而忙碌的世界，夺走了此行终将在某个奇异的远僻之地收束的许诺——在这里已看不见了。

===Original===

“This must be the docks,” she said politely.

With the curt permission of a sentinel policeman they went through a gateway appearing suddenly before them in a high grey wall. Miriam hurried forward to meet the open scene for one moment alone and found herself on a little quay surrounding a square basin of motionless grey water shut in by wooden galleries, stacked with mouldering casks. But the air was the air that moves softly on still days over wide waters and in the shadowed light of the enclosure, the fringe of green where the water touched the grey stone of the quay gleamed brilliantly in the stillness. She breathed in, in spite of herself, the charm of the scene; an ordered completeness, left to itself in beauty; its lonely beauty to be gathered only by the chance passerby.

===Chinese===

「这想必就是船坞了。」她客客气气地说。

凭着一名站岗警察简短的许可，他们穿过一道大门，那门忽然出现在一堵高高的灰墙跟前。米丽亚姆抢步上前，要独自先同那豁然敞开的景致会上一面，却发现自己立在一座小小的码头上，环着一方滞滞的灰水船池，四围着木造的回廊，堆满朽坏的木桶。可这空气，正是无风的日子里在大片水面上轻轻拂动的那种空气；在这围场的荫翳光线里，水面挨着码头灰石的那一线绿缘，在静谧中灼灼生辉。她不由自主地，把这景致的魅力吸了进去；一种井然的圆满，独自留存于美之中；它那寂寞的美，唯有偶然路过的人方能拾得。

===Original===

“This is a strange romantic place,” said Mr. Shatov conversationally by her side.

“There is nothing,” said Miriam unwillingly, feeling her theme weaken as she looked away from it to voice well-known words, “*Nothing* that reveals more completely the spiritual,” her voice gave over the word which broke into meaninglessness upon the air, “the status of a man as his estimate of women.”

“I entirely agree. I was a feminist in my college days. I am still a feminist.”

===Chinese===

「这是个别致的浪漫地方。」沙托夫先生在她身旁，用闲谈的口气说。

「没有什么，」米丽亚姆不情愿地说，一面感到自己的题旨正弱下去，因为她把目光从它上面挪开，去说出那些尽人皆知的字句，「*没有什么*能更全然地揭示一个男人的精神——」她的嗓音把那个词放掉了，它在空气里碎成了无意义，「——境地了，凭的是他对女人的估量。」

「我完全同意。我在大学时代就是个女权主义者。现在也还是。」

===Original===

Miriam pondered. The word was new to her. But how could anyone be a feminist and still think women most certainly inferior beings?

===Chinese===

米丽亚姆沉吟。这个词她还是头一回听见。可一个人怎么能既做女权主义者，又依旧认为女人千真万确是低一等的存在呢？

===Original===

“Ah,” she cried, “you are one of the Huxleys.”

“I don’t follow you.”

“Oh well. *He*, impertinent schoolboy, graciously suggested that women should be given every possible kind of advantage, educational and otherwise; saying almost in the same breath that they could never reach the highest places in civilisation; that Nature’s Salic Law would never be repealed.”

“Well, how is it to be repealed?”

“I don’t know I’m sure. I’m not wise enough to give instruction in repealing a law that has never existed. But who is Huxley, that he should take upon himself to say what are the highest places in civilisation?”

===Chinese===

「啊，」她叫道，「你是赫胥黎（Huxley）他们那一伙的。」

「我没听懂你的意思。」

「哦，好吧。*他*，那个放肆的学童，恩赐似的建议给女人一切可能的好处，教育上的以及其他方面的；几乎同一口气里又说，她们永远登不上文明中的最高位置；说自然的萨利克法（Salic Law）永远不会被废除。」

「那么，它该怎么废除呢？」

「我确实不知道。我还没有聪明到能指点人怎么去废除一条从来不曾存在过的法律。可赫胥黎算什么人，凭什么由他来断定什么是文明中的最高位置？」

===Original===

“Miriam,” he said, coming round to stand before her. “We are not going to quarrel over this matter.” She refused to meet his eyes.

“It is not a question of quarrelling, or even discussion. You have told me all I want to know. I see exactly where you stand; and for my part it *decides*, many things. I don’t say this to amuse myself or because I want to, but because it is the only thing I can possibly do.”

“Miriam. In this spirit nothing can be said at all. Let us rather go and have tea.”

===Chinese===

「米丽亚姆，」他说，绕到她跟前站定。「我们不要为这件事吵架。」她不肯迎上他的眼睛。

「这不是吵不吵架的问题，连讨论都谈不上。你要我知道的你都告诉我了。你站在哪儿，我看得一清二楚；而在我这一面，它*决定*了许多事情。我说这话不是为了自遣，也不是因为我想说，而是因为这是我这儿唯一可能做的事。」

「米丽亚姆。抱着这种心绪，根本什么都谈不成。我们还是去喝杯茶吧。」

===Original===

Poor little man, perhaps he was weary; troubled in this strange grey corner of a country not his own, isolated with an unexpected anger. They had tea in a small dark room behind a little shop. It was close packed with an odorous dampness. Miriam sat frozen, appalled by the presence of a negro. He sat near by, huge, bent snorting and devouring, with a huge black bottle at his side. Mr. Shatov’s presence was shorn of its alien quality. He was an Englishman in the fact that he and she could *not* sit eating in the neighbourhood of this marshy jungle. But they were, they had. They would have. Once away from this awful place she would never think of it again. Yet the man had hands and needs and feelings. Perhaps he could sing. He was at a disadvantage, an outcast. There was something that ought to be said to him. She could not think what it was. In his oppressive presence it was impossible to think at all. Every time she sipped her bitter tea it seemed that before she should have replaced her cup, vengeance would have sprung from the dark corner. Everything hurried so. There was no *time* to shake off the sense of contamination. It *was* contamination. The man’s presence was an outrage on something of which he was not aware. It would be possible to make him aware. When his fearful face, which she sadly knew she could not bring herself to regard a second time, was out of sight, the outline of his head was desolate, like the contemplated head of any man alive. Men ought not to have faces. Their real selves abode in the expressions of their heads and brows. Below, their faces were moulded by deceit. …

===Chinese===

可怜的小个子男人，他也许是倦了；在这不属于他的国度的、灰暗陌生的角落里烦恼着，被一场意想不到的怒火孤立着。他们在一家小铺子后头一间幽暗的小屋里用了茶。屋里塞满了一股带味的潮气。米丽亚姆僵坐着，被一个黑人的在场骇住了。他坐在近旁，块头硕大，佝偻着，呼哧呼哧地大嚼大咽，手边立着一只黑色的大酒瓶。沙托夫先生的在场被削去了它的异质感。在这一层上他是个英国人——凭的是：他和她*不能够*坐在这片沼泽丛林近旁吃东西。可他们坐着呢，已经坐过了。往后也会坐的。一旦离开这个可怕的地方，她就再不会想起它。可这人也有手，有需要，有感觉。也许他会唱歌。他处于劣势，是个被逐的人。有一样话本来该对他说。她想不出是什么。在他那压人的在场里，根本没法思考。每啜一口苦茶，她都觉得还没等她把杯子放回原处，复仇就会从那幽暗的角落里蹿出来。一切都这样匆匆。没有*时间*把那沾染之感抖落。它*就是*沾染。这人的在场，冒犯着某样他并不自知的东西。要让他自知，是办得到的。等那张可怕的脸——她悲哀地知道自己无法强使自己再看它第二眼——从视野里消失时，他头颅的轮廓便显出荒凉，如同凝望世上任何一个活人的头颅。男人本不该有脸。他们真正的自我，栖居在头与眉的表情里。再往下，他们的脸是由欺诈塑成的。……

===Original===

While she had pursued her thoughts, advantage had fallen to the black form in the corner. It was as if the black face grinned, crushing her thread of thought.

“You see, Miriam, if instead of beating me, you will tell me your *thoughts*, it is quite possible that mine may be modified. There is at least nothing of the bigot in me.”

“It is not what people may be made to see for a few minutes in conversations that counts. It is the conclusions they come to, instinctively, by themselves.” He wanted to try and think as she did … “*chose attendrissante; il me ressemblaient*” … *life* … *was* different, to everybody, even to intellectual male vain-boasters, from everybody’s descriptions; there was nothing to point to anywhere that exactly corresponded to spoken opinions. But the relieving truth of this was only realised privately. The things went on being said. Men did not admit their private discoveries in public. It was not enough to see and force the admittance of the holes in a theory privately, and leave the form of words going on and on in the world perpetually parroted, infecting the sky. “Wise women know better and go their way without listening,” is not enough. It is not only the insult to women; a contempt for men is a bulwark against that, but introduces sourness into one’s own life. … It is the impossibility of witnessing the pouring on of a vast, repeating public life that is missing the significance of everything.

===Chinese===

在她追寻自己思绪的当儿，优势落到了角落里那具黑色的身形上。仿佛那张黑脸咧开笑了，碾碎了她思绪的线。

「你瞧，米丽亚姆，你要是不揍我，而是把你的*想法*讲给我听，我的想法是很可能因此得到修正的。我身上至少没有半点盲信者的气味。」

「要紧的，不是人们在交谈里被弄得看见几分钟的东西。要紧的，是他们本能地、独自一人得出的那些结论。」他想试着也照她那样去想……「chose attendrissante; il me ressemblaient」（动人的事；他与我相像）……*生活*……*是*不同的，对每个人都不同，甚至对那些有智识的、自负吹嘘的男人也不同，不同于任何人写下的描述；天下没有一样东西指得出来，恰好对得上口说的意见。可这层叫人宽解的真相，只被人私下里识得。那些话照旧被说着。男人们不肯当众承认自己私下的发现。光是私下看出一条理论里的窟窿、强逼自己认下来，却任那套字句在世上没完没了地被人鹦鹉学舌下去、感染着天空，这并不够。「聪明的女人更懂事，不听那一套，径自走她们的路」，这并不够。这不止是对女人的侮辱；对男人的蔑视固然是抵御它的壁垒，却把酸涩引进了自己的生活。……无法忍受的，是眼睁睁看着一场浩大的、周而复始的公共生活不住地倾泻下去，而它错失着万事万物的意义。

===Original===

Yet what a support, she thought with a sideways glance, was his own gentleness … *gentilesse* … and humanity, to his own theory. He was serene and open in the presence of this central bitterness. If she could summon, in words, convincing evidence of the inferiority of man, he would cheerfully accept it and go on unmaimed. But a private reconstruction of standards in agreement with one person would not bring healing. It was history, literature, the way of stating records, reports, stories, the whole method of statement of things from the beginning that was on a false foundation.

===Chinese===

然而，她斜眼一瞥，想道：他自身的那份温柔……温文（*gentilesse*）……与仁厚，对他自己的理论是多大的一个支撑。在这桩核心的苦涩跟前，他安然而敞亮。倘若她能用言辞召来男人低贱的可信证据，他会欣然领受，毫发无损地照旧过活。可是同单独一个人私下里重建标准，带不来疗愈。是历史，是文学，是陈述史册、报道、故事的方式，是开天辟地以来陈述事物的整套方法，坐落在一道虚假的地基上。

===Original===

If only one could speak as quickly as one’s thoughts flashed, and several thoughts together, all with a separate life of their own and yet belonging, everybody would be understood. As it was, even in the most favourable circumstances, people could hardly communicate with each other at all.

===Chinese===

但愿人能像思绪闪现那样快地说话，还能几个念头一并地说，个个都有各自独立的生命而又彼此归属，那样人人都能够被理解。而现实是，即便在最有利的情形之下，人们也几乎根本无法彼此沟通。

===Original===

“I have nothing to say. It is not a thing that can be argued out. Those women’s rights people are the worst of all. Because they think women have been ‘subject’ in the past. Women never have been subject. Never can be. The proof of this is the way men have always been puzzled and everlastingly trying fresh theories; founded *on* the very small experience of women any man is capable of having. Disabilities, imposed by law, are a stupid insult to women, but have never touched them as individuals. In the long run they injure only men. For they keep back the civilisation of the outside world, which is the only thing men can make. It is not everything. It is a sort of result, poor and shaky because the real inside civilisation of women, the one thing that has been in them from the first and is not in the natural man, not made by ‘things,’ is kept out of it. Women do not need civilisation. It is apt to bore them. *But* it can never rise above their level. They keep it back. That does not matter, to themselves. But it matters to men. And if they want their old civilisation to be anything but a dreary-weary puzzle, they must leave off imagining themselves a race of gods fighting against chaos, and thinking of women as part of the chaos they have to civilise. There isn’t any ‘chaos.’ Never has been. It’s the principal masculine illusion. It is not a truth to say that women must be civilised. Feminists are not only an insult to womanhood. They are a libel on the universe.” In the awful presence she had spoken herself out, found and recited her best most liberating words. The little unseen room shone, its shining speaking up to her from small things immediately under her eyes. Light, pouring from her speech, sent a radiance about the thick black head and its monstrous bronze face. He might have his thoughts, might even look them, from the utmost abyss of crude male life, but he had helped her, and his blind unconscious outlines shared the unknown glory. But she doubted if she would remember that thoughts flowed more easily, with surprising ease, as if given, waiting, ready to be scanned and stated, when one’s eyes ceased to look outwards. If she could remember it, it might prove to be the solution of social life.

===Chinese===

「我没有什么可说的。这不是一件能辩出个结果来的事。那些争女权的人是最糟糕的。因为他们以为女人过去曾经『从属』过。女人从来不曾从属过。永远也不会。证明便是：男人们始终困惑不解、没完没了地搬出新理论；而那些理论，*建基*于任何一个男人所能有的、对女人极其微末的经验。法律强加的种种限制，是对女人愚蠢的侮辱，却从未触及她们之为个体。到头来，它们只伤害男人。因为它们拖住了外部世界的文明——那是男人唯一造得出来的东西。它并非一切。它只是一种结果，贫乏而摇晃，因为女人真正内在的文明——那从一开头就在她们体内、不曾存在于自然状态的男人身上、不由『外物』造出来的唯一之物——被排除在它外面。女人不需要文明。文明容易叫她们厌倦。*可*文明永远升不到她们的水准之上。是她们拖住了它。这对她们自己，无关紧要。可对男人们，要紧。男人们若不想让他们那套旧文明沦为一场阴郁倦人的谜局，就必须别再把自己想象成与混沌作战的一族神明，别再把女人当作他们必须开化的混沌的一部分。根本没有什么『混沌』。从来不曾有过。那是男人头一号的幻觉。说女人必须被开化，不是一句真理。女权主义者不仅是对女性气质的侮辱。他们是对宇宙的诽谤。」在这骇人的在场跟前，她把满腹的话说尽了，找到并诵出了她最好、最解放的字句。这间无人看见的小屋亮了起来，它的光亮从她眼皮底下那些小物件上向她诉说。光，从她的话语里倾泻出来，在那颗浓黑的头颅和那张古铜色怪脸的四周铺出一圈光晕。他可以有他的念头，甚至可以从粗粝男性生活的极深之渊里把它们望出来，可他帮了她，而他那盲然无觉的轮廓分享着这无名的荣光。只是她怀疑自己记不记得住：当一个人的眼睛不再向外看时，思绪会流得更畅，畅得惊人，仿佛是现成给定的，等在那儿，随时可供检视与陈述。倘若她能记住，这或许会被证明是社会生活的解法。

===Original===

“These things are all matters of opinion. Whereas it is a matter of indisputable *fact* that in the past women have been subject.”

“If you believe that it is impossible for us to associate. Because we are living in two utterly different worlds.”

“On the contrary. This difference is a most excellent basis for association.”

“You think I can cheerfully regard myself as an emancipated slave, with traditions of slavery for memory and the form of a slave as an everlasting heritage?”

“Remember that heredity is crosswise. You are probably more the daughter of your father …”

“*That* won’t help you, thank you. If anything I am my mother’s son.”

===Chinese===

「这些都是见仁见智之事。而女人在过去曾经从属，却是一件不容置疑的*事实*。」

「你若信这个，我们便不可能相与。因为我们活在两个截然不同的世界里。」

「恰恰相反。这差异恰是相与的再好不过的基础。」

「你以为我能欢欢喜喜地把自己看成一个得了解放的奴隶，拿奴役的传统充当记忆，拿奴隶的形骸充当一份永恒的遗产么？」

「要记得，遗传是交叉的。你多半更像你父亲的女儿……」

「*那*可帮不上你的忙，多谢。真要说，我是我母亲的儿子。」

===Original===

“Ah—ah, what is this, you are a *son*. Do you see?”

“That’s a piece of English feudalism.”

“The demands of feudalism do not explain a woman’s desire for sons.”

“That is another question. She hopes *they* will give her the understanding she never had from their father. In that I *am* my mother’s son forever. If there’s a future life, all I care for is to meet *her*. If I could have her back for ten minutes I would gladly give up the rest of my life. … Is heredity really crisscross? Is it proved?”

“Substantially.”

“Oh yes. Of course. I know. To prevent civilisation going ahead too fast! I’ve seen that somewhere. Very flattering to men. But it proves there’s no separate race of men and women.”

“Exactly.”

“Then how have men the face to go on with their generalisations about women?”

“You yourself have a generalisation about women.”

“That’s different. It’s not about brains and attainments. I can’t make you see. I suppose it’s Christianity.”

===Chinese===

「啊——啊，这是怎么说的，你竟是个*儿子*呢。你明白么？」

「这是英国封建主义的一小块。」

「封建主义的种种要求，解释不了女人对儿子的渴望。」

「那是另一个问题。她指望*他们*能给她那份从他们父亲那里从不曾得到的理解。在这一层上，我永远*是*我母亲的儿子。倘若有来生，我在乎的只是见到*她*。倘若能换她回来十分钟，我甘愿舍掉我的余生。……遗传当真是纵横交叉的么？证实过么？」

「大体上证实了。」

「哦是的。当然。我知道。为的是不让文明跑得太快！这话我在哪儿见到过。对男人真是抬举得很。可这恰恰证明了，并没有彼此分立的男人的种族与女人的种族。」

「正是。」

「那么男人还有什么脸面，继续他们关于女人的以偏概全？」

「你自己就有一个关于女人的以偏概全。」

「那不一样。那与头脑和造诣无关。我没法叫你看见。我想，那是基督教吧。」

===Original===

“What is Christianity? You think Christianity is favourable to women? On the contrary. It is the Christian countries that have produced the prostitute and the most vile estimations of women in the world. It is only in Christian countries that I find the detestable spectacle of men who will go straight from association with loose women into the society of innocent girls. That I find unthinkable. … With Jews womanhood has always been sacred. And there can be no doubt that we owe our persistence as a race largely to our laws of protection for women; *all* women. Moreover in the older Hebrew civilisation women stood very high. You may read this. Today there is a very significant Jewish wit which says that women make the best wives and mothers in the world.”

===Chinese===

「基督教是什么？你以为基督教于女人有利？恰恰相反。正是基督教的国家产出了娼妓，产出了世上对女人最卑污的评价。也唯独在基督教国家里，我见到那可憎的景象：男人们前脚刚离开放荡女人的相伴，后脚便直闯无辜少女的社会。这在我看是不可想象的。……在犹太人这里，女性素来是神圣的。而且毫无疑问，我们之能作为一个族类绵延存续，很大程度上要归功于我们保护女人的律法；保护*一切*女人。再者，在更古老的希伯来文明里，女人的地位极高。这一点你们可以去读到。今天有一句很有意味的犹太妙语，说女人是天底下最好的妻子和母亲。」

===Original===

“There you are. No Englishman would make a joke like that.”

“Because he is a hypocrite.”

“No. He may, as you say, think one thing and say another; but long long ago he had a jog. It *was* Christianity. Something happened. Christ was the first man to see women as individuals.”

===Chinese===

「你瞧。没有哪个英国人会开这样的玩笑。」

「因为他是伪君子。」

「不。他或许如你所说，想的是一回事，说的是另一回事；可是很久很久以前，他得过一次点拨。那*正是*基督教。有些事情发生过。基督是头一个把女人看做个体的男人。」

===Original===

“You speak easily of Christianity. There is no Christianity in the world. It has never been imagined, save in the brain of a Tolstoy. And he has shown that if the principles of Christianity were applied, civilisation as we know it would at once come to an end.”

===Chinese===

「你说起基督教来好不轻巧。世上并没有基督教。它从来不曾被想象出来过，除了在托尔斯泰（Tolstoy）一个人的头脑里。而他已经表明：基督教的各项原则一旦付诸实行，我们所知的文明顷刻便会完结。」

===Original===

“There may not be much Christianity. But Christianity has made a difference. It has not given things to women that were not there before. Nothing can do that. But it has shed a light on them *which* the best women run away from. Never imagine I am speaking of myself. I’m as much a man as a woman. That’s why I can’t help seeing things. But I’m not really interested. Not inside myself. Now look here. You prefer Englishwomen to Jewesses. I can’t bear Jewesses, not because they are not really like other women, but because they reflect the limitations of the Jewish male. They talk and think the Jewish man’s idea of them. It has nothing to do with them as individuals. But they are waiting for the light to go up.”

===Chinese===

「基督教也许所剩无多。可基督教毕竟造成了不同。它不曾把女人先前没有的东西给她们。没有什么能做到这个。可它在她们身上投下了一束光，*这束光*偏偏是最好的女人们要逃开的。千万别以为我说的是我自己。我身上的男人和女人一样多。正因如此，我免不了看见这些事。可我并不真有兴趣。在我自己的内里，没有。现在你听着。比起犹太女子，你更偏爱英国女人。我受不了犹太女子，不是因为她们真的不像别的女人，而是因为她们映照出犹太男人的局限。她们说出的、想出的，都是犹太男人对她们的观念。这与她们作为个体毫无干系。可她们在等着光亮起来。」

===Original===

“I speak always of these assimilated and half-assimilated English Jewesses. Certainly to me they are most inimical.”

“More so than the Germans?”

“In a different way. They have here less social disabilities. But they are most absolutely terre-à-terre.”

===Chinese===

「我说的始终是这些同化了和半同化了的英国犹太女子。对我说来，她们无疑最怀敌意。」

「比德国的还要敌对？」

「是另一种方式的敌对。她们在此地较少社交上的限制。可她们彻头彻尾是 terre-à-terre（只讲实际、毫无理想）。」

===Original===

“Why are Russian Jewesses different?”

“Many of them are idealist. Many live altogether by one or two ideas of Tolstoy.”

“Why do you smile condescendingly?”

“These ideas can lead only to revolution. I am not a revolutionary. While I admire everywhere those who suffer for their ideals.”

===Chinese===

「为什么俄国的犹太女子就不同？」

「她们中有许多是理想主义者。有许多人全然靠着托尔斯泰的一两个观念生活。」

「你为什么带着优越感微笑？」

「这些观念只能通向革命。我不是革命者。不过，对那些为理想受苦的人，我处处心怀敬佩。」

===Original===

“You admit that Tolstoy has influenced Russian Jewesses. *He* got his ideas from Christ. So you say. I did not know he was religious.”

“It is a later development. But you remember Levin. But tell me, do you not consider that wife and mother is the highest position of woman?”

“It is neither high nor low. It may be anything. If you define life for women, as husbands and children, it means that you have no consciousness at all where women are concerned.”

“There is the evidence of women themselves. The majority find their whole life in these things.”

“That is a description, from outside, by men. When women use it they do not know what they say.”

===Chinese===

「你承认托尔斯泰影响了俄国的犹太女子。*他*的观念得自基督。这是你说的。我原先不知道他有宗教信仰。」

「那是他后来的发展。可你总记得列文（Levin）吧。但请告诉我：你不认为妻子与母亲是女人的最高位置么？」

「它既不高，也不低。它什么都可以是。你若把女人的生活界定为丈夫和孩子，那便意味着：凡涉及女人之处，你根本没有半点意识。」

「有女人自己的证言。大多数女人正是在这些东西里找到她们的整个生命。」

「那是男人从外面做出的一种描述。女人自己用起这话来，并不知道自己在说什么。」

## XIII / XIII

===Original===
It was strange that it should be the house that had always caught her eye, as she crossed the square; one of the spots that always made the years of her London life show as a continuous communion with the rich brightness of the west-end. The houses round about it were part of the darker colour of London, creating even in sunlight the beloved familiar London atmosphere of dun-coloured mist and grime. But this house was a brilliant white, its windows fringed, during the season, with the gentle deep velvet pink of ivy-leaf geraniums and having, across the lower half of its façade, a fine close trellis of green painted wood, up which a green creeper clambered, neat and sturdy, with small bright polished leaves making a woodland blur across the diamond patterned mesh of white and green. There were other creepers in the square, but they hung in festoons, easily shabby, spoiled at their brightest by the thought of their stringy bare tendrils hung with shrivelled leaves. These small green leaves faded and dried and fell crisply, leaving a network of clean twigs to gleam in the rain, and the trellis bright green against the white house-front, suggesting summer all the year around.

===Chinese===
真怪，偏偏是这座房子，每逢她穿过广场，总会攫住她的目光；它属于那样一些地点——总让她伦敦生活的经年累月，显现为同西区富丽明亮的一场绵延不绝的共融。它四围的宅子是伦敦较深色调的一部分，即便在阳光下，也营造着那可爱而熟悉的伦敦氛围——暗褐色的雾霭与尘垢。可这座房子是一片灿然的白，到了当令时节，窗沿镶着藤叶天竺葵柔和的深丝绒粉红，临街立面的下半幅覆着一面细密的绿漆木格架，一株绿色的攀藤攀援而上，齐整而茁壮，细小锃亮的叶子在白绿相间的菱形网格上晕开一片林间似的迷蒙。广场上另有几株攀藤，却都垂成一道道花彩，动辄显出寒酸，最葱茏时也叫人败兴——一想到它们那多筋光秃的卷须上挂着枯萎的叶子。而这一株的小绿叶子褪色、干枯、爽脆地落下，留下一副洁净的细枝网络在雨中闪光，那格架鲜亮地绿着，衬在白色屋面前，叫人觉得一年到头都是夏天。

===Original===
She went eagerly towards this permanent summer created by wealth, warmed by the imagined voice of a power that could transform all difficulties, setting them in a beauty that lived by itself.

===Chinese===
她热切地朝这片由财富造就的永恒夏天走去，被一个想象出来的声音暖着——那是一种权能的声音，能把一切艰难化开，将它们安放进一种自为其生的美里。

===Original===
The little leaves, seen from the doorstep, shone like bright enamel in the misty twilight; but their beautiful wild clean-cut shapes, so near, suddenly seemed helpless, unable to escape, forced to drape the walls, life-fevered within, to which their stems were pinned. … But there was a coming in and out. … All people in houses had a coming in and out, those moments of coming, anew out into endless space. And everywhere at moments, in houses, was the sense of the life of the whole world flowing in. Even Jewish houses were porous to the life of the world, and to have a house, however strangely shaped one’s life, would be to have a vantage point for breathing in the life of the world. … She stood in a lull, reprieved, her endlessly revolving problem left behind, the future in abeyance, perhaps to be shown her by the woman waiting within, set in surroundings that now called to her jubilantly, proclaiming themselves to be the only object of her visit. For a moment she found herself back in her old sense of the marvel of existence, gazing at the miraculous spectacle of people and things, existing; herself, however, perplexed and resourceless, within it, everything sinking into insignificance beside the fact of being alive, having lived on to another moment of unexplainable glorious happiness. Light-heartedly she rang the bell. The small movement of her lifted hand was supported, a permitted part of the whole tremendous panorama; and in that whole she was *England*, a link in the worldwide being of England and English life. The bell, grinding out its summons within the house, brought her back within the limits of the occasion, but she could not drive away the desire to go forward without return, claiming welcome and acceptance, in a life permanently set in beauty.

===Chinese===
从门阶上望去，那些小叶子在雾蒙蒙的暮色里，亮得像鲜亮的珐琅；可它们那美丽、野性、轮廓分明的形状，这样近，忽然显得无助，逃脱不得，被迫披覆在墙面上——墙的内里为生命发起热来——它们的茎便被钉在这墙上。……可这里有一种进进出出。……凡是住房子的人都有一种进进出出，那些走出来的时刻，重新走进无尽的空间。而各处的房子里，时时都有整个世界的生命流进来的感觉。连犹太人的宅子，对世界的生活也是可渗透的；拥有一座房子，无论一个人的生活被塑成怎样奇怪的形状，便是拥有一个据点，可以呼吸进世界的生活。……她驻留在一阵平息里，得了缓刑，她那无休止盘旋的问题被留在身后，未来悬而未决，也许会由屋里等着的那位女子指点给她；她置身于此刻正朝她欢声召唤的环境里，它们宣布自己才是她此行唯一的目的。有一刻，她发觉自己回到了旧日那种对存在之奇迹的感受里，凝望着人与物存在着的奇观；而她自己，尽管困惑而无援，也在其中；一切都在「活着」这一事实旁边，沉入微不足道——她又活到了另一个无从解释的、辉煌的幸福时刻。她轻快地按了铃。她抬起的手那小小的动作是被承托着的，是那整幅浩大全景里被许可的一部分；而在那个整体里，她就是*英国*，是英国与英国生活那遍布寰球的存在中的一环。铃，在宅子里磨出它的传唤，把她带回到此刻的限度之内，可她驱不散那个愿望：一往向前、不再回返，索要欢迎与接纳，在一种被永久安放于美里的生活之中。

===Original===
The door flew open revealing a tall resentfully handsome butler past whom she went confidently announcing her appointment, into an immense hall, its distances leading in every direction to doors, suggesting a variety of interiors beyond her experience. She was left standing. Someone who had come up the steps as the door opened, was being swiftly conveyed, a short squat polished wealthy old English Jew with curly grey hair and an eager busy plunging gait, across the hall to the centremost door. It opened on a murmur of voices and the light from within fell upon a table just outside, its surface crowded with gleaming top-hats. Some kind of men’s meeting was in progress. The woman was not in it. … Had she anticipated, before she married, what it would be, however she might fortify herself with scorn, to breathe always the atmosphere of the Jewish religious and social oblivion of women? Had she had any experience of Jewesses, their sultry conscious femineity, their dreadful acceptance of being admitted to synagogue on sufferance, crowded away upstairs in a stuffy gallery, while the men downstairs, bathed in light, draped in the symbolic shawl, thanked God aloud for making them men and not women? Had she thought what it must be to have always at her side a Jewish consciousness, unconscious of her actuality, believing in its own positive existence, seeing her as human only in her consecration to relationships?

===Chinese===
门霍地开了，露出一个高个的、含着忿色的英俊管家；她自信地从他身边走过，报上自己的约会，走进一座巨大的厅堂，它的纵深朝着四面八方通向一道道门，暗示着种种超出她经验的室内天地。她被留在那儿站着。开门时上台阶来的某个人，正被匆匆引送着穿过厅堂，朝正中那道门去——一个矮壮敦实、油光水滑、富有的英国老犹太人，灰色鬈发，一副急切忙碌、一往直前的步态。那门开在一阵人语的低鸣上，里面的灯光落在门外近旁一张桌子上，桌面挤满了锃亮的大礼帽。某种男人们的聚会正在进行。那女子不在其中。……她婚前可曾料到，那会是什么滋味——无论她怎样用轻蔑把自己武装起来——永远呼吸道的是犹太人那种宗教与社交上对女人的遗忘？她可曾领教过犹太女子，她们那种湿热而自觉的女性气质，她们那可怕的接受：被勉强获准进入犹太会堂，塞到楼上间气闷人的楼廊里，而楼下的男人们浸在光里，披着象征性的祈祷披巾，高声感谢上帝使他们生为男人而非女人？她可曾想过，身边永远携着一种犹太意识会是什么滋味——那意识对她的实际存在浑然不觉，只信着自己确凿的存在，唯有在她献身于种种关系时，才把她看做一个人？

===Original===
The returning butler ushered her unannounced through a doorway near at hand into a room that spread dimly about her in a twilight deepened by a single core of rosy light at the centre of the expanse.

===Chinese===
返回的管家引着她，未经通传，穿过手边一道门洞，进了一个房间；那房间昏昏地在她四周铺展开一片暮色，因那空阔正中一团玫瑰色的光芯，而愈发深沉。

===Original===
Mrs. Bergstein had risen to meet her, her head obscured in the gloom above the lamplight, so that only her gown met Miriam’s first sally of investigation; a refined middle-class gown of thin dull black whose elbow sleeves and little vee neck were softened at the edge with a ruche of tulle; the party dress of a middle-aged spinster schoolmistress. Miriam braced herself in vain against its seductions; it called her so powerfully to come forth and rejoice. She revelled off, licensed and permitted, the free deputy of this chained presence, amongst the enchantments of the great house; the joy of her escapade leaping bright against the dark certainty that there was no help awaiting her. It was no longer to be feared that an unscrupulous, successful, brightly cajoling woman would persuade her that her problem did not exist; but neither from this woman to whom the fact of life as a thing in itself never had time to appear, could she hope for support in her own belief in the unsoundness of compromise.

===Chinese===
贝格斯坦太太已起身迎她，她的头隐没在灯光上方的幽暗里，于是只有她的裙衫，来接米丽亚姆第一番窥探的出击；一件精致的中产阶级裙衫，薄薄的暗黑色，肘袖和小小的V形领口在边缘上镶了一圈绢网抽褶，添了几分柔化；一件中年独身女教师的赴会裙衫。米丽亚姆枉自振作，抵挡它的诱惑；它这样有力地唤她出来、去欢庆。她纵情畅游开去，领了特许、得了许可，做这被锁链缚住的身影的自由代表，在那巨宅的诸般迷人处之间；她这趟偷欢的雀跃明亮地跳跃着，衬着那桩阴郁的确知：没有援手在等她。不必再怕一个不择手段、春风得意、满脸光彩地哄人的女人，会说服她相信她的问题并不存在；可也休想从这个女人这里指望到支持——对这样一个女人，生命作为自在之物的这一事实，从来不曾有时间显现——支持她自己对妥协之不健全的信念。

===Original===
Mrs. Bergstein bowed, murmured a greeting and indicated a little settee near the low chair into which she immediately subsided, her face still in shadow, the shape of her coiffure so much in keeping with the dress that Miriam could hardly refrain from departing then and there. She sat down, a schoolgirl waiting for judgment against which she was armed in advance, and yet helpless through her unenvious, scornful admiration.

===Chinese===
贝格斯坦太太躬了躬身，低声说了句问候，指了指矮椅旁一张小小的长榻，自己随即陷进那矮椅里，脸仍隐在暗处；她那发式的形状同这裙衫这样相称，以致米丽亚姆几乎按捺不住，想当场就走。她坐下了，一个等着宣判的女学生，事先已武装好对付那判决，却仍旧无助——因着她那不带嫉妒的、含着轻蔑的钦佩。

===Original===
“I was much interested by your letter,” said Mrs. Bergstein.

The interview was at an end. There was no opening in the smooth close surface represented by the voice, through which questions could be driven home. She was smitten into silence where the sound of the voice echoed and reechoed, whilst she fumbled for a suitable phrase, clinging to the memory of the statement, still somewhere, which she had come, so desperately, to hear and carry away and set down, a ray of light in the darkness of her revolving thoughts. A numb forgetfulness assailed her, threatening the disaster of irrelevance of speech or behaviour coming from the tides of expression she felt beating below it. She forced a murmured response from her lips, and the tumult was stilled to an echo that flung itself to and fro within, answering the echo of the woman’s voice on the air. She had caught hold and contributed. It was now the turn of the other to go on and confirm what she had revealed. …

===Chinese===
「我对你的信很感兴趣。」贝格斯坦太太说。

会谈已告终结。这声音所代表的那平滑致密的表面上，没有一道裂口，可以把问题敲进要害。她被击入沉默，那声音的音响在沉默里回响又回响，而她摸索着一个合适的字句，紧抓着那段陈述的记忆——它还在某处——她正是为了它，这样不顾死活地赶来，要听它、带走它、记下它，做她盘旋思绪的黑暗里的一线光。一阵麻木的遗忘袭上她来，威胁着一场灾祸：说出不相干的话、做出不相干的事，从她在它底下感到搏动的那些表达的潮水里冒出来。她从唇间逼出一句含混的应答，骚动便平息成一道回声，在心里来回掷荡，应和着那女子嗓音在空气里的回声。她抓住了，也投上了一分。现在轮到另一位接着往下，证实她已揭示的东西。……

===Original===
“Music is so *beautiful*—so *elevating*.” “That depends upon the music.” Never said. Kept treacherously back for the sake of things that might be lost in a clashing of opinions … the things they never thought of in exercising their benevolence, and demanding in return acceptance of their views … the light of a whole world condensed in the bright old town, the sweet chiming sound of it, coming in at the windows, restoring childhood, the expanses of leisure made by their small hard circle, a world of thoughtless ideas, turning a short weekend into a life, lived before, familiar, building out in the nerves a glorious vitality. …

It was the same voice, the English lady’s voice, bringing all Christendom about her, all the traditions within which, so lately, she had felt herself committed steadfastly to tread. But there was something left out of it, a warmth was missing, it had not in it the glow that was in those other women’s voices, of kindliness towards the generous things they had secretly, willingly renounced. It had, instead, something that was like a cold clean blade thrusting into an intelligible future, something inexorable, founded not upon fixed ideas, but upon ideas, single and cold. This woman would not make concessions; she would always stand, uncompromisingly, in face of everyone, men and women, for the same things, clear cut, delicate and narrowly determining as her voice.

===Chinese===
「音乐是这样*美*——是这样*令人升华*。」「那得看是什么音乐。」从未说出口。被不忠地按住不说，为了那些可能在意见相撞中失落的东西……那些东西，他们在施行仁慈时从不想到，却要求以接受他们的见解作回报……整个世界的光，凝结在那座明亮的老城里，它那甜美鸣响的声息，从窗子里进来，恢复童年，他们那小小坚硬的圈子造出的闲暇的旷野，一个由不假思索的观念组成的世界，把一个短短的周末变成一段生活——一段先前活过的、熟悉的生活——在神经里向外筑起一股辉煌的元气。……

这是同一种嗓音，英国女士的嗓音，把整个基督教世界聚拢到她身边，把那些传统全部聚拢来——直到不久之前，她还感到自己已受命于其中、要坚定地行走。可它里面漏掉了什么，缺了一种温暖，它没有那种光——在那些别的女人嗓音里的光，对那些慷慨之物的温厚，那些她们私下里、心甘情愿舍弃了的。它所有的，反倒是一种东西，像一柄冰冷洁净的刀，楔进一个可以理喻的未来，某种不容宽贷的东西，根基不是固守的意见，而是一条条意见，孤单而冰冷。这个女人不会让步；她将永远站着，不妥协地，面对每一个人，男人和女人，为了同样的东西，像她的嗓音一样，轮廓分明、精致，又狭隘地定夺一切。

===Original===
“You are considering the possibility of embracing the Jewish faith?”

“Well, *no*,” said Miriam startled into briskness by the too quickly developing accumulation of speech. “I heard that you had done so; and wondered, how it was possible, for an Englishwoman.”

“You are a Christian?”

“I don’t know. I was brought up in the Anglican Church.”

“Much depends upon the standpoint from which one approaches the very definite and simple creed of Judaism. I myself was a Unitarian, and therefore able to take the step without making a break with my earlier convictions.”

===Chinese===
「你正在考虑皈依犹太教的可能性？」

「哦，*不*，」米丽亚姆说，被这进展得太快、层层堆积的话语惊得利落起来。「我听说你这样做了；于是纳闷，这怎么可能，对一个英国女人。」

「你是基督徒么？」

「我不知道。我是在英国国教里长大的。」

「很多取决于一个人从什么立场去接近犹太教那非常明确而简单的信条。我自己原是唯一神格派（Unitarian）教徒，因此能走出这一步，而不与我早年的信念决裂。」

===Original===
“I see,” said Miriam coldly. Fate had deceived her, holding in reserve the trick of this simple explanation. She gazed at the seated figure. The glow of her surroundings was quenched by the chill of a perpetually active *reason*. … Science, ethics, withering common sense playing over everything in life, making a harsh bareness everywhere, seeing nothing alive but the cold processes of the human mind; having Tennyson read at services because poetry was one of the superior things produced by humanity. … She wondered whether this woman, so exactly prepared to meet a Jewish reform movement, had been helplessly born into Unitarianism, or had taken it up as she herself had nearly done.

===Chinese===
「我明白了。」米丽亚姆冷冷地说。命运骗了她，把这张简单解释的牌扣在了手里。她凝望那个坐着的身影。她周遭的光辉，被一种永远在运转的*理性*的寒气浇灭了。……科学，伦理，凋萎一切的常识拂过生活里的每样东西，到处造成一种粗粝的赤裸，除了人类头脑那些冰冷的过程，看不见任何活物；礼拜时让人诵读丁尼生，因为诗歌是人类产出的高级事物之一。……她纳闷，这个女人，这样分毫不差地预备好去迎对一个犹太教改革运动，究竟是无助地生入了唯一神格派，还是像她自己险些做过的那样，是自己拾起了它。

===Original===
“Much of course depends upon the synagogue through which one is admitted.” Ah; she *had* felt the impossibilities. She had compromised and was excusing her compromise.

“Of course I have heard of the reform movement.” … The silence quivered with the assertion that the reformers were as much cut off from Judaism as Unitarianism from Anglican Christianity. To enter a synagogue that made special arrangements for the recognition of women was to admit that women were dependent on recognition. The silence admitted the dilemma. Mrs. Bergstein had passed through these thoughts, suffering? Though she had found a way through, following her cold clear reason, she still suffered?

===Chinese===
「当然，很多还取决于一个人经由哪座犹太会堂被接纳。」啊；她*是*感到过那些不可能的。她妥协过，此刻正在为她的妥协辩白。

「我当然听说过改革运动。」……沉默里颤动着这样一个断言：改革者们与犹太教的隔绝，一如唯一神格派与英国国教基督教的隔绝。走进一座为承认女人而作了特别安排的犹太会堂，便是承认女人依赖于承认。这沉默承认了那道两难。贝格斯坦太太也曾穿过这些思绪，受着苦么？尽管她循着她那冰冷清晰的理性找到了一条穿过去的路，她仍在受苦么？

===Original===
“I think I should find it impossible to associate with Jewish *women*.”

“*That* is a point you must consider very carefully indeed.” The room leapt into glowing reality. They *were* at one; Englishwomen with a common incommunicable sense. Outcasts. … Far away, within the warm magic circle of English life, sounded the careless easy slipshod voices of Englishmen, she saw their averted talking forms, aware in every line, and protective, of something that Englishwomen held in their hands.

===Chinese===
「我想我会发现，同犹太*女人们*相与是不可能的。」

「*那*正是你必须十分仔细考虑的一点。」房间跃入了灼灼的现实。她们*确是*一体的；英国女人们，怀着一种共同的、无从言传的识见。被放逐的人们。……远远地，在英国生活那温暖的魔圈之内，响起英国男人们漫不经心、轻松散漫的嗓音；她看见他们侧过身去交谈的身影，浑身的线条都意识着、并且卫护着一样什么东西——英国女人们握在手里的东西。

===Original===
“Don’t you find,” she began breathlessly, but calm even tones drove across her eagerness: “What is your fiancé’s attitude towards religion?”

“He is not exactly religious and not fully in sympathy with the reform movement because he is a Zionist and thinks that the old ritual is the only link between the persecuted Jews and those who are better placed; that it would be treachery to break with it as long as any are persecuted. … Nevertheless, he is willing to renounce his Judaism.”

===Chinese===
「你不觉得么，」她气息急促地开了口，但平静匀整的声调横扫过她的热切：「你的未婚夫对宗教持什么态度？」

「他算不上虔信宗教，也不完全同情改革运动，因为他是个犹太复国主义者，认为古老的仪礼是受迫害的犹太人与那些境遇较好者之间唯一的纽带；认为只要还有人受着迫害，与它决裂便是背信。……尽管如此，他甘愿舍弃他的犹太教。」

===Original===
The Queen, who is religious, puts love before religion, for woman. Her Protestantism. He for God only, she for God in him and able to change her creed when she marries. A Catholic *couldn’t*. And she would call Catholics idolators. *She* is an idolator; of men.

Mrs. Bergstein was amazed at his willingness. Envious… *I am a Jew, a “head” man incapable of “love.”… It is your eyes. I must see them always.… I know now what is meant by love.… I am even willing to renounce my Judaism.… Michael* to think and say that. I am crowned, for life; by a sacrifice I cannot accept. He must keep his Judaism.… You *must* marry me.… The discovery, flowing through the grey noisy street, of the secret of the “mastery” idea; that women can only be sure that a man is sure when—

===Chinese===
那位女王，本自虔信宗教，却为了女人，把爱情置于宗教之前。她的新教。他只为上帝，她为他身上的上帝，出嫁时便可以改换自己的信条。天主教徒*不能*。而她还会把天主教徒称做拜偶像者。*她*才是拜偶像者；拜男人。

贝格斯坦太太为他的甘愿感到惊异。羡慕……*我是个犹太人，一个「头脑」之人，不懂得「爱」。……是你的这双眼睛。我必须永远望着它们。……如今我知道了爱指的是什么。……我甚至甘愿舍弃我的犹太教。……迈克尔*，竟去想、去说这样的话。我被加冕了，以终身；以一场我无法领受的牺牲。他必须守住他的犹太教。……你*必须*嫁给我。……那发现，流过灰色喧闹的街道，是「主宰」这一观念的秘密——女人唯有当一个男人确信时，才能确信——

===Original===
“There is then *no* common religious feeling between you?”

She had moved. The light fell upon her. She was about *forty*. She had come forth, so late, from the secret numbness of her successful independent life, and had not found what she came to seek. She was still alone in her circling day. At the period of evening dress she put on a heavy gold bracelet, ugly, a heavy ugly shape. Her face was pinched and drawn; before her lay the ordeal of belated motherhood. Vulgarly violating her refined endurance had come this incident. Dignified condemnation spoke from her averted eyes. She had said her say and was desiring that there should be no further waste of time.

===Chinese===
「那么，你们之间*没有*共同的宗教感情？」

她动过了。灯光落在她身上。她约莫*四十*岁。她这样迟才从她那成功的独立生活的秘密麻木里走出来，却没有找到她来寻的东西。在她周而复始的一天里，她仍旧独自一人。到了穿晚礼服的时辰，她戴上了一只沉甸甸的金手镯，丑陋，一副沉重丑陋的形状。她的脸枯瘦而扭曲；她面前横着一场迟来为母的磨难。这桩事，就这般粗俗地亵渎了她那份精致的忍耐。端庄的谴责，从她那转避开的眼睛里说了出来。她该说的已经说完，只盼着不要再有进一步的耗时光。

===Original===
Miriam made no sound. In the stillness that followed the blow she faced the horrible summary, stricken to her feet, her strength ebbing with her thoughts into the gathering swirling darkness. She waited for a moment. But Mrs. Bergstein made no sign. Imponderable, conscious only of the weight of her body about her holding her to the ground beneath her feet, she went away from the room and the house. In the lamplit darkness her feet carried her joyously forward into the freshness of the tree-filled air. The large square lying between her and the street where he was waiting seemed an immensity. She recovered within it the strange unfailing freedom of solitude in the sounding spaces of London and hurried on to be by his side generally expressive of her rejoicing. The world’s condemnation was out of sight behind her. But he would ask, and whatever she said, the whole problem would be there afresh, insoluble. He would never see that it had been confirmed, never admit anything contemptible in their association.… It was because there was no contempt in him that she was hurrying. But alone again with him, the troubled darkness behind her would return with its maddening influence. She was fleeing from it only towards its darkest centre.

===Chinese===
米丽亚姆没有出声。在这打击之后的静默里，她面对着那可怕的总断，被击中钉在原地，力气随同思绪一起，退落进渐聚渐旋的黑暗里。她等了片刻。可贝格斯坦太太没有表示。她轻若无物，只意识到周身躯体的重量把自己拴在脚下的地面上，就这样走出了房间，走出了宅子。在灯光点亮的黑暗里，她的双脚欢欣地载着她向前，走进满布树木的清新空气里。横在她与那条街之间的那座大广场——他正在那儿等着——显得浩浩无边。她在其中重新获得了伦敦鸣响的空间里那种奇异的、从不爽约的独处之自由，匆匆赶去他身旁，浑身上下表露着她的欢欣。世界的谴责已被甩在身后，看不见了。可他会问的，而无论她说什么，整个问题都会重新摆在眼前，无解。他永远不会看到它已被证实，永远不会承认他们的相与之中有什么可鄙之处。……正因为他身上没有轻蔑，她才这样赶去。可再同他单独在一起时，身后那片纷乱的黑暗，还会带着它令人发狂的影响卷土重来。她从它那里逃开，却只是逃向它最黑暗的中心。
